=== add test/test-piiscan-session-e2e.mjs
new 8966f798bf6aedb1e648c412
+/**
+ * A review session with the redaction on, driven through the real UI, with a developer at the modal.
+ *
+ * The unit tests prove the matcher. The boundaries test proves the two seams call it with the right file. This is
+ * the thing those two are for: an application full of personal data, a session that reads it, and a person
+ * deciding what to reveal — end to end, through the real server, the real page and the real approval modal.
+ *
+ * The model is scripted so the session is deterministic and free. What is NOT scripted is everything the product
+ * does around it: masking on the way out, unmasking tool arguments, re-masking results, raising the modal,
+ * recording the scope the developer chose, and honouring it on the next read. The live sibling
+ * (live-piiscan-session.mjs) runs the same application against a real model.
+ *
+ * The assertion that matters is negative and absolute: none of the planted values ever appears in a request body
+ * or in the transcript on disk, except the ones a person deliberately revealed.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-piiscan-session-e2e.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, mkdir, readFile, writeFile, rm, cp } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync, mkdtempSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+process.env.CB_SKILLS_MAINTAINER ??= '0';   // (the skills maintainer reads each run since 2026-10-29; its calls are not this test's)
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+const HOME = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-piiscan-e2e-home-'));
+process.env.CB_HOME = HOME;
+process.env.CB_MANDATORY_PLUGINS = '0';   // the mandatory filter is switched off below, on purpose
+
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+const { userPluginsDir } = await import('../src/plugins/paths.mjs');
+const { CANARIES, buildApp } = await import('./_piiscan-app.mjs');
+const { EXAMPLE_TOKEN } = await import('../examples/plugins/piiscan__1.mjs');
+
+const PORT = Number(process.env.CB_PIISCAN_E2E_PORT || 29613);
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+const SEP = String.fromCharCode(92);
+const chrome = [
+  'C:/Program Files/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',
+  (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '').split(SEP).join('/') + '/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',
+  'C:/Program Files (x86)/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',
+].find((p) => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+// ── the scripted model ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+// One entry per assistant turn. A turn that ends in a tool call is followed by another entry for the turn after
+// the result comes back, exactly as a real model would be re-invoked.
+const ws = (inner) => `<remote-workspace><js>${inner}</js></remote-workspace>`;
+const SCRIPT = [
+  // 1. read the properties file — a data file, so the key layer is live
+  () => 'Reading the configuration. ' + ws('return await read("src/main/resources/application.properties");'),
+  (seen) => `The support contact and clinic address are set there. I can see ${(seen.match(/\[PII:/g) || []).length} redacted values in it.`,
+  // 2. read a source file — the same shapes, but this one is code
+  () => 'Now the validator. ' + ws('return await read("src/main/java/com/northgate/Validators.java");'),
+  () => 'validPostalCode requires exactly five digits, so a ZIP+4 fails it.',
+  // 2b. A SEARCH, and TWO FILES IN ONE SNIPPET. Neither result names a file the filter could be told about: the
+  //     hits come back as a JSON array, and two files are joined into one string. The values in the .properties
+  //     file are known to the filter only BY that file - so these are the two shapes that used to let a name and a
+  //     ZIP+4 through to the model (measured live, 2026-10-08).
+  () => 'Searching for the contact key. ' + ws('const hits = await search({ content: "contact_name|postal_code" }); console.log("hits: " + JSON.stringify(hits)); return hits;'),
+  () => 'It is set in the properties file only.',
+  () => 'Both files together. ' + ws('const a = await read("src/main/resources/application.properties"); const b = await read("src/main/java/com/northgate/Validators.java"); return a + "\\n----\\n" + b.slice(0, 200);'),
+  () => 'Compared.',
+  // 3. ask for a value where the characters genuinely matter
+  { needs: 'ZIP', say: (seen, tok) => `I need the exact postal code to say whether it fails. ${ws(`return await pii_request({ token: "${tok.ZIP}", reason: "the length of the postal code decides whether validPostalCode rejects it" });`)}` },
+  () => 'With the real value in hand: it is nine digits plus a hyphen, so it fails the five-digit check.',
+  // 4. ask again for something it does not need — the developer refuses
+  { needs: 'NAME', say: (seen, tok) => `Out of interest, who is the support contact? ${ws(`return await pii_request({ token: "${tok.NAME}", reason: "curiosity about the maintainer" });`)}` },
+  () => 'Understood, carrying on with the token.',
+  // 5. read the same properties file again — the file-scoped clearance should now show through
+  () => 'Re-reading the configuration. ' + ws('return await read("src/main/resources/application.properties");'),
+  () => 'Done.',
+  // 6. WRITE a token. The agent has only ever held tokens, so a test it writes contains tokens — and the file on
+  //    disk has to end up with the real characters, or the redaction quietly corrupts the application under review.
+  { needs: 'SSN', say: (seen, tok) => 'Adding the test. ' + ws(`return await write("src/test/java/com/northgate/ValidatorsTest.java", ${JSON.stringify(`package com.northgate;
+public class ValidatorsTest {
+  // the SSN from the first fixture row
+  static final String SSN = "${tok.SSN}";
+  static final String PHONE = "${tok.PHONE || tok.SSN}";
+}
+`)});`) },
+  () => 'Written.',
+  // 7. EDIT using a token as the find-string. The token must resolve to exactly the characters in the file, or
+  //    the edit misses and the agent is told a string it can see is not there.
+  { needs: 'SSN', say: (seen, tok) => 'Renaming the constant. ' + ws(`return await edit("src/test/resources/fixtures/enrollees.csv", "${tok.SSN}", "REDACTED-BY-TEST");`) },
+  () => 'Edited.',
+];
+
+function scriptedAdapter(state) {
+  const reply = (text) => ({ choices: [{ message: { content: text } }] });
+  return {
+    baseURL: '(scripted)',
+    async listModels() { return [{ id: 'scripted' }]; },
+    async complete(request) {
+      const msgs = Array.isArray(request?.messages) ? request.messages : [];
+      state.requests.push(msgs.map((m) => (typeof m.content === 'string' ? m.content : JSON.stringify(m.content))).join('\n'));
+      const seen = state.requests[state.requests.length - 1];
+      // Tokens from TOOL RESULTS only. The plugin's own prompt carries a worked example, [PII:SSN#a1b2c3d4], and
+      // a scan of the whole transcript picked that up and asked about a value that never existed. (The refusal
+      // that produced was correct and said so — but it is the test that was wrong.)
+      const tok = { any: null };
+      for (const b of seen.matchAll(/<remote-workspace-result[^>]*>[\s\S]*?<\/remote-workspace-result>/gi))
+        for (const m of b[0].matchAll(/\[PII:([A-Z]+)#[0-9a-f]{8}\]/g)) {
+          if (m[0] === EXAMPLE_TOKEN) continue;   // the worked example rides along in the priming, and resolves to nothing
+          tok[m[1]] = tok[m[1]] || m[0]; tok.any = tok.any || m[0];
+        }
+      // A STEP THAT NEEDS A TOKEN WAITS FOR ONE rather than firing blind. The server calls the adapter for its
+      // own reasons too (a title, a fork), so the step index and the call count are not the same number — and a
+      // step that asked about `tok.ZIP` before any file had been read sent the literal string "null". The
+      // difference was invisible while a worked example in the prompt supplied a token-shaped fallback.
+      const step = SCRIPT[state.turn];
+      if (!step) return reply('Nothing further.');
+      if (step.needs && !tok[step.needs]) {
+        const from = step.needs === 'SSN' ? 'src/test/resources/fixtures/enrollees.csv' : 'src/main/resources/application.properties';
+        return reply('Reading. ' + ws(`return await read("${from}");`));
+      }
+      state.turn++;
+      return reply((step.say || step)(seen, tok));
+    },
+  };
+}
+
+// ── a developer at the modal ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+async function answerModal(page, decide) {
+  const seen = await page.evaluate(() => {
+    const bg = document.getElementById('approvalModalBg');
+    if (!bg || !bg.classList.contains('show')) return null;
+    return {
+      title: (document.getElementById('approvalTitle') || {}).textContent || '',
+      body: (document.getElementById('approvalBody') || {}).innerText || '',
+      choices: [...document.querySelectorAll('[data-pp-choice] [data-pp-choice-val]')].map((b) => b.dataset.ppChoiceVal),
+      submitDisabled: !!(document.getElementById('approvalApproveBtn') || {}).disabled,
+    };
+  });
+  if (!seen) return null;
+  const d = decide(seen);
+  if (d.scope) {
+    await page.evaluate((v) => {
+      const b = [...document.querySelectorAll('[data-pp-choice] [data-pp-choice-val]')].find((x) => x.dataset.ppChoiceVal === v);
+      if (b) b.click();
+    }, d.scope);
+    await sleep(150);
+  }
+  await page.evaluate((yes) => document.getElementById(yes ? 'approvalApproveBtn' : 'approvalCancelBtn')?.click(), !!d.approve);
+  return seen;
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  if (!chrome) { console.error('Chrome not found'); process.exit(2); }
+  const NL = String.fromCharCode(10);
+  const proj = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-piiscan-e2e-'));
+  await buildApp(proj);
+  const pdir = userPluginsDir();
+  await mkdir(pdir, { recursive: true });
+  await cp(path.join(ROOT, 'examples', 'plugins', 'piiscan__1.mjs'), path.join(pdir, 'piiscan__1.mjs'));
+  // piiscan is no longer the filter that ships (piishape is): this test is about piiscan, so it runs ALONE here
+  await writeFile(path.join(path.dirname(pdir), 'plugin-disabled.json'), JSON.stringify(['piishape']));
+
+  const state = { turn: 0, requests: [] };
+  const html = (await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8'))
+    .replace('</head>', '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'scripted', baseURL: '(scripted)' }) + ';</script></head>');
+  const { server } = await startServer({
+    html, baseURL: '(scripted)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 'd', token: 'd' },
+    defaultModel: 'scripted', port: PORT, initialProject: proj, modelsList: [{ id: 'scripted' }], adapter: scriptedAdapter(state),
+  });
+  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+  const modals = [];
+  try {
+    const page = await browser.newPage();
+    await page.setViewport({ width: 1300, height: 950 });
+    await page.goto('http://127.0.0.1:' + PORT + '/');
+    await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 20000 });
+
+    console.log('\n[A] the plugin is on before the first message — nothing below means anything otherwise');
+    for (let i = 0; i < 20; i++) {
+      const on = await page.evaluate(async () => { try { const j = await (await fetch('/api/state')).json(); return (j.plugins || []).some((p) => p.name === 'piiscan' && p.enabled); } catch { return false; } });
+      if (on) break;
+      await sleep(700);
+    }
+    ok(await page.evaluate(async () => { const j = await (await fetch('/api/state')).json(); return (j.plugins || []).some((p) => p.name === 'piiscan' && p.enabled); }), 'piiscan is loaded and enabled');
+
+    console.log('\n[B] a review session, with a developer answering the modal');
+    const status = () => page.evaluate(() => (window.__sessionRef || {}).status || 'unknown');
+    const says = [
+      'Read the configuration and tell me what is in it.',
+      'Now read the validator and tell me what it accepts.',
+      'Search the project for where the contact name and postal code are set.',
+      'Show me the configuration and the start of the validator side by side.',
+      'Does the postal code in the configuration pass that check?',
+      'Who is the support contact?',
+      'Read the configuration one more time.',
+      'Add a unit test that pins the SSN and phone from the first fixture row.',
+      'Now redact that SSN in the CSV fixture.',
+    ];
+    // Reveal the FIRST request with a file-scoped clearance; refuse the second. That is a person deciding, not a
+    // switch: the difference between the two is whether the characters mattered.
+    let asked = 0;
+    const decide = (seen) => {
+      if (!/reveal a redacted value/i.test(seen.title)) return { approve: true };
+      asked++;
+      return asked === 1 ? { approve: true, scope: 'file' } : { approve: false };
+    };
+    for (const say of says) {
+      await page.evaluate(() => { const el = document.getElementById('input'); el.value = ''; el.focus(); });
+      await page.type('#input', say);
+      await page.keyboard.press('Enter');
+      for (let k = 0; k < 40 && await status() !== 'running'; k++) await sleep(250);
+      const t0 = Date.now();
+      for (;;) {
+        const m = await answerModal(page, decide);
+        if (m) modals.push(m);
+        const st = await status();
+        if (['done', 'idle', 'error'].includes(st)) break;
+        if (Date.now() - t0 > 90000) break;
+        await sleep(300);
+      }
+      await sleep(200);
+    }
+    ok(state.requests.length >= says.length, `the scripted model was called ${state.requests.length} times`);
+

+    // The one value a person deliberately revealed is expected to be there; nothing else may be.
+    const released = '75201-1188';
+    const leaked = CANARIES.filter((c) => c !== released && blob.includes(c));
+    if (process.env.CB_PIISCAN_DUMP) {
+      await writeFile(path.join(tmpdir(), 'piiscan-e2e-requests.json'), JSON.stringify(state.requests, null, 1), 'utf8');
+      for (const c of leaked) { const i = state.requests.findIndex((r) => r.includes(c)); const r = state.requests[i]; const at = r.indexOf(c);
+        console.log('    LEAK ' + JSON.stringify(c) + ' first in request ' + i + ':' + NL + '      ...' + r.slice(Math.max(0, at - 320), at + 120).split(String.fromCharCode(10)).join(' ') + '...'); }
+      console.log('    modals seen: ' + JSON.stringify(modals.map((m) => m.title)));
+    }
+    ok(leaked.length === 0, `no planted value reached the model (${leaked.length ? leaked.join(', ') : 'none'})`);
+    ok(/\[PII:[A-Z]+#[0-9a-f]{8}\]/.test(blob), 'the model saw tokens where the values were');
+    ok(/\[PII:SSN#/.test(blob) || /\[PII:NAME#/.test(blob), 'including for the shapes the application is full of');
+
+    console.log('\n[D] the modal is a decision, not a switch');
+    const pii = modals.filter((m) => /reveal a redacted value/i.test(m.title));
+    ok(pii.length >= 2, `the agent asked twice and both raised the modal (${pii.length})`);
+    ok(pii[0] && /75201-1188/.test(pii[0].body), 'the developer was shown the real value');
+    ok(pii[0] && /postal code decides/.test(pii[0].body), "and the agent's stated reason");
+    ok(pii[0] && pii[0].choices.includes('file') && pii[0].choices.includes('project') && pii[0].choices.includes('global'),
+      `all three scopes were offered (${JSON.stringify(pii[0] && pii[0].choices)})`);
+    ok(pii[0] && pii[0].submitDisabled, 'and Reveal is blocked until a scope is chosen — unanswered is not an answer');
+
+    console.log('\n[E] the scope the developer chose is honoured on the next read');
+    const allow = JSON.parse(await readFile(path.join(proj, '.code_boss', 'piiscan-allow.json'), 'utf8'));
+    const entries = Object.values(allow.entries || {});
+    ok(entries.length === 1, `exactly one exception was recorded — the approval, not the refusal (${entries.length})`);
+    ok(entries[0] && entries[0].scope === 'file', 'recorded at the scope that was picked');
+    ok(entries[0] && (entries[0].paths || []).some((p) => p.endsWith('application.properties')), `naming the file it came from (${JSON.stringify(entries[0] && entries[0].paths)})`);
+    ok(!JSON.stringify(allow).includes(released), 'and the file holds the hash, never the value');
+    // A later turn re-read the same file. The cleared value is now in the clear there; the refused one is not.
+    const afterClear = state.requests.filter((r) => r.includes('northgate-enrollment')).pop() || '';
+    ok(afterClear.includes(released), 'the re-read of that file shows the cleared value in the clear');
+    ok(!afterClear.includes('Rutherford Havisham'), 'while the value the developer refused is still redacted');
+
+    console.log('\n[F] the transcript on disk is what gets screenshotted, so it is checked too');
+    let transcript = '';
+    try { transcript = await readFile(path.join(proj, '.code_boss', 'conversation.jsonl'), 'utf8'); } catch { transcript = ''; }
+    ok(transcript.length > 0, 'the conversation was persisted');
+    const onDisk = CANARIES.filter((c) => c !== released && transcript.includes(c));
+    ok(onDisk.length === 0, `no unreleased value is in the transcript (${onDisk.length ? onDisk.join(', ') : 'none'})`);
+
+    console.log('\n[F2] AND THE RAW LOG, which is written for every project whether or not anyone asked');
+    {
+      // Every app-to-model exchange is captured to .code_boss/raw-log.jsonl. Recording is ALWAYS ON — the
+      // Settings toggle gates only whether the UI shows the payloads — so this file exists in every project a
+      // developer opens. If the masking sat on the wrong side of it, this would be the largest deposit of
+      // personal data on the machine, in the project directory, and nobody would have opted into it.
+      //
+      // It does not, and the ordering is why: the filter wraps the transaction recorder rather than the other way
+      // round, so what the recorder snapshots is what the model was actually sent.
+      let raw = '';
+      try { raw = await readFile(path.join(proj, '.code_boss', 'raw-log.jsonl'), 'utf8'); } catch { raw = ''; }
+      ok(raw.length > 0, `the raw log was written without anyone turning it on (${raw.length} bytes)`);
+      ok(/\[PII:[A-Z]+#[0-9a-f]{8}\]/.test(raw), 'and it records the tokens the model actually saw');
+      const inRaw = CANARIES.filter((c) => c !== released && raw.includes(c));
+      ok(inRaw.length === 0, `no unreleased value is in the raw log (${inRaw.length ? inRaw.join(', ') : 'none'})`);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[G] the files themselves were never touched');
+    const props = await readFile(path.join(proj, 'src', 'main', 'resources', 'application.properties'), 'utf8');
+    ok(props.includes('Rutherford Havisham') && props.includes('75201-1188'), 'the real values are still on disk, unchanged');
+
+    console.log('\n[H] a token the agent WRITES becomes the real characters on disk');
+    {
+      // This is the one that decides whether the redaction is usable at all: the agent has only ever held tokens,
+      // so anything it writes contains them. A file left full of [PII:…] would be a corrupted application.
+      const written = await readFile(path.join(proj, 'src', 'test', 'java', 'com', 'northgate', 'ValidatorsTest.java'), 'utf8');
+      ok(!/\[PII:/.test(written), `no token survived to disk (${(written.match(/\[PII:[A-Z]+#[0-9a-f]{8}\]/g) || []).join(', ') || 'none'})`);
+      ok(/482-11-9037|601-44-2288|325-90-1174/.test(written), 'the real SSN is in the file the agent wrote');
+      // …and the transcript of that same write shows the token, not the value.
+      const writeTurn = state.requests.find((r) => r.includes('ValidatorsTest.java'));
+      ok(writeTurn && !/482-11-9037/.test(writeTurn), 'while the request that carried it shows only the token');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[I] a token used as an EDIT find-string matches the characters in the file');
+    {
+      const csv = await readFile(path.join(proj, 'src', 'test', 'resources', 'fixtures', 'enrollees.csv'), 'utf8');
+      ok(csv.includes('REDACTED-BY-TEST'), 'the edit landed — the token resolved to exactly what was in the file');
+      ok(!csv.includes('482-11-9037'), 'and replaced it');
+      ok(csv.includes('601-44-2288'), 'leaving the other rows alone');
+    }
+  } finally {
+    try { await browser.close(); } catch {}
+    try { server.close(); } catch {}
+    await rm(HOME, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+    await rm(proj, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+  }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error(e); process.exit(1); });

=== add test/test-piishape-contest.mjs
new 553e270cce86c1f2e8deb385
+/**
+ * The inverted masker from the PII contest (test/pii-contest/piishape.mjs): the properties everything else rests on.
+ *
+ *   ROUND TRIP  unmasking what was masked gives back the original, byte for byte — including a text that already
+ *               contains the marker character. An edit the model makes is mapped back this way, so a lossy mask is a
+ *               corrupted file.
+ *   GROUPS      a run is judged with its neighbours: the digit groups of an SSN, a phone and a card each look like a
+ *               harmless short number, and none of them may be shown.
+ *   THE SKETCH  the HelloWorld the design started from masks the way it was drawn.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-piishape-contest.mjs
+ */
+import { createShapeMasker } from './pii-contest/piishape.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+const m = createShapeMasker({ marker: '#' });
+const s = createShapeMasker({ marker: '§' });
+
+console.log('\n[1] round trip');
+{
+  const samples = [
+    ['A.java', 'public class HelloWorld {\n  private String owner = "Rutherford Havisham, 482-11-9037";\n  // #include is not C here, ## is not a macro\n}\n'],
+    ['data.json', '{"firstName": "Siobhán", "lastName": "O\'Brien", "note": "email jane.doe@army.mil # ok"}'],
+    ['x.csv', 'first_name,last_name,ssn\nHope,Okonkwo,601-44-2288\nZoë,Müller,537-20-4411\n'],
+    ['README.md', '# Title §§ with a section sign §\nThanks to Grant Mason (gmason@va.gov).\n'],
+  ];
+  for (const [f, t] of samples) {
+    const a = m.maskText(t, { filePath: f });
+    ok(m.unmaskText(a) === t, `# marker restores ${f} exactly`);
+    const b = s.maskText(t, { filePath: f });
+    ok(s.unmaskText(b) === t, `§ marker restores ${f} exactly`);
+  }
+  let refused = false;
+  try { m.unmaskText('value #Xxxx#zzzzzzz here'); } catch { refused = true; }
+  ok(refused, 'an id this session never issued is refused, not passed through');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[2] ids');
+{
+  const a = m.maskText('"Havisham" and "HAVISHAM"', { filePath: 'x.md' });
+  const ids = [...a.matchAll(/#([Xx0]+)#([0-9a-z]{7})/g)].map((x) => x[2]);
+  ok(ids.length === 2 && ids[0] === ids[1], `the same value in a different case gets the same id (${ids.join(', ')})`);
+  ok(/#Xxxxxxxx#/.test(a) && /#XXXXXXXX#/.test(a), 'while the shape keeps the case');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[3] a run is judged with its neighbours');
+{
+  for (const [label, value] of [['SSN', '482-11-9037'], ['phone', '(214) 555-0182'], ['card', '4111 1111 1111 1111']]) {
+    const out = m.maskText(`// contact: ${value}`, { filePath: 'A.java' });
+    ok(!/\d/.test(out.replace(/#(?:0{1,40}(?:[-./:␣]0{1,40}){1,20}|[Xx0]+)#[0-9a-z]{7}/g, '')), `${label}: no digit of ${value} is visible (${out})`);
+  }
+  // DATES ARE SHOWN, in every common format (the developer, 2026-10-22)

=== add test/test-piishape-plugin.mjs

+/**
+ * piishape — the inverted masker as a plugin: everything is masked, rules reveal what is safe.
+ *
+ * The RULES are measured elsewhere (test/pii-contest, PII-CONTEST.md) against planted corpora — 99.7% of planted
+ * personal data hidden on held-out seeds. What is asserted here is the part the contest does not have: the plugin
+ * around them. Does masking survive a round trip through a file the agent writes back, is a token from another
+ * session refused rather than guessed at, does a reveal actually stick, does an exception survive the next read of
+ * the same file, and does the file stay in step with the sources it is generated from.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-piishape-plugin.mjs
+ */
+import { execFile } from 'node:child_process';
+import { mkdtemp, mkdir, readFile, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import { promisify } from 'node:util';
+
+const ROOT = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..');
+const HOME = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-piishape-home-'));
+const PROJ = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-piishape-proj-'));
+process.env.CB_HOME = HOME;
+
+const P = await import('../examples/plugins/piishape__1.mjs');
+const plugin = P.default;
+const run = promisify(execFile);
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const ctx = { homeDir: HOME, projectDir: PROJ };
+const mask = (t, extra = {}) => P.maskText(t, { ...ctx, ...extra });
+const TOK = /#(?:0{1,40}(?:[-./:␣]0{1,40}){1,20}|[Xx0]+)#[0-9a-z]{7}/g;   // a number in groups is one token (2026-10-14)
+const ids = (t) => [...String(t).matchAll(/#(?:0{1,40}(?:[-./:␣]0{1,40}){1,20}|[Xx0]+)#([0-9a-z]{7})/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
+const NL = String.fromCharCode(10);

+/**
+ * A SECRET UNDER ITS KEY (piishape rule 9; the developer, 2026-10-28: "yes" to masking any value next to a key named like
+ * password, passwd, pwd, secret or credential - on the box the agent showed a decrypted data source password in a
+ * table). Through the shipped plugin and the real filter pipeline, with code_boss's token store:
+ *   [1] password: x, db.password=x, "apiKey":"x", -Dweblogic.password=x, <password-encrypted>x</...>, in a process's
+ *       output and in config files: the WHOLE value is one #@secret#id token, no shape shown
+ *   [2] left as they are: a reference (${DB_PW}, $DB_PW), a call, null, stars, an object; in a code file only a

+ *       word everywhere): the word stays readable in the next sentence and the next text

+]) {
+  const out = await mask(text, file);
+  const val = label === 'an api key' ? 'abc123def456' : label === 'config.xml' ? 'Zm9vYmFy' : label === 'a plain word' ? 'correct-horse-battery' : PW;
+  ok(SECRET.test(out) && !out.includes(val) && !/\^\^|0000|Xxxx/.test(out.match(/#@secret[^\s"<]*/)[0]), `${label}: ${out.replace(/#@secret#[0-9a-z]{7}/g, '#@secret#…').replace(/\n/g, ' | ').slice(0, 110)}`);
+}
+
+console.log('\n[2] left as they are');
+for (const [label, text, file] of [
+  ['a reference', 'password: ${DB_PW} and pwd=$DB_PW', ''],
+  ['a call', 'password = getPassword(); ', ''],
+  ['null and stars', 'password: null, secret: ********', ''],
+  ['an object', '"password": {"type": "string"}', ''],
+  ['a type in TypeScript', 'interface U { password: string; }', 'src/u.ts'],
+  ['a call in Java', 'String pwd = getPwd();', 'src/A.java'],
+]) {
+  const out = await mask(text, file);
+  ok(!SECRET.test(out), `${label}: ${out.slice(0, 90)}`);
+}
+
+console.log('\n[3] hidden where it stands, not looked for elsewhere');
+const a = await mask('password: password');
+ok(/^password: #@secret#[0-9a-z]{7}$/.test(a), `"password: password" hides the value (${a.replace(/#[0-9a-z]{7}$/, '#…')})`);
+const b = await mask('Enter your password and press OK. The admin password policy applies.');
+ok(/Enter your password and press OK/.test(b) && /admin password policy/.test(b), `the word "password" is still readable in other text (${b})`);
+const c = await mask(`Decrypted: ${PW}`);
+ok(!SECRET.test(c), 'a value seen under a key is not hunted elsewhere by its token (a plugin that knows it is a secret issues it)');
+
+console.log('\n[4] back to a tool, and twice');
+const out1 = await mask(`password: ${PW}`);
+const tok = out1.match(SECRET)[0];
+const back = await pipe.unmaskDeep({ password: tok }, { toolName: 'x' });
+ok(back.password === PW, 'the token handed back to a tool is the value');
+ok((await mask(out1)) === out1, 'masking the masked text again changes nothing');
+const out2 = await mask(`password: ${PW}`);
+ok(out2 === out1, 'the same value gets the same token');
+
+await rm(home, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);

+/**
+ * piishape keeps what it must keep in the store OUTSIDE the container (host.vault; vault.mjs) - the developer,
+ * 2026-10-10. Two processes, one store (a test's local one), as two sessions or two code_boss windows would have:

+  out.again = await pipe.mask('Zqwexleyharbinson again', { hook: 'tool-result', projectDir: PROJ });
+}

+  const P = mgr.enabledPlugins().find((p) => p.name === 'piishape');

+const run = (...args) => {
+  const r = spawnSync(process.execPath, [SESSION, ...args], { encoding: 'utf8', env: { ...process.env, CB_HOME: HOME }, timeout: 120000 });
+  const line = String(r.stdout).split('\n').find((l) => l.startsWith('RESULT ')) || 'RESULT {}';
+  if (!line.slice(7).trim() || line === 'RESULT {}') console.log(String(r.stderr).slice(0, 800));
+  return JSON.parse(line.slice(7));
+};
+
+console.log('\n[1] the first session masks a value; the value is kept in the store, not in the project');
+writeFileSync(path.join(PROJ, 'people.txt'), 'owner: Zqwexleyharbinson\n');

+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);

=== add test/test-plugin-adhoc-mask-e2e.mjs
new 7d18e615b0620be2e790155e
+/**
+ * A PLUGIN MASKS ITS OWN RESULT AD HOC — pii-testdata's maskFields — so a query's rows reach the agent with the
+ * sensitive columns as #@<field>#<id> tokens, WITHOUT a family: the Test data panel (the developer's) does not grow.
+ *   [A] the fixture "sql" plugin calls host.use('pii-testdata').maskFields({ data: rows, fields: [...] }) inside its
+ *       tool and returns the masked rows: the transcript + the model's next request carry tokens and never a value;
+ *       the unmasked columns (id, status) pass through as they are; no family file was written (only the dotfile map)
+ *   [B] the agent hands a token to write() in a snippet → the FILE holds the real value (H2), the transcript still holds the token
+ *   [C] the same value in a LATER, unrelated tool result is caught by the result mask with the SAME token (H3)
+ *   [D] the prompt addition told the model what a plugin-issued token is
+ * Run: node test/test-plugin-adhoc-mask-e2e.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, writeFile, mkdir, readFile, readdir } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { mkdtempSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { cleanupDir } from './_helpers.mjs';
+
+process.env.CB_HOME = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-adhoc-home-'));
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+const { userPluginsDir } = await import('../src/plugins/paths.mjs');
+
+const PORT = 29241;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+const ROWS = [
+  { id: 1042, last_name: 'Okonkwo-Zed', ssn: '482-11-9037', status: 'active' },
+  { id: 1043, last_name: 'Vantongeren', ssn: '333-22-1111', status: 'active' },
+];
+
+const PLUGIN = `export default {
+  description: 'sql fixture: query → rows masked ad hoc',
+  dependencies: { 'pii-testdata': true },
+  tools: [{ verb: 'sql_query', name: 'sql_query', schema: { description: 'run a query', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: { sql: { type: 'string' } } } },
+    impl: async (args, ctx) => {
+      const rows = ${JSON.stringify(ROWS)};
+      const pii = ctx.host.use('pii-testdata');
+      const masked = await pii.maskFields({ projectDir: ctx.projectDir, data: rows, fields: ['last_name', 'ssn'] });
+      return { content: 'ROWS ' + JSON.stringify(masked) };
+    } },
+  { verb: 'app_log', name: 'app_log', schema: { description: 'read the app log', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: {} } },
+    impl: async () => ({ content: 'LOG 2026-09-16 login ok for Okonkwo-Zed (ssn 482-11-9037)' }) }],
+};
+`;
+
+// Flat transport: route on the LAST marker in the whole conversation. The token id is only known at run time, so the
+// write step lifts the last_name token straight out of the conversation text.
+const reqs = [];
+function reply(t) {
+  const pos = (s) => t.lastIndexOf(s);
+  if (pos('STEP3') >= 0) return pos('LOG ') > pos('STEP3') ? 'Read it. STEP3_DONE' : 'Reading the log. <remote-workspace><js>return await app_log({});</js></remote-workspace>';
+  if (pos('STEP2') >= 0) {
+    if (pos('<remote-workspace-result') > pos('STEP2')) return 'Written. STEP2_DONE';
+    const m = /#@last_name(?:~[^#\s]+)?#[0-9a-z]{7}/.exec(t);
+    return m ? `Writing the name. <remote-workspace><js>return await write("out.txt", "${m[0]}");</js></remote-workspace>` : 'No token found. STEP2_DONE';
+  }
+  if (pos('STEP1') >= 0) return pos('ROWS') > pos('STEP1') ? 'Got the rows. STEP1_DONE' : 'Querying. <remote-workspace><js>return await sql_query({ sql: "SELECT * FROM enrollees" });</js></remote-workspace>';
+  return 'ready';
+}
+const adapter = {
+  async *completeStream(req) {
+    const text = (req && req.messages ? req.messages : []).map((m) => (typeof m.content === 'string' ? m.content : JSON.stringify(m.content))).join('\n');
+    reqs.push(text);
+    yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: reply(text) } }] };
+  },
+  async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] }; },
+  async listModels() { return [{ id: 'm' }]; },
+};
+
+async function main() {
+  const pdir = userPluginsDir();
+  await mkdir(pdir, { recursive: true });
+  await writeFile(path.join(pdir, 'sqladhoc__1.mjs'), PLUGIN, 'utf8');
+  const projectDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-adhoc-proj-'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(projectDir, 'README.md'), 'hello\n');

+  const B = `http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}`;
+  const j = (p) => fetch(B + p).then((r) => r.json());
+  const post = (p, b) => fetch(B + p, { method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify(b) }).then((r) => r.json());
+  const send = async (text, marker) => { await post('/api/message', { text, model: 'm' }); for (let i = 0; i < 80; i++) { await sleep(150); const s = await j('/api/state'); if ((s.events || []).some((e) => e.type === 'assistant-text' && String(e.content || '').includes(marker))) return s; } throw new Error('timeout waiting for ' + marker); };
+  const RAW = /Okonkwo-Zed|Vantongeren|482-11-9037|333-22-1111/;
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[A] the query result reaches the agent with the named columns as plugin-issued tokens — and no family');
+    const s1 = await send('STEP1 run the query', 'STEP1_DONE');
+    // the snippet returns sql_query's content, so the js_eval result row IS the query result; the inner call is a js-step
+    const res = (s1.events || []).filter((e) => e.type === 'tool-result' && e.name === 'js_eval').pop();
+    const content = String(res && res.content || '').replace(/\n\nWhat the snippet did, in order:\n[\s\S]*$/, '');
+    ok(!!res && /^ROWS /.test(content), 'the tool result is in the transcript');
+    ok((s1.events || []).some((e) => e.type === 'js-step' && e.fn === 'sql_query'), 'the snippet made the sql_query call (js-step)');
+    ok(!RAW.test(content), `no raw value in the transcript's result (${content.slice(0, 140)})`);
+    const toks = content.match(/#@(last_name|ssn)(?:~[^#\s]+)?#[0-9a-z]{7}/g) || [];
+    ok(toks.length === 4 && new Set(toks).size === 4, `the two named columns of both rows read as #@<field>#<id> tokens (${toks.length} tokens, ${new Set(toks).size} distinct: ${content.slice(0, 260)})`);
+    ok(/"id":(1042|#0000#[0-9a-z]{7})/.test(content) && /"status":"active"/.test(content), 'the unnamed columns are not touched by the test-data plugin (the shipped filter may still mask the number: it masks what it cannot prove safe)');
+    const after = reqs[reqs.length - 1] || '';
+    ok(/#@last_name(?:~[^#\s]+)?#[0-9a-z]{7}/.test(after) && !RAW.test(after), 'the model saw the tokens and never a value');
+    // the map is in the store OUTSIDE the container (vault.mjs), not in the project
+    await sleep(400);   // the store writes in batches
+    const STORE = path.join(process.env.CB_HOME, '.code_boss', 'secure');
+    const files = await readdir(path.join(STORE, 'plugins', 'pii-testdata')).catch(() => []);
+    ok(existsSync(path.join(STORE, 'tokens', 'issued.json')) && !files.includes('families') && !existsSync(path.join(projectDir, 'testdata')), `no family was written - only code_boss's token store, nothing in the project (${files.join(', ') || 'no plugin folder'})`);
+
+    console.log('\n[B] the agent hands a token to write() → the file holds the real value, the transcript the token');
+    await send('STEP2 write the first last name to out.txt', 'STEP2_DONE');
+    const fileText = await readFile(path.join(projectDir, 'out.txt'), 'utf8').catch(() => null);
+    ok(fileText === 'Okonkwo-Zed', `the file holds the REAL value via H2 (${JSON.stringify(fileText)})`);
+    const s2 = await j('/api/state');
+    const wcall = (s2.events || []).filter((e) => e.type === 'tool-call' && e.name === 'js_eval' && /write\(/.test(String((e.args || {}).code || ''))).pop();
+    ok(!!wcall && /#@last_name(?:~[^#\s]+)?#[0-9a-z]{7}/.test(JSON.stringify(wcall.args || {})) && !/Okonkwo-Zed/.test(JSON.stringify(wcall.args || {})), 'the tool-call event keeps the token, not the value');
+    const wstep = (s2.events || []).filter((e) => e.type === 'js-step' && e.fn === 'write').pop();
+    ok(!!wstep && !/Okonkwo-Zed/.test(JSON.stringify(wstep)), `the write step (label + diff) keeps the token, not the value (${JSON.stringify(wstep && { label: wstep.label, diff: wstep.diff })})`);

+    const s3 = await send('STEP3 read the app log', 'STEP3_DONE');
+    const log = (s3.events || []).filter((e) => e.type === 'tool-result' && e.name === 'js_eval').pop();
+    ok((s3.events || []).some((e) => e.type === 'js-step' && e.fn === 'app_log'), 'the snippet made the app_log call (js-step)');
+    const logText = String(log && log.content || '');
+    const nameTok = toks.find((t) => /^#@last_name[~#]/.test(t)), ssnTok = toks.find((t) => /^#@ssn[~#]/.test(t));
+    ok(!RAW.test(logText), `the log result carries no raw value (${logText})`);
+    ok(logText.includes(nameTok) && logText.includes(ssnTok), 'and the values read as the SAME tokens the query issued');
+
+    console.log('\n[D] the prompt told the model what a plugin-issued token is');
+    ok(/A NAMED TOKEN has a name where the shape would be/.test(after) && /a value a plugin's result handed you/.test(after), 'the prompt (piishape\'s, for every named token) says what a plugin-issued token is');
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.message || e); }
+  finally { try { server.close(); } catch {} await cleanupDir(projectDir); }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);

+    '      .then((r) => { globalThis.__cbGuard = { result: r }; })',

+    // the load; that trust/quarantine gate was ripped out (server.mjs / plugins/index.mjs: "no 'approve before it

=== add test/test-plugin-arg-validation.mjs
new c58f75e2c9a74d480786ea74
+/**
+ * Plugin tool calls are validated against their declared schema at the single dispatch chokepoint (execute() in
+ * tools.mjs), so a bad call becomes a LOUD, correctable error instead of running with an empty/undefined arg and
+ * returning "undefined" (the browser_eval failure). Checks: a missing required param is named; an empty required
+ * string counts as missing; a scalar param that cannot coerce to its type is flagged; a complete call runs and gets
+ * typed args; and BUILT-IN tools are NOT subjected to this (they validate themselves — many tests rely on their
+ * bespoke errors). Run: node test/test-plugin-arg-validation.mjs
+ */
+// executeForModel, aliased: these assert each tool's ERROR TEXT, which is what the model reads. execute() itself
+// now THROWS for a call that cannot be made (inside a <js> snippet that is what a failure is) — see tools.mjs.
+import { executeForModel as execute, extractContent } from '../src/agent/tools.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+// A plugin tool shaped like browser_eval: required label + js, plus a typed number and boolean.
+const tool = {
+  name: 'demo_eval', verb: 'demo_eval',
+  schema: { description: 'x', parameters: { type: 'object',
+    properties: { label: { type: 'string' }, js: { type: 'string' }, count: { type: 'integer' }, headless: { type: 'boolean' } },
+    required: ['label', 'js'] } },
+  impl: async (a) => ({ content: JSON.stringify(a) }),
+};
+const extra = { demo_eval: tool };
+const run = (args, ctx = {}) => execute('demo_eval', args, extra, ctx);
+const isErr = (r) => typeof r === 'string' && r.startsWith('ERROR:');
+
+console.log('\n[A] a missing required param is named — the call never reaches the impl');
+let r = await run({ label: 's1' });                       // js missing (the dropped-body case)
+ok(isErr(r) && /"js"/.test(r) && /not provided/.test(r), `missing "js" is reported (${isErr(r) ? r.slice(0, 90) : 'ran anyway'})`);
+
+console.log('\n[B] an EMPTY required string counts as missing (the exact "undefined" failure)');
+r = await run({ label: 's1', js: '' });
+ok(isErr(r) && /"js"/.test(r) && /empty/.test(r), `empty "js" is reported as empty (${isErr(r) ? r.slice(0, 90) : 'ran anyway'})`);
+
+console.log('\n[C] a complete call runs and the impl receives TYPED args');
+r = await run({ label: 's1', js: 'return 1;', count: '5', headless: 'false' });
+ok(!isErr(r), 'a complete call is not rejected');
+const got = (() => { try { return JSON.parse(extractContent(r)); } catch { return {}; } })();
+ok(got.js === 'return 1;' && got.label === 's1', 'the impl saw label + js');
+ok(got.count === 5 && typeof got.count === 'number', `count coerced to a number (${JSON.stringify(got.count)})`);
+ok(got.headless === false && typeof got.headless === 'boolean', `headless coerced to a boolean (${JSON.stringify(got.headless)})`);
+
+console.log('\n[D] a scalar that cannot coerce to its declared type is flagged');
+r = await run({ label: 's1', js: 'x', count: 'abc' });
+ok(isErr(r) && /"count"/.test(r) && /number/.test(r), `non-numeric "count" is flagged (${isErr(r) ? r.slice(0, 90) : 'ran anyway'})`);
+
+console.log('\n[E] BUILT-IN tools are NOT subjected to plugin validation (scoped to plugin tools)');
+r = await run(undefined);                                 // even undefined args on a plugin tool → validation, not a crash
+ok(isErr(r), 'undefined args on a plugin tool are handled (validation error, not a throw)');
+// read_file is a built-in; calling it with no path must NOT produce the plugin-validation "ERROR: read_file —" shape.
+const rb = await execute('read_file', {}, {}, { cwd: process.cwd() });
+ok(!(typeof rb === 'string' && rb.startsWith('ERROR: read_file —')), `built-in read_file uses its own error path, not plugin validation (${String(extractContent(rb)).slice(0, 70)})`);
+
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+

+/**
+ * LIVE BUTTONS IN A BLOCKING PANEL — a plugin's "test before you approve" dialog, through the real UI:
+ *   [A] the plugin's ask-panel opens with a note, a Test and a Refresh button (live) and a text field
+ *   [B] Test → the plugin's onEvent runs with the current values; the dialog REPAINTS (the note now carries the
+ *       result) and STAYS OPEN; text already typed is kept
+ *   [C] Refresh → another round-trip; the typed text is still there
+ *   [D] Approve → the plugin gets { submitted: true, values } with the typed note, and its onEvent log
+ *   [E] an onEvent that throws shows the error under the tree; the dialog is still usable; no page errors
+ * Run: node test/test-plugin-ask-panel-live-e2e.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, writeFile, mkdir, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';

+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+import { cleanupDir } from './_helpers.mjs';
+

+const PORT = 29243;

+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+

+  description: 'data-source approval fixture',
+  tools: [{ verb: 'src_approve', name: 'src_approve', schema: { description: 'ask', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: {} } },
+    impl: async (args, ctx) => {
+      const host = ctx.host; host.state.log = [];
+      let tests = 0, refreshes = 0;
+      const tree = (status, stamp) => ({ type: 'stack', children: [
+        { type: 'note', text: 'Data source: db.json (listing #' + stamp + ')' },
+        { type: 'note', text: 'Status: ' + status },
+        { type: 'row', children: [ { type: 'button', id: 'test', label: 'Test', live: true }, { type: 'button', id: 'refresh', label: 'Refresh', live: true }, { type: 'button', id: 'boom', label: 'Boom', live: true } ] },
+        { type: 'text', id: 'why', label: 'Note', placeholder: 'why approve' },
+      ] });
+      const { submitted, values } = await host.request('ask-panel', { title: 'Use this data source?', tree: tree('not tested', 0), submitLabel: 'Use it',
+        onEvent: async ({ id, values }) => {
+          host.state.log.push({ id, why: values.why || '' });
+          if (id === 'boom') throw new Error('probe exploded');
+          if (id === 'test') { tests++; return tree('OK: 3 tables (test #' + tests + ')', refreshes); }
+          if (id === 'refresh') { refreshes++; return { tree: tree(tests ? 'OK: 3 tables (test #' + tests + ')' : 'not tested', refreshes) }; }
+          return null;
+        } });
+      return { content: 'RESULT ' + JSON.stringify({ submitted, values, log: host.state.log }) };
+    } }],
+};
+`;
+let call = 0;
+const adapter = {
+  async *completeStream() { call++; yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: call === 1 ? 'Asking. <remote-workspace><js>return await src_approve({});</js></remote-workspace>' : 'Done. ASK_DONE' } }] }; },
+  async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] }; },
+  async listModels() { return [{ id: 'm' }]; },

+    console.log('\n[C] Refresh → another round-trip; the text is still there');
+    await page.click('#panelAskBody button[data-ppid="refresh"]');
+    await page.waitForFunction(() => /listing #1/.test((document.getElementById('panelAskBody') || {}).textContent || ''), { timeout: 8000 });
+    ok(/listing #1/.test(await bodyText()) && /test #1/.test(await bodyText()), 'the listing refreshed and the earlier test result is still shown (the plugin chose what to keep)');
+    ok((await page.evaluate(() => document.querySelector('#panelAskBody input[data-ppid="why"]').value)) === 'looks legit', 'typed text kept across the second repaint');

+    const j = JSON.parse((result.match(/RESULT (\{.*\})/) || [])[1] || '{}');
+    ok(j.submitted === true && j.values && j.values.why === 'looks legit', `submitted with the typed note (${JSON.stringify(j.values)})`);
+    ok(Array.isArray(j.log) && j.log.map((l) => l.id).join(',') === 'test,refresh,boom' && j.log.every((l) => l.why === 'looks legit'), `onEvent saw each click with the current values (${JSON.stringify(j.log)})`);

+/**
+ * Plugin approval (Layer 2) END TO END: a plugin's init(host) calls host.request('ask-user', …); the SYSTEM
+ * surfaces the blocking approval modal (auto-approve can't skip it) with the plugin's identity + question +
+ * warning + custom button labels; the developer's Approve/Cancel decision returns straight to the plugin — the
+ * agent is never involved. Drives the REAL server + UI via Puppeteer. Run: node test/test-plugin-ask-user-e2e.mjs
+ */
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+import { startServer } from '../src/server.mjs';
+import { userPluginsDir } from '../src/plugins/paths.mjs';
+import { mkdtemp, writeFile, mkdir, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+const ROOT = process.cwd();
+const PORT = 29871;

+async function main() {

+    '      .then((r1) => { g.first = r1; return host.request("ask-user", { title: "DevServer", question: "Delete the build cache?", approveLabel: "Delete", cancelLabel: "Keep" }); })',
+    '      .then((r2) => { g.second = r2; })',
+    '      .catch((e) => { g.error = String(e && e.message || e); });',
+    '  },',
+    '};',
+  ].join('\n');

+    // gate; ripped out", plugins/index.mjs: "no 'approve before it runs' step, only an opt-out toggle"). This test
+    // drove the #pluginWarn -> Enable dance to trigger the load, and timed out once that gate was gone. What it
+    // actually exercises -- a plugin's init reaching the USER via host.request('ask-user') -- is unchanged.
+    console.log('\n[A] the plugin loads -> its init asks the USER for approval (modal appears)');

+    ok(globalThis.__cbApproveLeakedToAgent === undefined, 'the agent was never involved (no agent tool call)');

+    ok(got && got.second && got.second.approved === false, `Cancel → the plugin got approved:false (got: ${JSON.stringify(got && got.second)})`);
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.message || e); }
+  finally { await browser.close(); try { server.close(); } catch {} }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);

=== add test/test-plugin-authored-persist-e2e.mjs
new 6f8e5accaa352a048abc229c
+/**
+ * When the AGENT authors a plugin (writes <name>__<version>.mjs into the GLOBAL plugin folder via write() in a snippet),
+ * it must LOAD promptly (the post-write reconcile-soon hook — not the slow 5s poll) and still be loaded after a
+ * RESTART. There is no trust system any more — every plugin in the folder loads by default and no trust file is
+ * ever written — so the write IS the whole install. Also proves the agent may write into the global folder
+ * (outside its project) via the standing grant. Run: node test/test-plugin-authored-persist-e2e.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+// CB_HOME → a temp dir BEFORE importing the server, so the global plugin folder resolves there (isolated + real).
+const HOME = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-plgauth-home-'));
+process.env.CB_HOME = HOME;
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+const { userPluginsDir } = await import('../src/plugins/paths.mjs');
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const PORT = 29153;
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+
+const PLUGIN_BODY = 'export default { tools: [{ verb: "myfoo", name: "myfoo", schema: { parameters: { type: "object", properties: {} } }, impl: async () => "ok" }] };';
+const GLOBAL_PLUGIN_FILE = path.join(userPluginsDir(), 'myfoo__1.mjs');
+// Turn 1: write the plugin in the project, deployPlugin() puts it in the GLOBAL folder (the install location). Turn 2: finish.
+function adapter() {
+  let r = 0;
+  const responses = [
+    // (the deployed-plugins folder is closed to the agent: it writes the file in the project and deployPlugin() installs it)
+    '<remote-workspace><js>await write("myfoo__1.mjs", ' + JSON.stringify(PLUGIN_BODY) + '); return await deployPlugin({ file: "myfoo__1.mjs" });</js></remote-workspace>',
+    'Done — installed the myfoo plugin.',
+    '',
+  ];
+  const next = () => responses[Math.min(r++, responses.length - 1)];
+  return {
+    async *completeStream() { yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: next() } }] }; },
+    async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: next() } }] }; },
+  };
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  const proj = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-plgauth-'));   // NOT a git repo → no branch gate
+  const { server } = await startServer({ html: '<body>x</body>', baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'test-key', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter: adapter(), modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: proj });
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[A] the agent writes the plugin into the GLOBAL folder (standing grant) → it loads promptly');
+    const r = await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/message`, { method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify({ text: 'install a plugin called myfoo', model: 'm' }) });
+    ok(r.status === 200 || r.status === 202, `/api/message accepted (status ${r.status})`);
+    // wait for the run to finish + the post-write reconcile-soon (now/350ms/1500ms) to land
+    for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) { const s = await (await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/state?limit=1`)).json().catch(() => ({})); if (s.status === 'done' || s.status === 'idle') break; await sleep(250); }
+    await sleep(2200);
+    ok(existsSync(GLOBAL_PLUGIN_FILE), 'deployPlugin put the plugin file in the global folder');
+    {
+      const st = await (await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/state`)).json().catch(() => ({}));
+      const plugins = st.plugins || (st.project && st.project.plugins) || [];
+      const foo = Array.isArray(plugins) ? plugins.find((p) => p.name === 'myfoo') : null;
+      ok(!!foo && foo.enabled === true && !foo.error, `myfoo LOADED right after the write — no approval step (got ${JSON.stringify(foo && { enabled: foo.enabled, error: foo.error })})`);
+    }
+    ok(!existsSync(path.join(HOME, '.code_boss', 'plugin-trust.json')), 'no plugin-trust.json is written (the trust system is gone)');
+
+    console.log('\n[B] a RESTART (fresh server) still loads it — nothing to re-approve');
+    server.close();
+    const { server: server2 } = await startServer({ html: '<body>x</body>', baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'test-key', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT + 1, adapter: adapter(), modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: proj });
+    try {
+      await sleep(800);   // background armPluginWatch reconcile
+      const st = await (await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT + 1}/api/state`)).json().catch(() => ({}));
+      const plugins = st.plugins || (st.project && st.project.plugins) || [];
+      const foo = Array.isArray(plugins) ? plugins.find((p) => p.name === 'myfoo') : null;
+      ok(!!foo && foo.enabled === true && !foo.error, `after restart myfoo is still enabled — ${JSON.stringify(foo && { enabled: foo.enabled, error: foo.error })}`);
+    } finally { try { server2.close(); } catch {} }
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.message || e); }
+  finally { try { server.close(); } catch {} await rm(proj, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); await rm(HOME, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main();

+/**
+ * console.log from plugin code lands in that plugin's log - the lines plugin_log reads (the developer, 2026-10-12:
+ * "seems like the plugin agent isn't able to see logs" - an agent debugging its plugin put console.log in a helper
+ * module, saw nothing, and went off to write trace files into the temp folder).
+ *   [1] a tool's own console.log, and one from a helper module with no host
+ *   [2] a callback the tool started (a timer), after the tool returned
+ *   [3] init()'s console, and a timer init started
+ *   [4] console outside any plugin is NOT logged as a plugin's
+ * Real reconcile of a real plugin file. Run: node test/test-plugin-console-log.mjs
+ */
+import { createPluginManager } from '../src/plugins/index.mjs';
+import { mkdtemp, mkdir, writeFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; process.stdout.write('  ok ' + l + '\n'); } else { fail++; process.stdout.write('  FAIL ' + l + '\n'); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+
+const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-plgcon-'));
+try {
+  const pdir = path.join(dir, 'plugins');
+  await mkdir(pdir, { recursive: true });
+  await writeFile(path.join(pdir, 'helper.mjs'), 'export const trace = (m) => console.log("[cdp] " + m);\n');
+  await writeFile(path.join(pdir, 'tracer__1.mjs'), [
+    'import { trace } from "./helper.mjs";',
+    'export default {',
+    '  init(host) { console.log("init ran"); setTimeout(() => console.warn("init timer fired"), 20); },',
+    '  tools: [{ verb: "tracer_go", name: "tracer_go", schema: { parameters: { type: "object", properties: {} } },',
+    '    impl: async () => { console.log("tool start", { step: 1 }); trace("socket opened"); setTimeout(() => console.error("late frame"), 30); return "ok"; } }],',
+    '};',
+  ].join('\n'));
+
+  const lines = [];
+  const mgr = createPluginManager({ onLog: (m) => lines.push(m), onHostRequest: async () => ({}) });
+  mgr.setPluginsDir(pdir);
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  await sleep(60);
+
+  process.stdout.write('\n[3] init()\n');
+  ok(lines.includes('[tracer] init ran'), `init's console.log is in the plugin's log (${JSON.stringify(lines.filter((l) => l.startsWith('[tracer]')))})`);
+  ok(lines.includes('[tracer] warn: init timer fired'), 'and so is a timer init started, with its level');
+
+  process.stdout.write('\n[1] a tool, and a helper module with no host\n');
+  const tool = mgr.enabledTools().tracer_go;
+  const r = await tool.impl({}, {});
+  ok(r === 'ok', 'the tool still returns what it returns');
+  ok(lines.includes('[tracer] tool start {"step":1}'), 'the tool\'s console.log, objects as JSON');
+  ok(lines.includes('[tracer] [cdp] socket opened'), 'a helper module\'s console.log');
+
+  process.stdout.write('\n[2] a callback the tool started, after it returned\n');
+  await sleep(80);
+  ok(lines.includes('[tracer] error: late frame'), 'a timer the tool set is still the plugin\'s');
+
+  process.stdout.write('\n[4] outside any plugin\n');
+  const before = lines.length;
+  console.log('server-side line, not a plugin');
+  ok(lines.length === before, 'console outside a plugin is not logged as one');
+} finally { await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }

+/**
+ * host.container - what a plugin uses to work with the container code_boss runs in - and what the plugin authoring

+  let said = '';

+    let body = '';
+    req.on('data', (d) => { body += d; });
+    req.on('end', () => {
+      seen.push(`${req.method} ${req.url} ${req.headers['x-cb-container']} ${body}`);

+      else if (req.url === '/v1/browser') res.end(JSON.stringify({ ok: true, browsers: [{ id: 'app-1' }] }));
+      else res.end(JSON.stringify({ ok: true, closed: true }));
+    });
+  });
+  await new Promise((r) => srv.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', r));

+  let said = '';
+  try { await c.browser.open({ label: 'bad' }); } catch (e) { said = e.message; }
+  ok(/Google Chrome was not found/.test(said), `a refused open() THROWS the manager's sentence (a plugin cannot go on without a browser): ${said}`);
+  await new Promise((r) => srv.close(r));

+  const s = b.slice(b.indexOf('12. INSIDE THE CONTAINER (host.container)'));

=== add test/test-plugin-data-dir.mjs
new 9d8a222ed1681cec25b4f9df
+/**
+ * A plugin gets a PERSISTENT per-plugin data dir via host.dataDir (init(host)) — created before init runs, under
+ * the configured pluginDataRoot, so a plugin can extract an embedded binary (a JAR) there once and run it, keeping
+ * state across reloads. Verifies the wiring end to end (real reconcile of a real plugin file). Run: node test/test-plugin-data-dir.mjs
+ */
+import { createPluginManager } from '../src/plugins/index.mjs';
+import { mkdtemp, mkdir, writeFile, rm, stat } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-plgdata-'));
+try {
+  const pdir = path.join(dir, 'proj', '.code_boss', 'plugins');
+  const dataRoot = path.join(dir, 'home', 'plugin-data');
+  await mkdir(pdir, { recursive: true });
+
+  // A plugin that records host.dataDir in init and, in its tool, writes a file into it (the "extract a JAR" shape).
+  const src = [
+    'let DATA = null;',
+    'export default {',
+    '  init(host) { DATA = host.dataDir; globalThis.__seenDataDir = host.dataDir; },',
+    '  tools: [{ verb: "dropfile", name: "dropfile", schema: { parameters: { type: "object", properties: {} } },',
+    '    impl: async () => { const { writeFileSync } = await import("node:fs"); const { resolve } = await import("node:path");',
+    '      const p = resolve(DATA, "extracted.bin"); writeFileSync(p, "JARBYTES"); return { content: p }; } }],',
+    '};',
+  ].join('\n');
+  await writeFile(path.join(pdir, 'jartool__1.mjs'), src);
+
+  const mgr = createPluginManager({ onLog: () => {}, onHostRequest: async () => ({}), pluginDataRoot: dataRoot });
+  mgr.setPluginsDir(pdir);
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+
+  console.log('\n[1] init(host) received a real, created dataDir under the configured root');
+  const seen = globalThis.__seenDataDir;
+  ok(typeof seen === 'string' && seen.length > 0, `host.dataDir was passed to init (got: ${seen})`);
+  ok(seen === path.join(dataRoot, 'jartool'), `dataDir = <root>/<plugin-name> (jartool) — got ${seen}`);
+  ok(existsSync(seen) && (await stat(seen)).isDirectory(), 'the data dir was CREATED before init ran (exists on disk)');
+
+  console.log('\n[2] the plugin can persist a file there (the extract-a-binary shape)');
+  const snap = mgr.snapshot().find((r) => r.name === 'jartool');
+  ok(snap && snap.enabled && !snap.error, `plugin loaded ok (${snap && snap.error ? snap.error : 'enabled'})`);
+  // call the tool through the registry the same way the worker would
+  const tool = mgr.getTools ? mgr.getTools().find((t) => t.name === 'dropfile') : null;
+  if (tool) {
+    const r = await tool.impl({}, { cwd: dir });
+    const wrote = typeof r === 'string' ? r : r.content;
+    ok(existsSync(wrote), `the tool wrote a file into the data dir (${path.basename(wrote)})`);
+  } else {
+    // getTools shape may differ; assert the dir is at least writable by creating a file
+    const { writeFileSync } = await import('node:fs');
+    writeFileSync(path.join(seen, 'probe.bin'), 'x');
+    ok(existsSync(path.join(seen, 'probe.bin')), 'the data dir is writable (probe file created)');
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[3] no data root configured → dataDir is null (bare test manager), and load still works');
+  const mgr2 = createPluginManager({ onLog: () => {}, onHostRequest: async () => ({}) });
+  mgr2.setPluginsDir(pdir);
+  globalThis.__seenDataDir = 'unset';
+  await mgr2.reconcile();
+  ok(globalThis.__seenDataDir === null, `dataDir is null when no root configured (got ${globalThis.__seenDataDir})`);
+  await mgr2.setPluginsDir(null); await mgr2.reconcile();
+  await mgr.setPluginsDir(null); await mgr.reconcile();
+} finally {
+  delete globalThis.__seenDataDir;
+  await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+}
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);

=== add test/test-plugin-dependencies.mjs
new 7fcd20fbaefed4c1239fce28
+/**
+ * Plugin-to-plugin dependencies + versioning. Covers the PURE version helpers (parse/compare/satisfies), the
+ * dependencies validation, and the reconcile enforcement pass (missing / disabled / version-mismatch / transitive /
+ * cycle) via a fixtured plugin dir. Run: node test/test-plugin-dependencies.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, writeFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { createPluginManager } from '../src/plugins/index.mjs';
+import { parseVersion, compareVersions, satisfiesVersion, validatePluginShape } from '../src/plugins/runtime-loader.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const find = (snap, name) => snap.find((r) => r.name === name);
+// A minimal plugin file; deps is an optional object literal spliced in.
+const P = (verb, deps) => `export default { description:'p', ${deps ? 'dependencies:' + JSON.stringify(deps) + ',' : ''} tools:[{ verb:'${verb}', name:'${verb}', schema:{ description:'d', parameters:{ type:'object', properties:{} } }, impl: async()=>({ content:'x' }) }] };`;
+
+// ── version helpers ──
+console.log('\n[A] version parse/compare');
+ok(JSON.stringify(parseVersion('1.2.3')) === '[1,2,3]', 'parseVersion 1.2.3');
+ok(compareVersions('1.2', '1.10') === -1, '1.2 < 1.10 (numeric, not lexical)');
+ok(compareVersions('1', '1.0') === 0, '1 == 1.0 (trailing zeros)');
+ok(compareVersions('2.0.1', '2') === 1, '2.0.1 > 2');
+
+console.log('\n[B] satisfiesVersion constraints');
+ok(satisfiesVersion('1', '*') && satisfiesVersion('9.9', 'any') && satisfiesVersion('1', ''), 'any/*/empty always satisfy');
+ok(satisfiesVersion('1', '1') && !satisfiesVersion('1.5', '1'), 'bare = EXACT (1 matches 1, not 1.5)');
+ok(satisfiesVersion('2', '>=2') && satisfiesVersion('3', '>=2') && !satisfiesVersion('1', '>=2'), '>=2');
+ok(satisfiesVersion('3', '>2') && !satisfiesVersion('2', '>2'), '>2 is strict');
+ok(satisfiesVersion('1', '<=2') && satisfiesVersion('2', '<=2') && !satisfiesVersion('3', '<=2'), '<=2');
+ok(satisfiesVersion('1.5', '^1') && satisfiesVersion('1.9', '^1.2') && !satisfiesVersion('2.0', '^1'), 'caret ^1 = >= and same major');
+ok(satisfiesVersion('1.2.9', '~1.2') && !satisfiesVersion('1.3.0', '~1.2'), 'tilde ~1.2 = same major.minor');
+ok(!satisfiesVersion(null, '>=1') && satisfiesVersion(null, '*'), 'a null version satisfies only the any-constraint');
+
+// ── dependencies validation ──
+console.log('\n[C] dependencies validation');
+ok(validatePluginShape({ dependencies: { a: '>=1' } }, 'me').ok, 'a valid deps map passes');
+ok(!validatePluginShape({ dependencies: ['a'] }, 'me').ok, 'an array is rejected');
+ok(!validatePluginShape({ dependencies: { a: 2 } }, 'me').ok, 'a non-string constraint is rejected');
+ok(!validatePluginShape({ dependencies: { me: '1' } }, 'me').ok, 'a self-dependency is rejected');
+ok(!validatePluginShape({ dependencies: { 'Bad Name': '1' } }, 'me').ok, 'an invalid dep name is rejected');
+ok(!validatePluginShape({ dependencies: { a: '>=1 <2' } }, 'me').ok, 'a compound range (silently mis-parses) is rejected at load');
+ok(!validatePluginShape({ dependencies: { a: 'abc' } }, 'me').ok, 'a garbage constraint is rejected');
+ok(!validatePluginShape({ dependencies: { a: 'v3' } }, 'me').ok, 'a non-numeric constraint "v3" is rejected');
+ok(validatePluginShape({ dependencies: { a: '^1.2', b: '*', c: '>=3' } }, 'me').ok, 'valid constraints (^, *, >=) pass');
+
+async function withDir(fn) {
+  const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-plugdep-'));
+  const mgr = createPluginManager({ onLog: () => {} });
+  mgr.setPluginsDir(dir);
+  try { await fn(dir, mgr); } finally { await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+}
+
+// ── enforcement: missing dependency ──
+console.log('\n[D] a plugin with a MISSING dependency is disabled with a clear reason');
+await withDir(async (dir, mgr) => {
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'app__1.mjs'), P('app_do', { lib: '>=1' }));
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  const r = find(mgr.snapshot(), 'app');
+  ok(r?.enabled === false && /requires plugin "lib" \(not installed\)/.test(r.error || ''), 'app is disabled: lib not installed');
+  ok(!mgr.enabledTools().app_do, "the disabled plugin's tool is NOT exposed");
+});
+
+// ── enforcement: satisfied → enabled; version mismatch → disabled ──
+console.log('\n[E] dependency present: version must match');
+await withDir(async (dir, mgr) => {
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'lib__1.mjs'), P('lib_do'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'app__1.mjs'), P('app_do', { lib: '>=2' }));
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  let r = find(mgr.snapshot(), 'app');
+  ok(r?.enabled === false && /requires "lib" >=2, found v1/.test(r.error || ''), 'app disabled: lib v1 does not satisfy >=2');
+  // bump lib to v2 → app enables
+  await rm(path.join(dir, 'lib__1.mjs'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'lib__2.mjs'), P('lib_do'));
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  r = find(mgr.snapshot(), 'app');
+  ok(r?.enabled === true, 'app re-enables once lib v2 satisfies >=2');
+  ok(!!mgr.enabledTools().app_do && !!mgr.enabledTools().lib_do, 'both tools now exposed');
+});
+
+// ── enforcement: transitive cascade ──
+console.log('\n[F] a broken dep cascades transitively (A→B→C, C missing → all three off)');
+await withDir(async (dir, mgr) => {
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'a__1.mjs'), P('a_do', { b: '*' }));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'b__1.mjs'), P('b_do', { c: '*' }));   // c is never installed
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  ok(find(mgr.snapshot(), 'b')?.enabled === false, 'b off (its dep c missing)');
+  ok(find(mgr.snapshot(), 'a')?.enabled === false, 'a off transitively (b disabled)');
+  ok(/depends on "b", which is disabled/.test(find(mgr.snapshot(), 'a')?.error || ''), 'a error names the disabled dep b');
+});
+
+// ── enforcement: circular dependency ──
+console.log('\n[G] a circular dependency is detected + disabled');
+await withDir(async (dir, mgr) => {
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'x__1.mjs'), P('x_do', { y: '*' }));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'y__1.mjs'), P('y_do', { x: '*' }));
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  const rx = find(mgr.snapshot(), 'x'), ry = find(mgr.snapshot(), 'y');
+  ok(rx?.enabled === false && ry?.enabled === false, 'both x and y are disabled on a cycle');
+  ok(/circular dependency/.test(rx?.error || '') || /circular dependency/.test(ry?.error || ''), 'the error names it a circular dependency');
+});
+
+// ── snapshot exposes declared dependencies ──
+console.log('\n[H] the snapshot row carries the declared dependencies');
+await withDir(async (dir, mgr) => {
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'lib__1.mjs'), P('lib_do'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'app__1.mjs'), P('app_do', { lib: '>=1' }));
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  ok(find(mgr.snapshot(), 'app')?.dependencies?.lib === '>=1', 'app snapshot lists { lib: ">=1" }');
+  ok(find(mgr.snapshot(), 'lib')?.dependencies == null, 'a plugin with no deps has dependencies:null');
+});
+
+// ── multiple dependencies: all must be satisfied ──
+console.log('\n[I] multiple dependencies — one missing disables the plugin');
+await withDir(async (dir, mgr) => {
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'lib__1.mjs'), P('lib_do'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'app__1.mjs'), P('app_do', { lib: '>=1', other: '*' }));   // `other` missing
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  const r = find(mgr.snapshot(), 'app');
+  ok(r?.enabled === false && /requires plugin "other" \(not installed\)/.test(r.error || ''), 'app off: one of two deps (other) is missing');
+  // install `other` → app enables (both deps now satisfied)
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'other__1.mjs'), P('other_do'));
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  ok(find(mgr.snapshot(), 'app')?.enabled === true, 'app enables once BOTH deps are present');
+});
+
+// ── constraint tolerance: whitespace after the operator ──
+console.log('\n[J] a constraint with whitespace ("&gt;= 2") still parses');
+ok(satisfiesVersion('2', '>= 2') && !satisfiesVersion('1', '>= 2'), '">= 2" parses with a space');
+
+// ── statusBrief: the agent-facing landscape (enabled + DISABLED with reasons) ──
+console.log('\n[K] statusBrief lists enabled + disabled plugins with reasons');
+await withDir(async (dir, mgr) => {
+  // With ZERO plugins the brief is NOT empty any more: it names the GLOBAL folder (that is exactly when the agent
+  // is about to author its first plugin and needs the absolute path). It IS empty only when no dir is configured.
+  const emptyBrief = mgr.statusBrief();
+  ok(/INSTALLED PLUGINS: none yet/i.test(emptyBrief) && !emptyBrief.includes(dir) && /deployPlugin\(\{ file/.test(emptyBrief), 'with no plugins, the list says how to install one - deployPlugin - and does not name the folder');
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'lib__2.mjs'), P('lib_do'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'app__1.mjs'), P('app_do', { lib: '>=3' }));   // wants lib >=3, has v2 → disabled
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  const brief = mgr.statusBrief();
+  ok(/lib v2 — ENABLED/.test(brief), 'the brief marks lib ENABLED with its version');
+  ok(/app v1 — DISABLED: requires "lib" >=3, found v2/.test(brief), 'the brief marks app DISABLED with the dependency reason');
+  ok(/depends on lib >=3/.test(brief), 'the brief shows the declared dependency');
+  ok(/INSTALLED PLUGINS/.test(brief) && /tell them/i.test(brief) || /reason/i.test(brief), 'the brief explains its purpose to the agent');
+  // The agent was asking the developer to restart / enable a plugin it had just written, because nothing told it
+  // this list is the FEEDBACK CHANNEL for its own authoring loop (reloads in ~5s; the next turn's row is the result).
+  // (2026-10-28: the header no longer teaches installing by write/copy - deployPlugin, in the front, is the install)
+  ok(/global: one install serves every project/.test(brief) && !brief.includes(dir) && !/write\/edit\/copy that installs/.test(brief), 'says the list is the global set, and not where it lives or how to copy into it');
+  ok(/A plugin you deploy shows here on your next turn/.test(brief), 'tells the agent its own plugin shows here on the next turn');
+  // ONE way to call a tool (the snippet-only migration): a plugin tool is a FUNCTION in a <js> snippet, and the
+  // roster says so — once in the header, and per tool. (The agent once claimed "only the core workspace functions
+  // can be called through js", 2026-09-18; the roster is where that belief is corrected.)
+  ok(/tools are functions in your snippet, called by name with ONE args object/.test(brief), 'the header says every plugin tool is a function in the snippet');
+  ok(!/callable TWO ways|as a tag in a <remote-workspace>/.test(brief), 'and no longer offers a tag form');
+  // each tool on its own line, as it is called - its parameters, and what it says it does (2026-10-10: the name alone
+  // sent the model guessing at paths and parameter names)
+  ok(/· lib_do\(\{ [^}]*\}\)/.test(brief), `each tool is listed as a function call, with its parameters (${(brief.match(/· lib_do[^\n]*/) || [])[0]})`);
+});
+
+// ── a dependency that DISAPPEARS re-disables the dependent on the next reconcile ──
+console.log('\n[L] removing a dependency re-disables the dependent');
+await withDir(async (dir, mgr) => {
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'lib__1.mjs'), P('lib_do'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'app__1.mjs'), P('app_do', { lib: '*' }));
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  ok(find(mgr.snapshot(), 'app')?.enabled === true, 'app enabled while lib present');

+  ok(r?.enabled === false && /not installed/.test(r.error || ''), 'app re-disables after lib is removed');
+  ok(!mgr.enabledTools().app_do, "app's tool is withdrawn");
+});
+
+// ── a dependency key with UPPERCASE resolves to the (lowercased) plugin, not "not installed" ──
+console.log('\n[M] a dependency declared with uppercase resolves case-insensitively');
+await withDir(async (dir, mgr) => {
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'jira__2.mjs'), P('jira_do'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'app__1.mjs'), P('app_do', { Jira: '>=2' }));   // "Jira" (uppercase) → jira
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  ok(find(mgr.snapshot(), 'app')?.enabled === true, 'app enables — "Jira" >=2 matched the installed jira v2 (case-insensitive)');
+});
+
+// ── a dependency whose TARGET has a non-numeric filename version gives a clear message ──
+console.log('\n[N] a non-numeric dependency version yields a clear (not confusing) error');
+await withDir(async (dir, mgr) => {
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'lib__beta.mjs'), P('lib_do'));   // non-numeric version "beta"
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'app__1.mjs'), P('app_do', { lib: '>=1' }));
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  const r = find(mgr.snapshot(), 'app');
+  ok(r?.enabled === false && /non-numeric version "beta"/.test(r.error || ''), 'app off with a clear "non-numeric version" reason');
+  // but a "*" dependency on a non-numeric-versioned plugin is fine (no comparison)
+  await rm(path.join(dir, 'app__1.mjs'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'app2__1.mjs'), P('app2_do', { lib: '*' }));
+  await mgr.reconcile();
+  ok(find(mgr.snapshot(), 'app2')?.enabled === true, 'a "*" dependency on a non-numeric-versioned plugin still works');
+});
+
+// the developer, 2026-10-23, of the datasource plugin reporting 'plugin "weblogic" is not loaded' while weblogic was
+// active: "Could it be plugins not loaded in the right order?"
+console.log('\n[Z] every plugin is loaded before any init runs; a declared dependency is initialised first');
+await withDir(async (dir, mgr) => {
+  globalThis.__initOrder = [];
+  const tool = (v) => `tools:[{ verb:'${v}', name:'${v}', schema:{ description:'d', parameters:{ type:'object', properties:{} } }, impl: async()=>({ content:'x' }) }]`;
+  // "datasource" comes before "weblogic" in the folder; its init asks weblogic for something, as the real one does
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'datasource__1.mjs'), `export default { description:'p', dependencies: { weblogic: true }, ${tool('ds_do')}, init(host) { globalThis.__initOrder.push('datasource'); try { globalThis.__dsSaw = host.use('weblogic').servers(); } catch (e) { globalThis.__dsSaw = 'ERROR ' + e.message; } } };`);
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'weblogic__1.mjs'), `export default { description:'p', ${tool('wls_do')}, exports: { servers: () => ['AdminServer'] }, init() { globalThis.__initOrder.push('weblogic'); } };`);
