=== add test/test-preamble-scan.mjs
new 80fda458acce36b4a51c862a
+/**
+ * SNAPSHOT #77 — did it repeat itself in THIS conversation?
+ *
+ * A session on build 1714 reproduced the repeated-plan behaviour twice, on a build predating every diagnostic
+ * written for it. Raw-log capture has been always-on since July, so that conversation is still on disk with its
+ * complete requests and responses — which makes reproducing it unnecessary.
+ *
+ * It is also, on that project, full of patient data. So the whole design constraint is: read the file where it
+ * sits, report lengths and counts, and let NOTHING out that could identify anything in the conversation. A
+ * diagnostic that leaks its subject is worse than no diagnostic on a machine where the output gets screenshotted.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-preamble-scan.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, writeFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { scanRawLogForPreambles, formatPreambleScan } from '../src/agent/preamble-scan.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+// The production shape, verbatim in structure: the same plan four ways before the first block.
+const PADDED = 'I will write a script to apply all of these updates:\n1. Rename PATIENT_SECRET.\n2. Table the pool.\n\n'
+  + 'I will read index.html, update the labels, and refactor the pool into a table.\n\n'
+  + 'I will update index.html to address all of those requests.\n\n'
+  + 'This includes:\n1. Renaming.\n2. Converting.\n\n'
+  + '<remote-workspace><read path="index.html"/></remote-workspace>';
+const TIGHT = 'Reading the file first.\n<remote-workspace><read path="index.html"/></remote-workspace>';
+
+const entry = (content, extra = {}) => JSON.stringify({
+  id: 'rl_x', type: 'chat_completions', at: Date.now(), durationMs: 216746, model: 'gemini-3.1-pro-preview',
+  request: 'PATIENT_SECRET in the prompt too',
+  response: { choices: [{ message: { role: 'assistant', content } }] },
+  ...extra,
+});
+
+async function withLog(lines, fn) {
+  const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-pscan-'));
+  const file = path.join(dir, 'raw-log.jsonl');
+  await writeFile(file, lines.join('\n') + '\n', 'utf8');
+  try { return await fn(file); } finally { await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  console.log('\n[1] a conversation that repeated itself is identified as such');
+  await withLog([entry(PADDED), entry(TIGHT), entry(PADDED)], async (file) => {
+    const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+    ok(scan.ok && scan.rows.length === 3, `all three replies were read (${scan.rows.length})`);
+    const worst = scan.rows.find((r) => r.intents > 1);
+    ok(worst && worst.intents === 4, `the padded reply counts four statements of intent (${worst && worst.intents})`);
+    const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+    ok(/^REPRODUCED IN THIS CONVERSATION/.test(f.body), 'the verdict says it reproduced');
+    ok(/2 of 3 replies/.test(f.body), 'and how many of how many');
+    ok(f.severity === 'warn', 'flagged (' + f.severity + ')');
+  });
+
+  console.log('\n[2] NOTHING from the conversation leaves the function');
+  await withLog([entry(PADDED)], async (file) => {
+    // The constraint that matters most. This runs against a project whose payloads carry real data, and the
+    // output gets screenshotted. Lengths and counts only.
+    const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+    ok(!/PATIENT_SECRET/.test(f.body), 'no content from the response');
+    ok(!/index\.html/.test(f.body), 'no filenames');
+    ok(!/Rename|refactor|Converting/.test(f.body), 'no fragments of what the model said');
+    ok(/I will|This includes/.test(f.body), 'the intent VERBS are shown, which are the grammar of the pattern rather than anything about the work');
+    const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+    const leaked = JSON.stringify(scan.rows).match(/PATIENT_SECRET|index\.html/);
+    ok(!leaked, 'and the rows themselves carry no content either, so a caller cannot leak it by accident');
+  });
+
+  console.log('\n[2b] the reasoning-token comparison, which needs no new session');
+  {
+    // Three readings say the repeats track how long a call runs. On a model whose thinking is always on, the
+    // question that raises is whether they are long because it thought hard — and the raw log already carries
+    // usage on every call, so this is answerable from calls already recorded rather than by reproducing.
+    const withUsage = (content, ms, think) => JSON.stringify({
+      type: 'chat_completions', at: Date.now(), durationMs: ms, model: 'gemini-3.1-pro-preview', request: 'x',
+      response: { choices: [{ message: { content } }], usage: { completion_tokens_details: { reasoning_tokens: think } } },
+    });
+    await withLog([withUsage(PADDED, 213000, 40000), withUsage(TIGHT, 6000, 500), withUsage(PADDED, 200000, 38000)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/repeats THOUGHT more/.test(f.body), 'the comparison is drawn when the repeats reasoned harder');
+      ok(/no prompt rule of ours reaches/.test(f.body), 'and it says what that rules out');
+    });
+    // The opposite reading has to be reportable too, or the tile only ever confirms the hypothesis.
+    await withLog([withUsage(PADDED, 213000, 500), withUsage(TIGHT, 6000, 40000)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/did NOT think more/.test(f.body), 'a negative result is stated as plainly as a positive one');
+    });
+    // And an endpoint that reports nothing must not be read as reporting zero.
+    await withLog([entry(PADDED), entry(TIGHT)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/reports no reasoning-token count/.test(f.body), 'silence is reported as silence, not as zero thinking');
+    });
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[2c] with no reasoning count, the OUTPUT RATE answers the same question');
+  {
+    // api.genai.mil reports no reasoning tokens at all, so [2b] can never settle anything there and the tile
+    // would end on a shrug. Characters per second is the substitute: generation runs at a roughly steady rate,
+    // so a long call at the same rate simply wrote more, and a long call at a fraction of the rate spent its
+    // time somewhere unwritten. It needs nothing from the endpoint that is not already in the log.
+    const at = (content, ms) => JSON.stringify({
+      type: 'chat_completions', at: Date.now(), durationMs: ms, model: 'gemini-3.1-pro-preview', request: 'x',
+      response: { choices: [{ message: { content } }] },
+    });
+    const clean600 = TIGHT + 'y'.repeat(600 - TIGHT.length); // 600 chars in 6s = 100 ch/s
+
+    // Same rate, more output: the long call was long because it WROTE more, which is a lever we hold.
+    await withLog([at(PADDED + 'x'.repeat(21000), 213000), at(clean600, 6000)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/GENERATED more/.test(f.body), `steady rate reads as generation (${f.body.slice(0, 60)})`);
+      ok(!/somewhere other than writing/.test(f.body), 'and not as hidden time');
+    });
+
+    // A fraction of the rate: minutes that produced almost nothing went somewhere we cannot see.
+    await withLog([at(PADDED, 213000), at(clean600, 6000)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/somewhere other than writing/.test(f.body), 'a stalled rate reads as unwritten time');
+      // It must NOT then name thinking as the answer: with no count reported, a wrapper looks identical, and
+      // naming the wrong one sends someone to argue with an ops team about the wrong thing.
+      ok(/thinking and a wrapper look identical/.test(f.body), 'without claiming which');
+    });
+
+    // The rate line is a fallback, not an override: an endpoint that DOES report reasoning keeps that reading.
+    const withThink = (content, ms, think) => JSON.stringify({
+      type: 'chat_completions', at: Date.now(), durationMs: ms, model: 'm', request: 'x',
+      response: { choices: [{ message: { content } }] , usage: { completion_tokens_details: { reasoning_tokens: think } } },
+    });
+    await withLog([withThink(PADDED, 213000, 40000), withThink(clean600, 6000, 500)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/repeats THOUGHT more/.test(f.body), 'a real count still wins');
+      ok(!/characters a second/.test(f.body), 'and the fallback stays out of the way');
+    });
+
+    // One row per call, so the number can be checked by eye rather than taken on trust.
+    await withLog([at(PADDED + 'x'.repeat(21000), 213000), at(clean600, 6000)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/100 ch\/s/.test(f.body), 'the table carries a ch/s column');
+    });
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[2d] which of the two repetition mechanisms this is');
+  {
+    // The session that reproduced this ran on build 1714 — AFTER 1700 shipped the stream cut — so "the cut fixes
+    // it" is already falsified for whatever this is. The cut aborts on a forged turn boundary and nothing else,
+    // so the question left is whether the restating carries one. It decides where the fix goes, and the two are
+    // indistinguishable to anyone reading the screen.
+    const FORGED = 'I will update the file.\n\n--- developer hi6u ---\nok\n\n--- you hi6u ---\n'
+      + 'I will update the file to address that.\n' + TIGHT;
+    await withLog([entry(FORGED), entry(TIGHT)], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows[0].forged > 0, `the forged heading is counted (${scan.rows[0].forged})`);
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+      ok(/forging a turn boundary/.test(f.body), 'and named as the fabricated-continuation shape');
+      ok(/#74/.test(f.body), 'pointing at the column that says whether the cut caught it');
+      ok(/FORGED/.test(f.body), 'the row is marked, so it can be counted by eye');
+    });
+
+    // The other reading, which is what the production numbers look like: no boundary anywhere, just the same
+    // paragraph written again. Saying so plainly is the point — it stops the next fix being more transport
+    // cutting, which cannot reach prose.
+    await withLog([entry(PADDED), entry(TIGHT)], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows.every((r) => r.forged === 0), 'no boundary is found where there is none');
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+      ok(/blind to this by construction/.test(f.body), 'the cut is reported as unable to see it');
+      ok(!/#74/.test(f.body), 'and nobody is sent to check a column that cannot answer it');
+    });
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[2e] the readings that need no new probe, only fields already in the log');
+  {
+    const tx = (content, extra = {}) => JSON.stringify({
+      type: 'chat_completions', at: Date.now(), durationMs: 9000, model: 'm', request: 'x',
+      response: { choices: [{ message: { content }, finish_reason: extra.finish ?? 'stop' }] },
+      streamed: extra.streamed, endedEarly: extra.endedEarly,
+    });
+
+    // The cut lives on the streaming path. A gateway that answers stream:true with ordinary JSON is handled
+    // silently and correctly, and on those calls no build number means anything.
+    await withLog([tx(PADDED, { streamed: false }), tx(TIGHT, { streamed: true })], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/came back NOT streamed/.test(f.body), 'a non-streamed call is called out');
+      ok(/NOSTREAM/.test(f.body), 'and marked on its row');
+    });
+
+    // Ran out of room is not the same as decided to stop.
+    await withLog([tx(PADDED, { streamed: true, finish: 'length' })], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/token CEILING/.test(f.body), 'a truncated reply is distinguished from a finished one');
+    });
+
+    // THE REPORTED TRIGGER. This is the one claim from real use that no reading has ever tested.
+    await withLog([tx(TIGHT, { streamed: true, endedEarly: true }), tx(PADDED, { streamed: true })], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/DIRECTLY AFTER a call that ended early/.test(f.body), 'a repeat following a cut call is reported');
+    });
+    // ...and the same shape with the cut call elsewhere must NOT be read as the trigger.
+    await withLog([tx(PADDED, { streamed: true }), tx(TIGHT, { streamed: true, endedEarly: true })], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/cancelling is not what triggers it/.test(f.body), 'and the negative is stated rather than left blank');
+    });
+    // An OLD log cannot answer this at all, and must not be read as answering "no". Before 1749 every aborted
+    // stream was logged as a clean finish, so silence there means nothing was recorded, not that nothing happened.
+    await withLog([entry(PADDED), entry(TIGHT)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/silence, not a no/.test(f.body), 'an older log says it cannot answer');
+      ok(!/cancelling is not what triggers it/.test(f.body), 'rather than clearing the hypothesis on no evidence');
+    });
+
+    // ACROSS calls versus INSIDE one call: identical on screen, different fixes.
+    const SAME = 'I will update index.html to rename the button and table the pool.\n' + TIGHT;
+    const SAME2 = 'I will update index.html to rename the button and table the pool, then check it.\n' + TIGHT;
+    await withLog([tx(SAME, { streamed: true }), tx(SAME2, { streamed: true })], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows[1].echo >= 50, `the second reply is measured as echoing the first (${scan.rows[1].echo}%)`);
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+      ok(/across separate calls/.test(f.body), 'and that is reported as the across-calls shape');
+    });
+    await withLog([tx(TIGHT, { streamed: true }), tx('Now writing the file.\n' + TIGHT, { streamed: true })], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(!/across separate calls/.test(f.body), 'two unrelated replies are not called an echo');
+    });
+    // The contrast only reads as a contrast when something was found. With repeats present and no echo between
+    // calls, "it is happening inside single calls" is the useful sentence; with nothing found at all it would be
+    // inventing a place where a bug is not.
+    await withLog([tx(PADDED, { streamed: true }), tx('Unrelated words entirely here now.\n' + TIGHT, { streamed: true })], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/INSIDE single calls rather/.test(f.body), 'with repeats but no echo, the location is stated');
+    });
+    await withLog([tx(TIGHT, { streamed: true }), tx(TIGHT, { streamed: true })], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(!/INSIDE single calls/.test(f.body), 'with nothing found, no location is claimed');
+    });
+
+    // The echo comparison needs the actual prose, so it is held on the row while it runs. It must not survive.
+    await withLog([tx(PADDED, { streamed: true }), tx(PADDED, { streamed: true })], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows.every((r) => r._pre === undefined), 'the text used for the comparison is deleted from every row');
+      ok(!/PATIENT_SECRET|index/.test(JSON.stringify(scan.rows)), 'so the new field leaks nothing either');
+      ok(typeof scan.rows[1].echo === 'number', 'while the number it produced survives');
+    });
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[2f] the stream SHAPE: was it one response, or several glued together upstream?');
+  {
+    // Every repeat in the real log took about 213s regardless of how much it wrote, and the developer saw
+    // PAUSES between the restatements. A model does not pause mid-paragraph. A gateway that re-ran the model
+    // on a failure and appended the new attempt to what it had already streamed would produce exactly that,
+    // and the chunk metadata is the only place it shows.
+    const shaped = (content, streamShape) => JSON.stringify({
+      type: 'chat_completions', at: Date.now(), durationMs: 213000, model: 'm', request: 'x',
+      response: { choices: [{ message: { content }, finish_reason: 'stop' }] }, streamed: true, streamShape,
+    });
+    const one = { chunks: 40, ids: 1, roles: 1, finishes: 1, usages: 1, gaps: [] };
+
+    await withLog([shaped(PADDED, { ...one, ids: 3, roles: 3 })], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows[0].ids === 3, `the completion count is read (${scan.rows[0].ids})`);
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+      ok(/MORE THAN ONE completion id/.test(f.body), 'several completions in one stream is named as concatenation');
+      ok(/not ours to fix in a prompt/.test(f.body), 'and the fix is placed where it belongs');
+      ok(/3id/.test(f.body), 'the row shows the count');
+    });
+
+    // The timing side of the same thing, for an endpoint that hides the ids: a restatement that begins right
+    // where the stream had gone quiet.
+    const second = PADDED.indexOf('I will', 10);
+    await withLog([shaped(PADDED, { ...one, gaps: [{ at: second - 2, ms: 9000 }] })], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows[0].stallsAtIntent === 1, `the stall is matched to the restatement (${scan.rows[0].stallsAtIntent})`);
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+      ok(/right where the stream had been silent/.test(f.body), 'and reported as a re-run seen from the timing side');
+      ok(/1stall\/9s 1@intent/.test(f.body), 'the row carries the stall, its length, and the match');
+    });
+    // A stall somewhere ELSE - mid-list, say - is not a restatement boundary and must not count as one.
+    await withLog([shaped(PADDED, { ...one, gaps: [{ at: PADDED.length - 30, ms: 9000 }] })], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows[0].stallsAtIntent === 0, 'a stall away from any restatement is not matched to one');
+    });
+
+    // The ordinary shape has to be reportable too, because it is the reading that sends the fix to the prompt.
+    await withLog([shaped(PADDED, one)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/stream shape is ordinary/.test(f.body), 'one clean completion is said to be one');
+      ok(/prompt is the place/.test(f.body), 'and the fix is sent to the prompt');
+    });
+
+    // WHAT THE TEXT AFTER A STALL DOES - the one reading the ids cannot give when the endpoint reports one id
+    // for everything. A re-run appended to a first attempt starts over, so the text after the silence repeats
+    // the text before it; a model that paused to think carries on with new words, even if it re-announces.
+    const A = 'I will write a script to apply all of these updates: rename the button, table the pool, highlight the matching columns and expand the note. ';
+    const BLOCK = '<remote-workspace><read path="index.html"/></remote-workspace>';
+    await withLog([shaped(A + String.fromCharCode(10) + A + BLOCK, { ...one, gaps: [{ at: A.length, ms: 12000 }] })], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows[0].stallEcho >= 50, `the text after the stall repeats the text before it (${scan.rows[0].stallEcho}%)`);
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+      ok(/REPEATS the text before them/.test(f.body), 'and that is named as a re-run appended by something upstream');
+      ok(/%rep/.test(f.body), 'the row carries the overlap');
+    });
+    const B = "I'll start by reading the current file to confirm its structure before touching anything else in it. ";
+    await withLog([shaped(A + String.fromCharCode(10) + B + BLOCK, { ...one, gaps: [{ at: A.length, ms: 12000 }] }), shaped(TIGHT, one)], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows[0].intents >= 2 && scan.rows[0].stallsAtIntent === 1, 'the continuation re-announces at the stall');
+      ok(scan.rows[0].stallEcho != null && scan.rows[0].stallEcho <= 25, `but does not repeat what came before (${scan.rows[0].stallEcho}%)`);
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+      ok(/does NOT repeat what came before/.test(f.body), 'and that is named as the model pausing to think, then carrying on');
+      ok(/reasoning effort/.test(f.body), 'with the lever named');
+      ok(/Silence mid-stream was about/.test(f.body), 'and how much of the call was silence');
+    });
+    // WHERE THE TEXT STOPPED when the silence began. A cut-off attempt breaks mid-thought; a deliberate pause
+    // ends a thought first. And how long the silence was against the call's own first-byte wait - with no cache,
+    // one first-byte wait is what a fresh call to the model costs.
+    const CUT = 'I will write a script to apply all of these updates: rename the button, table the';
+    await withLog([shaped(CUT + String.fromCharCode(10) + B + BLOCK, { ...one, ttfbMs: 12000, gaps: [{ at: CUT.length, ms: 12000 }] }), shaped(TIGHT, one)], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows[0].midStalls === 1 && scan.rows[0].midSentence === 1, 'a stall on a half sentence is counted as mid-sentence');
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+      ok(/began MID-SENTENCE/.test(f.body), 'and the verdict calls it a cut-off, not a pause');
+      ok(/about as long as this endpoint/.test(f.body), `and a stall the length of the first-byte wait is read as the cost of a fresh call`);
+      ok(/1\/1cut/.test(f.body), 'the row shows the count');
+    });
+    await withLog([shaped(A + String.fromCharCode(10) + B + BLOCK, { ...one, ttfbMs: 40000, gaps: [{ at: A.length, ms: 12000 }] }), shaped(TIGHT, one)], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows[0].midSentence === 0, 'a stall after a full stop is a boundary');
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+      ok(/at a sentence boundary/.test(f.body), 'and the verdict calls it a pause');
+      ok(/shorter than this endpoint/.test(f.body), 'and a stall much shorter than the first-byte wait is said not to be a fresh call');
+    });
+    // A recording from before 1766 has no ttfbMs; its first-byte wait is the gap at offset 0, and is read as such.
+    await withLog([shaped(A + String.fromCharCode(10) + B + BLOCK, { ...one, gaps: [{ at: 0, ms: 12000 }, { at: A.length, ms: 12000 }] }), shaped(TIGHT, one)], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows[0].ttfbMs === 12000 && scan.rows[0].midStalls === 1, 'the offset-0 gap is the first-byte wait, not a mid-stream stall');
+    });
+    // DID THE MODEL CLOSE ITS TURN? The prompt asks for '/--- you TAG ---' at the end; the cut ends the stream
+    // there, so a close is the last line of the recorded reply. On the production log not one reply had it.
+    const CLOSED = TIGHT + String.fromCharCode(10) + '/--- you ab12 ---';
+    await withLog([shaped(CLOSED, one), shaped(PADDED, one)], async (file) => {
+      const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+      ok(scan.rows[0].closed === true && scan.rows[1].closed === false, 'a reply ending on the delimiter is closed; one that just stops is not');
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+      ok(/1 of 2 replies closed their turn/.test(f.body), 'and the count is reported');
+      ok(/closed  /.test(f.body), 'with the row marked');
+    });
+    await withLog([shaped(PADDED, one), shaped(TIGHT, one)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/NOT ONE of the 2 replies closed its turn/.test(f.body), 'no close anywhere is said loudly - it is the rule not being followed');
+    });
+    await withLog([shaped('--- you ab12 ---' + String.fromCharCode(10) + TIGHT, one)], async (file) => {
+      ok(scanRawLogForPreambles(file).rows[0].openedOwn === true, 'a reply that writes its own opening heading is counted');
+    });
+    // A proper close is recorded consumer-stopped, like a cut. It is the turn ending well, not a cancellation.
+    const closedCut = (content) => JSON.stringify({ type: 'chat_completions', at: Date.now(), durationMs: 9000, model: 'm', request: 'x',
+      response: { choices: [{ message: { content }, finish_reason: null }] }, streamed: true, endedEarly: true, endedBecause: 'consumer-stopped', streamShape: one });
+    await withLog([closedCut(CLOSED), shaped(PADDED, one)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(!/DIRECTLY AFTER a call that ended early/.test(f.body), 'a repeat after a properly closed turn is not blamed on a cancellation');
+    });
+    // And a log from before the shape was recorded must say it cannot answer, not answer "ordinary".
+    await withLog([entry(PADDED)], async (file) => {
+      const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+      ok(/cannot be read from this log/.test(f.body), 'an older log says so');
+      ok(!/stream shape is ordinary/.test(f.body), 'rather than clearing the endpoint on no evidence');
+    });
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[3] a well-behaved conversation is not accused');
+  await withLog([entry(TIGHT), entry(TIGHT)], async (file) => {
+    const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+    ok(/^NOT SEEN HERE/.test(f.body), 'reported as not seen');
+    ok(f.severity === 'ok', 'and not flagged (' + f.severity + ')');
+  });
+
+  console.log('\n[4] long-but-not-repeated is called verbosity, not the reported pattern');
+  await withLog([entry('I will do the thing. ' + 'Here is some detail about it. '.repeat(30) + TIGHT)], async (file) => {
+    // Distinguishing these matters: one is the bug being chased, the other is a model being wordy, and
+    // conflating them would make the verdict useless.
+    const f = formatPreambleScan(scanRawLogForPreambles(file));
+    ok(/^NOT SEEN HERE/.test(f.body), 'not called a repeat');
+    ok(/verbosity rather than the reported pattern/.test(f.body), 'and the distinction is stated');
+  });
+
+  console.log('\n[5] it survives the shapes a real log actually contains');
+  await withLog([
+    'not json at all',
+    JSON.stringify({ type: 'error', at: Date.now(), response: { message: 'boom' } }),
+    JSON.stringify({ type: 'chat_completions', at: Date.now(), response: 'a clipped payload that stayed a string' }),
+    entry(PADDED),
+  ], async (file) => {
+    // A raw log holds error entries, clipped payloads and (after a rotation) a torn first line. A scanner that
+    // threw on any of them would report nothing on exactly the sessions worth reading.
+    const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(file);
+    ok(scan.ok, 'the scan completes');
+    ok(scan.rows.length >= 1, `and finds the real replies among them (${scan.rows.length})`);
+  });
+
+  console.log('\n[6] no log yet is said plainly, not reported as a clean bill of health');
+  {
+    const scan = scanRawLogForPreambles(path.join(tmpdir(), 'definitely-not-here-' + Date.now() + '.jsonl'));
+    ok(!scan.ok, 'the scan reports failure');
+    const f = formatPreambleScan(scan);
+    ok(/no raw log/.test(f.body), `and says why (${f.body.slice(0, 50)})`);
+    ok(!/NOT SEEN HERE/.test(f.body), 'rather than claiming nothing was found');
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n' + pass + ' passed, ' + fail + ' failed');
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main();

+// The PRODUCTION SPEC's own correctness. The spec is only worth running if a ✗ means what it says, so this file
+// scores each assertion against BOTH the behaviour we measured on 2026-08-11 and its opposite — an assertion that
+// cannot fail is decoration, and one that fires on a correct answer is worse than none.
+import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
+import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { buildProdSpec, buildSpecContext, scoreProdSpec, formatProdSpecReport, formatProdSpecProse, formatProdSpecDoc, PROD_SPEC_DOC, runProdSpec, walkKeys, diffEnvelopes, SPEC_LAYERS, ntToolTargets, ntModelIds, ntNamesFromText, ntLabel, ntCacheOf, ntRemaining, ntCacheDoc, ntSlope, ntLagCosts } from '../src/agent/prod-spec.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, label) => { if (c) { pass++; process.stdout.write(`  ok ${label}\n`); } else { fail++; process.stdout.write(`  FAIL ${label}\n`); } };
+
+const ctx = buildSpecContext();
+const SPEC = buildProdSpec(ctx);
+const byName = Object.fromEntries(SPEC.map((a) => [a.name, a]));
+const verdict = (name, text) => { const a = byName[name]; return a.check(text, {}, a); };
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[1] the spec is layered, ordered, and every claim is stated\n');
+{
+  ok(SPEC.length >= 25, `${SPEC.length} assertions`);
+  ok(new Set(SPEC.map((a) => a.name)).size === SPEC.length, 'every assertion has a distinct name');
+  ok(SPEC.every((a) => a.claim && a.claim.length > 15), 'every assertion states what we believe production does');
+  ok(SPEC.every((a) => SPEC_LAYERS.some((l) => l.id === a.layer)), 'every assertion belongs to a declared layer');
+  const layers = [...new Set(SPEC.map((a) => a.layer))];
+  ok(layers.length === SPEC_LAYERS.length && layers.every((l, i) => l === i), 'assertions are grouped by layer, floor first');
+  ok(SPEC.every((a) => ['check', 'checkRuns', 'checkRaw', 'plan'].some((k) => typeof a[k] === 'function')), 'every assertion is measurable');
+  // A claim marked expectBroken is one we ALREADY know production violates — it exists so a change shows up.
+  ok(SPEC.filter((a) => a.expectBroken).length >= 4, 'the known-broken claims are marked as such');

+process.stdout.write('\n[2] L1 delivery — the floor, scored against what api.genai.mil actually did\n');

+  ok(verdict('last-message-arrives', mk('last-message-arrives')).holds === true, 'the last message arriving = the claim holds');
+  ok(verdict('last-message-arrives', 'I have no idea what you mean.').holds === false, 'and its absence breaks the claim');
+

+  ok(verdict('one-turn-back-arrives', back).holds === false, 'if the prior turn starts arriving, the claim breaks — which is how we would notice a fix');
+

+  ok(tokens.length === 10, `the window probe really sends 10 distinct markers (${tokens.length})`);

+  ok(/3\/10/.test(partial.measured || ''), `and the count is measured, not just judged (${partial.measured})`);
+  ok(all10.check(tokens.join(', ')).holds === false, 'all ten arriving also breaks the claim — the belief would be wrong');

+  ok((wj.match(/SPEC-[A-Z]{3}\d+/g) || []).filter((v, i, s) => s.indexOf(v) === i).length === 10, 'and all ten of them');

+  ok(wrapped.check(tokens.slice(7).join(', ')).holds === true, 'any recall at all = nothing is broken in transport; a bare conversation is just ROUTED differently');
+  ok(/3\/10/.test(wrapped.check(tokens.slice(7).join(', ')).measured), 'and the count is what distinguishes a window from a fix');
+

+  ok(big.check(bigMk).holds === true && big.check('There is no marker.').holds === false, 'front-of-message is measured both ways');

+process.stdout.write('\n[3] L2 preamble — its format mandate vs ours\n');
+{

+  ok(verdict('our-format-override-wins', 'Sure! Here you go:\n\nBANANA').holds === false, 'padding it means its rules survived ours');

+  ok(verdict('leaves-symbols-unescaped', 'const ok = a < b && c > d || e_f * g | h;').holds === true, 'a raw line confirms the claim');
+  ok(verdict('leaves-symbols-unescaped', 'const ok = a < b && c > d || e\\_f \\* g \\| h;').holds === false, 'backslash-escaping breaks it');
+  ok(verdict('leaves-symbols-unescaped', 'const ok = a &lt; b &amp;&amp; c &gt; d;').holds === false, 'HTML-escaping breaks it too');
+  ok(verdict('plain-text-when-asked', 'red\ngreen\nblue').holds === true, 'plain text when asked = holds');
+  ok(verdict('plain-text-when-asked', '| Colour |\n|---|\n| red |').holds === false, 'a table instead of plain text breaks it');
+  ok(/TABLE/.test(verdict('plain-text-when-asked', '| Colour |\n|---|\n| red |').note), 'and the note names the shape it used');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[4] L3 delegation — execution is MEASURED, never self-reported\n');
+{
+  const ex = byName['code-really-executes'];
+  const want = createHash('sha256').update(ex.nonce).digest('hex');
+  ok(ex.check(want, {}, ex).holds === true, 'the correct digest of this run\'s nonce = real execution');
+  const faked = ex.check('a'.repeat(64), {}, ex);
+  ok(faked.holds === false && faked.measured === 'FABRICATED', 'a WRONG digest is called fabrication, not a miss');
+  ok(ex.check('(cannot execute)', {}, ex).holds === false, 'declining is recorded as not executing');
+  ok(verdict('our-instruction-survives-delegation', 'The result is <cb>121932631112635269</cb>.').holds === true, 'our tags surviving the hand-off = holds');
+  ok(verdict('our-instruction-survives-delegation', 'The result is 121932631112635269.').holds === false, 'losing them breaks the claim — a formatting rule does not reach the sub-agent');
+
+  // Could we hand the work to ITS environment instead of ours? Every sandbox claim is gated on a nonce digest,
+  // so a plausible-sounding answer that never ran measures NOTHING rather than confirming what we hoped.
+  const net = byName['sandbox-has-no-network'];
+  const D = createHash('sha256').update(net.nonce).digest('hex');
+  ok(net.check(`${D}\nNET-FAIL gaierror`, {}, net).holds === true, 'a REAL run that cannot connect confirms the no-egress claim');
+  ok(/gaierror/.test(net.check(`${D}\nNET-FAIL gaierror`, {}, net).measured), 'and the exception type is kept — it says WHY there is no egress');
+  const egress = net.check(`${D}\nNET-OK`, {}, net);
+  ok(egress.holds === false && egress.measured === 'EGRESS', 'a REAL run that connects breaks the claim — a checkout from its sandbox becomes possible');
+  const unproven = net.check('a'.repeat(64) + '\nNET-FAIL OSError', {}, net);
+  ok(unproven.holds === null && /not executed/.test(unproven.measured), 'a WRONG digest measures nothing — a fabricated NET-FAIL must not confirm no-egress');
+
+  const tc = byName['sandbox-lacks-our-toolchain'];
+  const T = createHash('sha256').update(tc.nonce).digest('hex');
+  ok(tc.check(`${T}\ngit MISSING\nnode MISSING\njava MISSING\nmvn MISSING`, {}, tc).holds === true, 'an empty toolchain confirms it cannot run our stack');
+  const some = tc.check(`${T}\ngit /usr/bin/git\nnode MISSING\njava /usr/bin/java\nmvn MISSING`, {}, tc);
+  ok(some.holds === false && some.measured === 'has git+java', 'and a present one is named, not just counted');
+  ok(tc.check('deadbeef\ngit MISSING', {}, tc).holds === null, 'an unproven toolchain answer measures nothing');
+
+  // 3g came back escaped on the live run, which cannot say WHERE the damage happened. These two separate "the
+  // file is wrong" from "the file is right and only the way out is lossy" — completely different problems.
+  const PAYLOAD = 'let a_1 = x|y && p<q && r>s || "z*w"; /* & */';
+  const bx = byName['file-roundtrip-is-byte-exact'];
+  const good = createHash('sha256').update(PAYLOAD).digest('hex');
+  ok(bx.check(`LEN=${PAYLOAD.length}\nSHA=${good}`).holds === true, 'the right digest means the sandbox holds our bytes and only the DISPLAY escapes them');
+  const wrong = bx.check(`LEN=48\nSHA=${'a'.repeat(64)}`);
+  ok(wrong.holds === false && /before the file, not on the way back/.test(wrong.note), 'a wrong digest puts the corruption BEFORE the file, which is the worse of the two');
+  ok(/it stored the escapes/.test(wrong.note), 'and a byte length longer than the line says why — the backslashes went in');
+  ok(bx.check('I wrote the file successfully.').holds === null, 'a confident sentence with no digest measures nothing');
+  ok(bx.check(`LEN=**${PAYLOAD.length}**\nSHA=${good}`).holds === true, 'and its own bolding does not break the read — a hex digest has nothing to escape');
+
+  const b64 = byName['base64-is-a-clean-channel'];
+  ok(b64.check(Buffer.from(PAYLOAD).toString('base64')).holds === true, 'base64 that decodes back to the payload = a working channel for file content');
+  // The payload as this endpoint was MEASURED mangling it (2e/3g): backslashes before every markdown character.
+  const dirty = b64.check(Buffer.from('let a\\_1 = x\\|y && p<q && r>s || "z\\*w"; /\\* & \\*/').toString('base64'));
+  ok(dirty.holds === false && /no clean channel/.test(dirty.note), 'base64 of ESCAPED content still breaks it — the escaping happened before the encoding');
+  ok(b64.check('Here is the file, it looks fine!').holds === null, 'and prose with no base64 in it measures nothing');
+
+  const rt = byName['body-survives-a-sandbox-round-trip'];
+  ok(rt.check('let a_1 = x|y && p<q && r>s || "z*w"; /* & */', {}, rt).holds === true, 'a byte-exact round trip = holds');
+  ok(/WRONG/.test(rt.check('let a\\_1 = x\\|y && p<q; /* & */', {}, rt).note), 'an escaped round trip names the consequence: the file is written WRONG');
+
+  const st = byName['sandbox-state-persists'];
+  ok(st.check('SPEC-IKO8', {}, st).holds === true, 'the marker coming back proves its sandbox outlives the turn');
+  // NOFILE here means NOTHING, and the note now says so outright rather than hedging. This rung fabricates its own
+  // earlier turn, and a fabricated prior turn never reaches the model — so the file was never written and its
+  // absence is not evidence about the sandbox. The rung that answers the question pins the session (layer 7).
+  ok(/MEANS NOTHING on its own/.test(st.check('NOFILE', {}, st).note), 'and NOFILE is called out as measuring nothing — the earlier turn never arrived, so the file was never written');
+  ok(/pinned-session version/.test(st.check('NOFILE', {}, st).note), 'pointing at the rung that actually answers it');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[5] L4 our protocol — the contract the harness actually needs\n');
+{
+  ok(verdict('emits-our-tool-block', 'Sure.\n<remote-workspace>\n<read path="src/config.ts"/>\n</remote-workspace>').holds === true, 'a real block = holds');
+  const orphan = verdict('emits-our-tool-block', '<read path="src/config.ts"/>');
+  ok(orphan.holds === false && /orphan/.test(orphan.note), 'a BARE verb is named as an orphan (it looks like a call and is a no-op)');
+  ok(verdict('tool-block-not-fenced', '```\n<remote-workspace><read path="a"/></remote-workspace>\n```').holds === false, 'a fenced block breaks the claim — nothing would execute');
+  ok(verdict('write-body-stays-raw', '<remote-workspace><write path="src/note.md">if (a < b && c > d) { x++; }</write></remote-workspace>').holds === true, 'a raw body = holds');
+  ok(verdict('write-body-stays-raw', '<write path="x">if (a &lt; b &amp;&amp; c &gt; d) { x++; }</write>').holds === false, 'an escaped body breaks it — the file would be written wrong');
+  ok(verdict('no-native-tool-call', 'I will use <remote-workspace><read path="a"/></remote-workspace>').holds === true, 'using our verbs = holds');
+  ok(verdict('no-native-tool-call', 'default_api:transfer_to_agent(agent="file_and_coding_agent")').holds === false, 'reaching for its native tools breaks it');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[6] L5 memory — the identifier is measured and NEVER printed\n');
+{
+  ok(verdict('no-cross-conversation-memory', '(nothing retained)').holds === true, 'a clean denial = holds');
+  ok(verdict('no-cross-conversation-memory', 'Yes — I remember you are working on a Java portlet.').holds === false, 'retained content breaks it');
+  const leak = verdict('does-not-volunteer-identity', 'Idempotence means f(f(x))=f(x), philip.d.example.ctr@mail.mil.');
+  ok(leak.holds === false, 'a volunteered identifier breaks the claim');
+  ok(!/@/.test(leak.note || '') && /withheld/i.test(leak.note || ''), `the identifier itself never enters the note (${leak.note})`);
+  // The whole report is screenshotted on a .mil machine — no leaked value may ride along in the evidence field.
+  const run = { model: 'm', at: '2026-08-11T00:00:00Z', results: [{ id: 1, layer: 5, name: 'does-not-volunteer-identity', claim: 'x', holds: false, note: leak.note, evidence: '' }] };
+  ok(!/@/.test(formatProdSpecReport(run)), 'and the rendered report carries no address');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[7] the report is COMPACT — held claims collapse to a list, only breaks get a line\n');
+{
+  const results = [
+    { id: '0a', layer: 0, name: 'answers-at-all', claim: 'c', holds: true },
+    { id: '0b', layer: 0, name: 'long-reply-survives', claim: 'c', holds: true, measured: 'reached 120' },
+    { id: '1b', layer: 1, name: 'one-turn-back-arrives', claim: 'c', holds: false, note: 'one turn back DID arrive', evidence: 'SPEC-WSX2' },
+    { id: '4a', layer: 4, name: 'emits-our-tool-block', claim: 'c', holds: null, note: 'call failed: HTTP 429' },
+  ];
+  const s = scoreProdSpec(results);
+  ok(s.brokenFloor && s.brokenFloor.id === 1, `the lowest broken layer is the floor, not the first listed (L${s.brokenFloor?.id})`);
+  const rep = formatProdSpecReport({ model: 'm', via: 'api.example.mil', at: '2026-08-11T18:09:00Z', results, layersDone: [0, 1], pending: 7 });
+  const lines = rep.split('\n');
+  // The entire point: a run where everything holds must be a handful of lines, because the developer photographs
+  // it. Held claims carry no information beyond their id.
+  // Six now: the Z line names the retired rungs, so an id missing from the results reads as a decision rather
+  // than a run that went wrong. Worth a line — 2h and 3h are quoted in reports already taken.
+  ok(lines.length === 6, `four results render in ${lines.length} lines (header, OK, M, Z, X, ?)`);
+  ok(/^OK 0a,0b$/m.test(rep), 'held claims collapse into one id list');
+  ok(!/answers-at-all/.test(rep), 'and a held claim never spends a line on its name');
+  ok(/^M 0b=reached 120$/m.test(rep), 'measurements survive even when the claim holds');
+  ok(/^X 1b one-turn-back-arrives: one turn back DID arrive ~ SPEC-WSX2$/m.test(rep), 'a break gets ONE line: id, name, why, evidence');
+  ok(/^\? 4a emits-our-tool-block: call failed: HTTP 429$/m.test(rep), 'an unmeasured claim is marked ? and says why');
+  ok(/floor=1/.test(rep) && /layers=0,1/.test(rep) && /pending=7/.test(rep), 'the header carries floor, finished layers and what is left');
+  ok(/via api\.example\.mil/.test(rep), 'the ENDPOINT is named, not just the model');
+  ok(/^SPEC m via api\.example\.mil 08-11 18:09 2\/3 /.test(rep), `the header is one line and starts with the score (${lines[0]})`);
+
+  // An all-holding run is three lines plus the retired list. That is the target.
+  const clean = formatProdSpecReport({ model: 'm', at: '2026-08-11T18:09:00Z', results: results.slice(0, 2) });
+  ok(clean.split('\n').length === 4, `an all-clear run is 4 lines — header, OK, M, retired (${clean.split('\n').length})`);
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[7a] server-side state: are turns LOST, or ignored in favour of its own transcript?\n');
+{
+  // The 08-12 run found a stable `gemini_enterprise.session`. If the endpoint keeps its own transcript, the turns
+  // we send may never have been lost at all — and "lost" and "ignored" have opposite fixes.
+  const a = byName['no-server-side-transcript'];
+  ok(Array.isArray(a.messagesSeq) && a.messagesSeq.length === 2, 'it sends two DIFFERENT conversations, not the same one twice');
+  const second = JSON.stringify(a.messagesSeq[1]);
+  ok(!/SPEC-OLP9/.test(second), 'and the marker is deliberately absent from the second request — that is the whole experiment');
+  const reply = (text, session) => ({ text, raw: session ? { gemini_enterprise: { session } } : {} });
+  ok(a.checkRaw([reply('noted', 'S'), reply('NONE', 'S')], a).holds === true, 'no recall on ONE session = it only sees what we send');
+  const leak = a.checkRaw([reply('noted', 'S'), reply('The marker was SPEC-OLP9.', 'S')], a);
+  ok(leak.holds === false && leak.measured === 'SERVER-STATE', 'recalling what we never sent breaks the claim — it has its own transcript');
+  ok(/IGNORED rather than lost/.test(leak.note), 'and the note names the consequence for the delivery diagnosis');
+  // The hole this closes: if every call gets a fresh session there was never anything to remember, and a NONE
+  // would have been recorded as proof of statelessness.
+  const split = a.checkRaw([reply('noted', 'S1'), reply('NONE', 'S2')], a);
+  ok(split.holds === null && /DIFFERENT sessions/.test(split.note), 'two calls on different sessions measure NOTHING, rather than confirming statelessness');
+
+  // The quadratic-growth hypothesis: our requests already carry the whole conversation, so a session that
+  // accumulated them would hold N copies of a growing conversation by turn N. Billing cannot hide that.
+  const g = byName['prompt-tokens-do-not-grow'];
+  const usage = (...n) => n.map((x) => ({ usage: { prompt_tokens: x } }));
+  ok(g.checkRaw(usage(120, 120, 120), g).holds === true, 'a flat token cost = nothing of ours is accumulating');
+  const grew = g.checkRaw(usage(120, 240, 360), g);
+  ok(grew.holds === false && grew.measured.startsWith('p=120/240/360'), 'a rising cost breaks it, and the actual numbers are reported');
+  ok(/accumulating a transcript we never sent/.test(grew.note), 'and the note names what that would mean');
+  ok(g.checkRaw([{}, {}], g).holds === null, 'an endpoint that reports no usage measures nothing');
+
+  ok(/no-cache-field/.test(g.checkRaw(usage(120, 120, 120), g).measured), 'and it says whether the endpoint reports cached input at all');
+  const withCache = g.checkRaw([{ usage: { prompt_tokens: 120, cached_tokens: 90 } }, { usage: { prompt_tokens: 120 } }], g);
+  ok(/cache:cached_tokens/.test(withCache.measured), 'a cache field is named when there is one — caching cuts cost, never the prompt_tokens count');
+
+  // Flat latency as a conversation grows reads as caching OR as a payload that never arrived. Only the token count
+  // separates them, and getting that backwards sends us to fix a prompt that was never the problem.
+  const recv = byName['big-payload-is-really-received'];
+  const call = (t, ms) => ({ usage: { prompt_tokens: t }, ms });
+  const forwarded = recv.checkRaw([call(10, 900), call(5000, 950)], recv);
+  ok(forwarded.holds === true && /CACHED/.test(forwarded.measured), 'tokens scale + time flat = forwarded and cached, which is the ORIGINAL reading');
+  ok(/NOT gateway truncation/.test(forwarded.detail), 'and it says what that rules out');
+  const cut = recv.checkRaw([call(10, 900), call(14, 890)], recv);
+  ok(cut.holds === false && /TRUNCATES before the model/.test(cut.note), 'tokens flat + time flat = the gateway truncated, and no prompt of ours could survive it');
+  ok(/s=10t\/900ms l=14t\/890ms/.test(cut.measured), 'both numbers are reported, because either alone is ambiguous');
+  ok(recv.checkRaw([call(10, 900), {}], recv).holds === null, 'and a missing count measures nothing');
+
+  const pre = byName['preamble-is-small'];
+  ok(/~1995t above us/.test(pre.checkRaw(usage(2000), pre).measured), 'the preamble above ours is priced in tokens');
+  ok(pre.checkRaw(usage(9000), pre).holds === false, 'and a big one breaks the claim — that is window we never get back');
+
+  // The seq really has to reach the adapter, or the experiment silently becomes "ask the same thing twice".
+  const sent = [];
+  const adapter = { baseURL: 'https://x.test/v1', async complete({ messages }) { sent.push(messages); return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'noted' } }] }; } };
+  await runProdSpec({ adapter, model: 'fake', layers: [1] });
+  ok(sent.some((m) => /Remember this marker/.test(JSON.stringify(m))) && sent.some((m) => /What marker did I ask/.test(JSON.stringify(m))),
+    'both halves of the sequence are really sent');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[7b] the response envelope: is there a handle we could resume from?\n');
+{
+  const keys = walkKeys({ id: 'x', usage: { total: 3 }, choices: [{ message: { content: 'hi' } }, { message: { content: 'ignored' } }] });
+  ok(keys.has('usage.total') && keys.has('choices[0].message.content'), 'the walk is recursive and reaches into arrays');
+  ok(!keys.has('choices[1].message.content'), 'and stops at one array element — a shape dump, not a transcript');
+  ok(walkKeys({ a: { b: 'x' } }).get('a.b') === 'str1', 'a leaf records its SHAPE, never its value');
+  ok(walkKeys({ s: '3f2504e0-4f89-11d3-9a0c-0305e82c3301' }).get('s') === 'uuid', 'an id-shaped string is recognised as one');
+
+  const env = (id, extra = {}, hdr = {}) => ({ raw: { id, object: 'chat.completion', ...extra }, http: { status: 200, headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json', ...hdr } } });
+  const plain = diffEnvelopes(env('req-1'), env('req-2'));
+  ok(plain.body.varies.includes('id') && plain.body.stable.includes('object'), 'a per-request id VARIES; a constant field is stable');
+  ok(plain.candidates.length === 0, 'and neither is mistaken for a session handle');
+  ok(!plain.candidates.includes('object'), 'a stable-but-boring field is not a handle just because it persists');
+
+  const named = diffEnvelopes(env('req-1', { conversation_id: 'abc' }), env('req-2', { conversation_id: 'abc' }));
+  ok(named.candidates.includes('conversation_id'), 'a stable field NAMED like a handle is flagged');
+  const U = '3f2504e0-4f89-11d3-9a0c-0305e82c3301';
+  const anon = diffEnvelopes(env('req-1', { x: U }), env('req-2', { x: U }));
+  ok(anon.candidates.includes('x'), 'and so is a stable uuid under an unhelpful name — a vendor need not name it clearly');
+  const churn = diffEnvelopes(env('r1', { x: U }), env('r2', { x: '00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000' }));
+  ok(churn.candidates.length === 0, 'a uuid that CHANGES every call carries no state and is not flagged');
+  const cookie = diffEnvelopes(env('r1', {}, { 'set-cookie': 's=1' }), env('r2', {}, { 'set-cookie': 's=1' }));
+  ok(cookie.hdr.stable.includes('set-cookie'), 'response HEADERS are walked too — a handle need not be in the body');
+
+  // The verdict, and the guarantee that matters most: no value ever reaches the report.
+  const a = byName['no-session-handle'];
+  const v = a.checkRaw([env('r1', { session_id: 'SECRET-VALUE' }), env('r2', { session_id: 'SECRET-VALUE' })], a);
+  ok(v.holds === false, 'finding a handle BREAKS the claim — which is the good outcome here');
+  ok(!/SECRET-VALUE/.test(JSON.stringify(v)), 'and the handle VALUE never reaches the report');
+  ok(/session_id/.test(v.detail) || /session_id/.test(v.measured), 'the key NAME does, because that is the finding');
+  // A handle stable across two calls seconds apart and one stable across DAYS are different facts — the first is a
+  // resume mechanism, the second is a boundary our conversations share. Only comparing runs separates them.
+  ok(/session_id#[0-9a-f]{6}/.test(v.measured + v.detail), 'a candidate is fingerprinted so runs can be compared over time');
+  const fp = diffEnvelopes(env('r1', { session_id: 'S' }), env('r2', { session_id: 'S' })).fingerprints.session_id;
+  ok(fp === createHash('sha256').update('S').digest('hex').slice(0, 6), 'the fingerprint is a truncated hash, never the handle');
+
+  const none = a.checkRaw([env('r1'), env('r2')], a);
+  ok(none.holds === true && /body-stable:/.test(none.detail), 'no handle = holds, and the key dump is reported either way');
+  ok(a.first === true, 'and it jumps the queue — it is two calls and the rest of the design waits on it');
+  ok(/^D 0d /m.test(formatProdSpecReport({ model: 'm', at: '2026-08-11T18:09:00Z', results: [{ id: '0d', layer: 0, name: 'n', holds: true, detail: 'body-stable:object' }] })), 'a detail gets its own D line');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[7g] the two paths we never compared: streamed vs plain, chat vs work\n');
+{
+  // PRODUCTION STREAMS. turn-stream.mjs calls adapter.completeStream whenever it exists; every probe in this file
+  // has always called adapter.complete. Different request, different handler, and never once compared.
+  const st = byName['streaming-and-not-agree'];
+  const two = (plainKnows, streamKnows) => ({
+    call: async () => ({ text: plainKnows ? 'Port 8443.' : 'UNKNOWN', raw: {} }),
+    callStream: async () => ({ text: streamKnows ? 'Port 8443.' : 'UNKNOWN', raw: {}, streamed: true, chunks: 12 }),
+  });
+  const found = await st.plan(two(false, true));
+  ok(found.holds === false, 'the streamed and plain paths disagreeing BREAKS the claim');
+  ok(found.measured === 'plain=N stream=Y', `and names which path kept the conversation (${found.measured})`);
+  ok(/12 chunks/.test(found.detail), 'the streamed reply reports how many chunks it arrived in — an empty stream and a lost one look alike otherwise');
+  ok(/measuring a path we never ship on/.test(found.note), 'and says what that means: layer 1 tested a path production does not use');
+  const inverted = await st.plan(two(true, false));
+  ok(inverted.holds === false && /production is on the losing path/.test(inverted.note), 'the reverse is a finding too — and a worse one');
+  ok((await st.plan(two(true, true))).holds === true, 'both paths agreeing = the claim holds');
+  ok((await st.plan(two(false, false))).holds === true, 'and both failing agree too — streaming is simply not the difference');
+  const noStream = await st.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'UNKNOWN', raw: {} }), callStream: async () => ({ unsupported: true }) });
+  ok(noStream.holds === null && /was not exercised/.test(noStream.note), 'an adapter that cannot stream measures NOTHING here rather than passing');
+
+  // The other live candidate: root_agent hands work to a sub-agent with a reformulated question rather than the
+  // transcript. If so, recall dies exactly when a request looks like work — which is every request code_boss makes.
+  const dg = byName['delegation-does-not-cost-us-the-history'];
+  let nth = 0;
+  const byTurn = (answers) => async () => ({ text: answers[nth++] ? 'Port 8443.' : 'UNKNOWN', raw: {} });
+  nth = 0;
+  const split = await dg.plan({ call: byTurn([true, false]) });
+  ok(split.holds === false && split.measured === 'chat=Y work=N', 'chatty recalls and worky does not = BROKEN, and that is the delegation hypothesis confirmed');
+  ok(/every request code_boss makes looks like work/.test(split.note), 'with the consequence stated for the product, not the probe');
+  nth = 0;
+  ok((await dg.plan({ call: byTurn([true, true]) })).holds === true, 'both recalling = delegation is not costing us the history');
+
+  // 1f/1g came back EMPTY with finish=stop while T showed 6622 tokens loaded — received in full, answered with
+  // nothing. Conflating that with "the marker did not arrive" is what let it hide inside a delivery verdict.
+  const big = byName['a-big-message-still-gets-an-answer'];
+  ok(String(big.messages[0].content).length > 20000, 'it really sends a ~24k message');
+  ok(big.check('READY').holds === true && big.check('   ').holds === false, 'any answer holds; silence is the finding');
+  ok(/silence, not a refusal/.test(big.check('').note), 'and it is named as silence rather than a refusal');
+
+  // Is the escaping UNCONDITIONAL? Its own rule is about "displaying data", and a fence or a tag is the universal
+  // signal for "not prose". If any wrapper comes back clean the fix is one line of prompt.
+  const esc = byName['escaping-is-unconditional'];
+  const shapes = (cleanSet) => { let i = 0; return async () => ({ text: cleanSet[i++] ? 'const ok = a < b && c > d || e_f * g | h;' : 'const ok = a < b && c > d || e\\_f \\* g \\| h;', raw: {} }); };
+  const allDirty = await esc.plan({ call: shapes([false, false, false]) });
+  ok(allDirty.holds === true && allDirty.measured === 'bare=N fence=N tags=N', 'escaped everywhere = unconditional, which is our current belief');
+  const fenceSaves = await esc.plan({ call: shapes([false, true, false]) });

+  ok(/ask for every file body that way/.test(fenceSaves.note), 'and the note is the fix, not just the finding');
+  const allClean = await esc.plan({ call: shapes([true, true, true]) });
+  ok(allClean.holds === false && /may be intermittent, which is worse/.test(allClean.note), 'nothing escaped at all is suspicious, not a pass');
+}

+process.stdout.write('\n[7f] window vs total loss vs a malformed transcript\n');
+{
+  // "Mostly sees previous messages, clumsier than it should be, sometimes forgets" is the shape of a WINDOW, not
+  // of total loss — and every other rung answers yes/no at ONE depth, which cannot tell those apart.
+  const depth = byName['how-far-back-can-it-see'];
+  // A fake that remembers only the most recent N exchanges.
+  const windowed = (n) => async (messages) => {
+    const kept = messages.slice(Math.max(0, messages.length - 1 - n * 2));

+  const wAll = await depth.plan({ call: windowed(99) });
+  ok(wAll.measured === '1Y 3Y 6Y 10Y' && wAll.holds === true, `full history = every depth answers and the claim holds (${wAll.measured})`);
+  const wNone = await depth.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'UNKNOWN', raw: {} }) });
+  ok(wNone.holds === false && /nothing at any depth/.test(wNone.note), 'and nothing anywhere is called that, rather than reported as a window of 0');

+  const picky = async (messages) => {

+  ok((await dbl.plan({ call: async (m) => ({ text: m.some((x) => /4471/.test(x.content)) ? '4471' : 'NONE', raw: {} }) })).holds === true, 'an endpoint that does not care = holds');

+  // A REAL-shaped working conversation: our prompt, a tool call, a tool result, then a recall question.
+  const real = byName['realistic-conversation-carries'];
+  ok(/system-reminder/.test(JSON.stringify(real.messages)) && /remote-workspace-result/.test(JSON.stringify(real.messages)),
+    'the realistic rung sends OUR prompt with tool traffic in it, not a bare marker conversation');
+  ok(real.check('You said the port must stay 8443.').holds === true, 'recalling the constraint holds');
+  ok(real.check('I do not see any constraint about a port.').holds === false, 'and losing it breaks');
+
+  // The candidate FIX measured alongside the diagnosis, so one run tells us both whether to change assembleConvo
+  // and whether the change would work.
+  const flat = byName['flattening-history-does-not-help'];
+  const onlyFlat = await flat.plan({ call: async (m) => ({ text: m.length === 1 && /8443/.test(m[0].content) ? 'Port 8443.' : 'UNKNOWN', raw: {} }) });
+  ok(onlyFlat.holds === false && onlyFlat.measured === 'turns=N flat=Y', 'flattening working where turns do not BREAKS the claim — that is the fix, proved in the same run');
+  ok(/last message/.test(onlyFlat.note) && /agent loop/.test(onlyFlat.note), 'and the note says where the conversation goes instead');
+  const neither = await flat.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'UNKNOWN', raw: {} }) });
+  // The reasoning here CHANGED on 08-14 and the assertion changed with it. "Neither shape recalled" used to mean
+  // "do not build the flattener". It cannot mean that any more: 0h read a 325k-token flattened transcript front

+  // verdict is that the two have to be read side by side rather than one of them being acted on.
+  ok(neither.holds === true && /contradicts 0h/.test(neither.note), 'if neither shape recalls, it says so CONFLICTS with 0h rather than telling us not to build it');
+  const both = await flat.plan({ call: async (m) => ({ text: JSON.stringify(m).includes('8443') ? 'Port 8443.' : 'UNKNOWN', raw: {} }) });
+  ok(both.holds === true && both.measured === 'turns=Y flat=Y', 'and if both work there was never anything to fix');

+  const desc = byName['can-describe-the-conversation'];
+  ok(desc.check('You asked 2 questions; the first was 2+2.').measured === 'describes it', 'describing the conversation is distinguished');
+  ok(desc.check('You have asked me 2 questions.').measured === 'counts only', 'from merely counting it');
+  ok(desc.check('I have no prior context.').measured === 'neither', 'from having nothing at all');

+process.stdout.write('\n[7e] the reply itself is kept — a scorer is a GUESS about the shape of a right answer\n');
+{
+  // "We asked for one word and matched a substring" is a guess, and a wrong guess reads as a fact about the

+process.stdout.write('\n[7d] the echo test: is layer 1 measuring the endpoint, or our own harness?\n');
+{
+  // Our adapter documents that Gemini 3 returns a thought_signature which "must round-trip verbatim" and that the
+  // agent loop "must echo assistant messages back intact". Every other rung in layer 1 fabricates its assistant
+  // turns, so every zero there may be an artefact of the probe rather than a fact about production.
+  const a = byName['a-fabricated-assistant-turn-is-fine'];
+  const M = 'SPEC-PLM10';
+  // A fake endpoint that HONOURS history only when the assistant object it returned is echoed back intact.
+  const picky = (opts = {}) => {
+    const sent = [];
+    const msg = { role: 'assistant', content: 'noted', thought_signature: 'SIG-ABC' };
+    return {
+      sent,
+      call: async (messages) => {
+        sent.push(messages);
+        const prior = messages.filter((m) => m.role === 'assistant');
+        const trusted = prior.every((m) => m.thought_signature === 'SIG-ABC');
+        const asking = /What marker/.test(String(messages[messages.length - 1].content));
+        const sawIt = messages.some((m) => String(m.content || '').includes(M));
+        const text = !asking ? 'noted' : (opts.alwaysBlind || (!trusted && !opts.blindToSignature) || !sawIt) ? 'NONE' : M;
+        return { text, raw: { choices: [{ message: opts.bareMessage ? { role: 'assistant', content: 'noted' } : msg }] } };
+      },
+    };
+  };
+
+  const picky1 = picky();
+  const v = await a.plan({ call: picky1.call });
+  ok(v.holds === false && v.measured === 'echo=Y fake=N', 'echo works and a hand-written turn does not = the claim BREAKS, and layer 1 is our harness');
+  ok(/our harness, not the endpoint/.test(v.note), 'and the note says which of the two we were measuring');
+  ok(/thought_signature/.test(v.detail), 'the opaque fields on the returned message are named — they are the thing being round-tripped');
+  ok(JSON.stringify(picky1.sent[1]).includes('SIG-ABC') && !JSON.stringify(picky1.sent[2]).includes('SIG-ABC'),
+    'the two calls differ ONLY in whether the real assistant object was echoed');
+
+  const blind = await a.plan({ call: picky({ blindToSignature: true }).call });
+  ok(blind.holds === true && blind.measured === 'echo=Y fake=Y', 'if the endpoint does not care, the claim holds and the marker probes were failing for another reason');
+  const dead = await a.plan({ call: picky({ alwaysBlind: true }).call });
+  ok(dead.holds === true && dead.measured === 'echo=N fake=N', 'and if neither recalls, history really is dropped — the round-trip is not what saves production');
+  const none = await a.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'noted', raw: {} }) });
+  ok(none.holds === null && /no assistant message object/.test(none.note), 'with no assistant object to echo there is nothing to compare');
+  ok(/no fields beyond role\/content/.test((await a.plan({ call: picky({ bareMessage: true }).call })).detail),
+    'and a reply with nothing opaque on it says so, rather than implying a signature that was not there');
+}
