+process.stdout.write('\n[7c] the raw numbers survive the run, for questions not yet asked\n');
+{
+  // Every argument in this investigation turned on a number that was in the response and got discarded. These are
+  // captured for EVERY call, verdict or no verdict.
+  const adapter = {
+    baseURL: 'https://x.test/v1',
+    async complete({ messages }) {
+      return {
+        model: 'gemini-3.1-pro-preview', gemini_enterprise: { session: 'SESSION-VALUE' },
+        usage: { prompt_tokens: 1500 + JSON.stringify(messages).length, completion_tokens: 7 },
+        choices: [{ message: { content: 'READY' }, finish_reason: 'length' }],
+      };
+    },
+  };
+  const run = await runProdSpec({ adapter, model: 'fake', layers: [0] });
+  const rep = formatProdSpecReport(run);
+  const s = run.results.find((r) => r.id === '0a').samples[0];
+  ok(s.sent > 0 && s.p > s.sent, 'what we SENT is recorded next to what they LOADED — the gap is the whole delivery question');
+  ok(s.f === 'length' && s.ses && s.mdl === 'gemini-3.1-pro-preview', 'finish_reason, session and the model they actually used are all kept');
+  ok(!/SESSION-VALUE/.test(JSON.stringify(run)) , 'the session VALUE is never stored — only its fingerprint');
+  ok(/^R ses=[0-9a-f]{6}\(\d+\) mdl=gemini-3\.1-pro-preview\(\d+\)/m.test(rep), 'the report counts DISTINCT sessions and models across the run');
+  ok(/^T .*0a=\d+>\d+\/\d+ms!length/m.test(rep), 'and prints sent>loaded/latency per call, flagging any finish that is not stop');
+  // One session for the whole run means the assertions are NOT independent of each other; a fresh one per call
+  // means 1h and 1i never had a session to measure. Either way the count is the thing that says which.
+  // Only calls that came back WITH a session are counted — a call that never reached the endpoint (this fake
+  // cannot stream, so 1r's streamed half returns unsupported) has no fingerprint and must not inflate the tally.
+  const calls = run.results.reduce((n, r) => n + (r.samples || []).filter((s) => s.ses).length, 0);
+  ok(new RegExp(`ses=[0-9a-f]{6}\\(${calls}\\) `).test(rep), `one session across all ${calls} calls is reported as exactly that`);
+  // R says how many sessions; S says WHICH assertions shared one. That distinction decided the 08-12 run: 1l
+  // recalled a marker while billed 18 prompt_tokens, so it was not in the prompt at all — and 1h ran the same
+  // experiment on two different sessions and recalled nothing.
+  const s10 = rep.split('\n').find((l) => l.startsWith('S '));
+  ok(s10 && /^S [0-9a-f]{6}:[0-6][a-z](\+[0-6][a-z])+/.test(s10), `every assertion on the one session is listed against it (${(s10 || '').slice(0, 40)})`);
+  ok(!/\+(0a|0b)\+\1/.test(s10 || ''), 'an assertion that made several calls on one session is listed once, not once per call');
+  let n2 = 0;
+  const fresh = { baseURL: 'https://x.test/v1', async complete() { n2++; return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'x' } }], gemini_enterprise: { session: 'ses-' + n2 } }; } };
+  const solo = formatProdSpecReport(await runProdSpec({ adapter: fresh, model: 'm', layers: [2] }));
+  ok(/^R ses=/m.test(solo) && !solo.split('\n').some((l) => l.startsWith('S ')), 'and a run where no two calls ever shared a session prints no S line at all');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[8] a grouped run: each group reported as it finishes, and nothing outside the selection\n');
+{
+  const calls = [];
+  const adapter = {
+    baseURL: 'https://api.example.mil/v1',
+    async complete({ messages }) { calls.push(messages); return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'x' }, finish_reason: 'stop' }] }; },
+  };
+  const layerOrder = [];
+  const partials = [];
+  // Layer 0 is in the selection so the `first` queue-jumper has a reason to publish; 4 and 1 give two more groups
+  // to land in sequence. The incremental reporting is what makes a long run readable, and that survives the change
+  // from "these first" to "only these".
+  const run = await runProdSpec({ adapter, model: 'fake', layers: [0, 4, 1], concurrency: 2,
+    onLayerDone: ({ layer, report }) => { layerOrder.push(layer); partials.push(report); } });
+  // Read the ids structurally rather than by pattern: they are the OK list plus the head of each X/? line.
+  const idsIn = (rep) => rep.split('\n').flatMap((l) => (l.startsWith('OK ') ? l.slice(3).split(',')
+    : (l.startsWith('X ') || l.startsWith('? ')) ? [l.split(' ')[1]] : []));
+  ok(layerOrder[0] === 'first', 'a `first` assertion publishes before any group — the cheap answer arrives first');
+  ok(layerOrder.slice(1).join(',') === '0,4,1', `then the SELECTED groups, in the order given (${layerOrder.slice(1).join(',')})`);
+  ok(layerOrder.length === 4, 'and NOTHING else runs afterwards — ticking chooses what runs, not what runs first');
+  ok(idsIn(partials[0]).join() === '0d', `the first report is ONLY the queue-jumper (${idsIn(partials[0]).join()})`);
+  ok(partials[1].split('\n')[0].includes('layers=first,0'), 'the next report says which group finished');
+  const layerIds = idsIn(partials[2]).filter((i) => i[0] !== '0');
+  ok(layerIds.length && layerIds.every((i) => i[0] === '4'), `a group report holds ONLY that group's ids (${[...new Set(layerIds)].join(',')})`);
+  ok(/pending=\d+/.test(partials[0]), 'and says how much is still to come');
+  // Retired rungs never run, so they are not part of what a selection covers.
+  const want = SPEC.filter((a) => [0, 4, 1].includes(a.layer) && !a.retired).length;
+  ok(run.results.length === want, `the final run covers every assertion in the SELECTION and no others (${run.results.length}/${want} of ${SPEC.length})`);
+  ok(run.results.every((r) => [0, 4, 1].includes(r.layer)), 'with no result carrying a layer nobody asked for');
+  ok(run.via === 'api.example.mil', 'the endpoint host is captured');
+  ok(calls.length > want, `repeated assertions really repeat (${calls.length} calls for ${want} assertions)`);
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[9] silence never confirms a claim\n');
+{
+  // Half the spec is a negative assertion ("does not escape", "does not append", "does not leak"), and an empty
+  // reply satisfies every one of them by containing no bad behaviour. A mute endpoint must score ?, not ✓.
+  let calls = 0;
+  const adapter = { baseURL: 'https://x.test/v1', async complete() { calls++; return { choices: [{ message: { content: '' } }] } } };
+  const run = await runProdSpec({ adapter, model: 'fake', layers: [2] });
+  const l2 = run.results.filter((r) => r.layer === 2);
+  ok(l2.length > 0 && l2.every((r) => r.holds !== true), 'a mute endpoint confirms NOTHING in the preamble layer');
+  ok(l2.some((r) => /empty reply TWICE/.test(r.note || '')), 'and says why');
+  // Silence is RETRIED once before it is believed. The 08-12 run lost 0e/1f/1g/1k to a single empty body, and
+  // those were the four rungs the delivery question needed most.
+  // checkRaw rungs are exempt — they score the envelope, which is there whether or not the model spoke — and a
+  // plan rung drives its own calls.
+  const mute = l2.filter((r) => !byName[r.name]?.checkRaw && !byName[r.name]?.plan);
+  ok(mute.length > 0 && mute.every((r) => /empty reply TWICE/.test(r.note || '')) && calls >= mute.length * 2, `a silent reply is retried before it voids a rung (${calls} calls, ${mute.length} text assertions)`);
+  ok(mute.every((r) => r.measured === 'SILENT'), 'and silence is reported as a measurement of its own, not a blank');
+
+  // ...and it must not REFUTE one either. This is the bug the 08-12 live run exposed: `1f`/`1g` were reported as
+  // "a big single message is cut from the FRONT" when the model had simply spent its whole token budget thinking
+  // and said nothing. A break drawn from silence is a break drawn from nothing.
+  // Envelope assertions are exempt on purpose — 1i/1j read prompt_tokens, which is there whether or not the model
+  // spoke — so this is about the ones that score TEXT.
+  const l1 = (await runProdSpec({ adapter, model: 'fake', layers: [1] })).results
+    // The echo rung is exempt for the same reason: an endpoint that says nothing recalls nothing either way, and
+    // "neither shape recalled" is a real (bleak) measurement rather than a hole.
+    .filter((r) => r.layer === 1 && !byName[r.name]?.checkRaw && !byName[r.name]?.plan);
+  ok(l1.length > 0 && l1.every((r) => r.holds === null), 'a mute endpoint also BREAKS nothing — silence cuts both ways');
+  const big = l1.find((r) => r.name === 'front-of-a-big-message-arrives');
+  ok(big && big.holds === null, 'the big-message claim in particular is ? on an empty reply, not ✗');
+  ok(!SPEC.some((a) => a.maxTokens && a.maxTokens < 2000), 'and no assertion runs on a budget a thinking model would spend entirely on reasoning');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[10] the 08-12 run\'s own contradictions: really-sent turns, attachments, size, and the handle\n');
+{
+  // 1l recalled its marker twice while 1b, 1c, 1m, 1n and 6a (0/4) all failed on the same three-message shape.
+  // The only thing 1l did differently is that its first turn was genuinely SENT before being asked about.
+  const t = byName['a-turn-we-actually-sent-is-remembered'];
+  // An endpoint that remembers what it PROCESSED and ignores the array. This is the hypothesis under test, and it
+  // is the one that would explain a production system working on top of a probe that says it cannot.
+  const memoried = () => {
+    let remembered = null;
+    return async (messages) => {
+      // Only a marker in the LAST message was really sent — everything earlier is a prior turn we are CLAIMING
+      // happened, and this endpoint does not take our word for it.
+      const last = String(messages[messages.length - 1]?.content || '');
+      const fresh = last.match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/);
+      if (fresh) remembered = fresh[0];
+      if (!/What marker/.test(last)) return { text: 'noted', raw: {} };
+      return { text: remembered || 'NONE', raw: {} };
+    };
+  };
+  const server = await t.plan({ call: memoried() });
+  ok(server.holds === false, 'an endpoint that remembers what it PROCESSED breaks the claim');
+  ok(/real-only=3\/3/.test(server.measured), `and the real-only arm is the one that recalls (${server.measured})`);
+  ok(/array=0\/3/.test(server.measured), 'while a history that only ever existed in the array recalls nothing');
+  ok(/running on ITS memory/.test(server.note), 'and it says what that means for production');
+  ok(/session across the two calls/.test(server.detail), 'the session fingerprints ride along — recall across DIFFERENT sessions could not be server-side memory');
+
+  // The opposite endpoint: reads the whole array, remembers nothing of its own. Every arm that contains the
+  // marker recalls; real-only does not, because we deliberately did not send it.
+  const arrayReader = async (messages) => {
+    const last = String(messages[messages.length - 1]?.content || '');
+    if (!/What marker/.test(last)) return { text: 'noted', raw: {} };
+    const m = messages.map((x) => String(x.content || '')).join(' ').match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/);
+    return { text: m ? m[0] : 'NONE', raw: {} };
+  };
+  const arr = await t.plan({ call: arrayReader });
+  ok(arr.holds === true && /array=3\/3/.test(arr.measured), `an endpoint that honours the array holds the claim (${arr.measured})`);
+  // And the shape that would explain everything: the array is honoured, but only for turns that really happened.
+  const verifying = () => {
+    const seen = new Set();
+    return async (messages) => {
+      const last = String(messages[messages.length - 1]?.content || '');
+      for (const k of last.matchAll(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/g)) seen.add(k[0]);
+      if (!/What marker/.test(last)) return { text: 'noted', raw: {} };
+      const inArray = (messages.map((x) => String(x.content || '')).join(' ').match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/g) || []);
+      const ok2 = inArray.find((k) => seen.has(k));
+      return { text: ok2 || 'NONE', raw: {} };
+    };
+  };
+  const v = await t.plan({ call: verifying() });
+  ok(v.holds === false && /really sent/.test(v.note), 'and "the array counts only for turns that really happened" is called out as its own verdict');
+  ok(/real\+array=3\/3/.test(v.measured) && /array=0\/3/.test(v.measured), `which is exactly the 1l-vs-1b split (${v.measured})`);
+
+  // Every arm uses a FRESH marker. Reusing one would let a recall in arm three be the endpoint remembering arm one.
+  const markers = new Set();
+  await t.plan({ call: async (messages) => {
+    for (const m of messages) for (const k of String(m.content || '').matchAll(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/g)) markers.add(k[0]);
+    return { text: 'NONE', raw: {} };
+  } });
+  ok(markers.size === 9, `each of the nine arms plants a marker that has never been used before (${markers.size})`);
+
+  // ── 1u: the tags 1c volunteered ──
+  const u = byName['the-conversation-is-not-an-attachment'];
+  const says = (withHist, alone, tags) => {
+    let n = 0;
+    return async () => ({ text: [withHist, alone, tags][n++] || '', raw: {} });
+  };
+  const converted = await u.plan({ call: says('ATTACHMENTS: 1 | NAMES: conversation.txt | FIRST-80: Project note', 'ATTACHMENTS: 0 | NAMES: NONE | FIRST-80: NONE', '<start_of_user_uploaded_file:\n<end_of_user_uploaded_file:') });
+  ok(converted.holds === false, 'attachments that appear only WHEN we send history break the claim');
+  ok(/converted into uploaded documents/.test(converted.note), 'and name the mechanism: our conversation is being uploaded, not conversed');
+  ok(/with-history=Y alone=N/.test(converted.measured), `with the control arm right there in the measurement (${converted.measured})`);
+  const furniture = await u.plan({ call: says('ATTACHMENTS: 2 | NAMES: a, b', 'ATTACHMENTS: 2 | NAMES: a, b', '<saved_memories>') });
+  ok(furniture.holds === true && /belongs to their harness/.test(furniture.detail), 'the same tags with NO history of ours is the control that exonerates them — 1c was reporting furniture');
+  const clean = await u.plan({ call: says('ATTACHMENTS: 0 | NAMES: NONE', 'ATTACHMENTS: 0 | NAMES: NONE', 'NONE') });
+  ok(clean.holds === true && /alone=N/.test(clean.measured), 'and a clean endpoint holds');
+
+  // ── 1v: what the fix costs at real size ──
+  const vv = byName['flattening-survives-a-real-sized-conversation'];
+  const ceiling = await vv.plan({ call: async (messages) => {
+    const body = String(messages[0].content);
+    const m = body.match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/);
+    // Recalls up to 16k, silent past it — the shape 0e/1f/1g/1k showed on the live run.
+    if (body.length > 20000) return { text: '', raw: {}, usage: { prompt_tokens: 7 } };
+    return { text: m[0], raw: {}, usage: { prompt_tokens: Math.round(body.length / 4) } };
+  } });
+  ok(ceiling.holds === false, 'a flattened conversation that stops working above a size breaks the claim');
+  ok(/2kY 8kY 16kY 24kE 32kE/.test(ceiling.measured), `the profile shows recall, miss and SILENCE separately (${ceiling.measured})`);
+  ok(/up to ~16k characters/.test(ceiling.note), 'and states the budget a fix has to fit inside');
+  ok(/24k=7t/.test(ceiling.detail), `prompt_tokens per size rides along — where the count stops tracking the payload is where the gateway stops inlining it (${ceiling.detail})`);
+  const roomy = await vv.plan({ call: async (messages) => ({ text: String(messages[0].content).match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/)[0], raw: {}, usage: { prompt_tokens: 900 } }) });
+  ok(roomy.holds === true && /32kY/.test(roomy.measured), 'and an endpoint that carries every size holds it');
+
+  // ── 1w: can we send the handle back? ──
+  // The envelope hands us a STABLE gemini_enterprise.session and a VARYING gemini_enterprise.assist_token — a
+  // thread id plus a per-call position, which is what a resume handle looks like. So the strongest arm echoes the
+  // WHOLE object verbatim rather than rebuilding the one field we think matters.
+  const w = byName['the-handle-cannot-be-sent-back'];
+  const ENV = { session: 'SES-1', assist_token: 'AT-9' };
+  // `resumes` decides which arm (if any) continues the conversation, so each shape can be tested on its own.
+  const endpoint = (resumes, opts = {}) => {
+    const bodies = [];
+    let planted = null;
+    const fn = async (messages, o) => {
+      bodies.push(o?.extra || null);
+      const last = String(messages[messages.length - 1]?.content || '');
+      const found = last.match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/);
+      if (found) planted = found[0];
+      const raw = { gemini_enterprise: opts.freshSession ? { ...ENV, session: 'SES-' + bodies.length } : ENV };
+      if (!/What marker/.test(last)) return { text: 'noted', raw };
+      if (opts.throwOn && o?.extra && opts.throwOn(o.extra)) throw new Error('HTTP 400 unknown field');
+      const shape = !o?.extra ? 'bare'
+        : o.extra.session ? 'toplevel'
+        : o.extra.gemini_enterprise?.assist_token ? 'verbatim' : 'session';
+      return { text: shape === resumes ? planted : 'NONE', raw };
+    };
+    return { fn, bodies };
+  };
+  const verb = endpoint('verbatim');
+  const pin = await w.plan({ call: verb.fn });
+  ok(pin.holds === false, 'a handle that RESUMES the conversation breaks the claim — in the best possible way');
+  ok(/as "verbatim"/.test(pin.note), 'and names WHICH shape worked, because that is the one we would ship');
+  ok(/verb=Y/.test(pin.measured) && /sess=N/.test(pin.measured) && /bare=N/.test(pin.measured), `each shape is scored separately, against a no-handle control (${pin.measured})`);
+  const sentVerbatim = verb.bodies.find((b) => b?.gemini_enterprise?.assist_token);
+  ok(sentVerbatim && sentVerbatim.gemini_enterprise.session === 'SES-1', 'the WHOLE object goes back out, assist_token and all — not a field we reconstructed');
+  ok(verb.bodies.some((b) => b?.gemini_enterprise && !b.gemini_enterprise.assist_token), 'the session-only shape is tried too, in case the extra field is what gets rejected');
+  ok(verb.bodies.some((b) => b?.session === 'SES-1'), 'and a plain top-level session field, in case their nesting is response-only');
+  ok(verb.bodies[verb.bodies.length - 1] === null, 'the control goes last and carries no handle at all');
+
+  const sessOnly = await w.plan({ call: endpoint('session').fn });
+  ok(sessOnly.holds === false && /as "session"/.test(sessOnly.note), 'and the session-only shape winning is reported as its own answer');
+
+  const inert = await w.plan({ call: endpoint('none').fn });
+  ok(inert.holds === true && /verb=N\+same/.test(inert.measured), `an accepted-but-inert handle holds, and says it came back on the same session (${inert.measured})`);
+  ok(/carries no transcript/.test(inert.detail), 'which is a different fact from being rejected, and worth the distinction');
+
+  const rejects = await w.plan({ call: endpoint('none', { throwOn: (e) => !!e.gemini_enterprise?.assist_token }).fn });
+  ok(rejects.holds === true && /verb=ERR/.test(rejects.measured), `a 400 on one shape costs us only that shape (${rejects.measured})`);
+  ok(/rejected: verbatim/.test(rejects.detail) && /sess=N/.test(rejects.measured), 'the arms after it still run, and the report says which one the endpoint refused');
+
+  // If the control recalls, no handle did anything — the endpoint simply remembered, and this rung measured
+  // something else entirely. That must not read as "handles do not work".
+  const remembers = await w.plan({ call: endpoint('bare').fn });
+  ok(remembers.holds === null && /CONTROL recalled/.test(remembers.note), 'a control that recalls with NO handle voids the rung rather than confirming it');
+
+  const noHandle = await w.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'noted', raw: {} }) });
+  ok(noHandle.holds === null && /nothing to send/.test(noHandle.note), 'and with nothing handle-shaped in the envelope there is nothing to measure');
+
+  // ── 1x RETIRED — its assertions went with it. ──
+  // It compared what arrived from a one-message request against a twenty-message one, to find out whether our
+  // array shape was delivering the static prompt. It was not: 438 tokens against 5,874. Acting on that removed
+  // the array, so both arms of the comparison now build the same single message. A rung comparing a thing to
+  // itself cannot fail, and a test asserting that it can is worse than no test.
+
+  // ── 1y: is role:'system' stripped, or delivered and overruled? ──
+  const y = byName['the-system-role-is-stripped-not-outranked'];
+  const sys = (opts) => async (messages) => {
+    const s = messages.find((mm) => mm.role === 'system');
+    const p = 5 + (s && opts.billed ? Math.round(String(s.content).length / 4) : 0);
+    const last = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+    if (!/deployment marker/.test(last)) return { text: 'OK', raw: {}, usage: { prompt_tokens: p } };
+    const mk = s ? String(s.content).match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/) : null;
+    return { text: opts.obeyed && mk ? mk[0] : 'NONE', raw: {}, usage: { prompt_tokens: p } };
+  };
+  const stripped = await y.plan({ call: sys({ billed: false, obeyed: false }) });
+  ok(stripped.holds === true, "a system message that costs nothing confirms it is stripped — the belief the adapter's design rests on");
+  ok(/sys=\+0t of ~2\d{3}t/.test(stripped.measured), `and the measurement is the delta against what it should have cost (${stripped.measured})`);
+  const overruled = await y.plan({ call: sys({ billed: true, obeyed: false }) });
+  ok(overruled.holds === false && /overruled rather than stripped/.test(overruled.note), 'billed but not obeyed is a PRECEDENCE problem, and named as one — the opposite fix');
+  const channel = await y.plan({ call: sys({ billed: true, obeyed: true }) });
+  ok(channel.holds === false && /that is where our prompt should ride/.test(channel.note), 'billed AND recalled means we found a delivered channel that is not the last user message');
+  ok(/recall=Y/.test(channel.measured), `with the recall arm scored separately from the bill (${channel.measured})`);
+
+  // ── 1z: the last ELEMENT, or the last USER element? ──
+  const z = byName['a-trailing-assistant-turn-is-not-a-channel'];
+  const prefill = await z.plan({ call: async (messages) => ({ text: String(messages[messages.length - 1].content).match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/)[0], raw: {} }) });
+  ok(prefill.holds === false && prefill.measured === 'trailing-assistant', 'an endpoint that takes the last ELEMENT breaks the claim — a trailing assistant turn would be writable');
+  ok(/a writable channel nobody has used/.test(prefill.note), 'and says what that would give us');
+  ok((await z.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'BANANA', raw: {} }) })).measured === 'last-user', 'obeying the last USER message instead is the claim holding');
+  const mute = await z.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'I cannot help with that.', raw: {} }) });
+  ok(mute.holds === null && /cannot be told apart from a refusal/.test(mute.note), 'and obeying neither measures nothing — a refusal is not evidence for either');
+
+  // ── 5d: enumerate rather than reassure ──
+  const md = byName['holds-no-saved-memories'];
+  const listed = md.check('Project preferences\nPreferred language\nDeployment target');
+  ok(listed.holds === false && listed.measured === '3 listed', `it counts what was enumerated (${listed.measured})`);
+  ok(!/Preferred language/.test(JSON.stringify(listed)), 'and never carries a title into the report — this runs on a .mil box');
+  ok(md.check('NONE').holds === true, 'a clean NONE holds');
+  ok(md.check('I do not have any saved memories about this account.').holds === true, 'and so does a prose denial');
+}

+process.stdout.write('\n[11] layer 7 — the architecture the 08-12 17:47 run implies, measured before it is built\n');
+{
+  // ids are quoted back in three characters, so the 27th assertion in a layer must not become `1{`.
+  ok(SPEC.every((a) => new RegExp(`^[0-${SPEC_LAYERS.length - 1}][a-z]{1,2}$`).test(a.id)), 'every id is still layer + one or two letters past z');
+  ok(new Set(SPEC.map((a) => a.id)).size === SPEC.length, 'and every id is distinct');

+  ok(SPEC_LAYERS.some((l) => l.id === 7), 'layer 7 is declared, so the UI picker and the report can name it');

+  // ── 7a: can we stop resending? ──
+  // Every turn after the first sends ONE message plus the handle. The control does the same four turns with no
+  // handle at all: without it, a recall proves the endpoint remembers something, not that our pinning reached it.

+    const seen = [];

+      n++;
+      const body = messages.map((mm) => String(mm.content || '')).join(' ');

+      if (!asking) { if (o?.extra || n === 1) seen.push((body.match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/) || [])[0]); else seen.length = 0, seen.push((body.match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/) || [])[0]); return { text: 'noted', raw: { gemini_enterprise: ENV(n) }, usage: { prompt_tokens: 20 } }; }
+      const carries = opts.pinnedCarries === false ? false : !!o?.extra;
+      return { text: carries ? seen[0] : 'NONE', raw: { gemini_enterprise: ENV(n) }, usage: { prompt_tokens: 20 } };

+  ok(/pinned=Y streamed=Y unpinned=N/.test(carried.measured), `plain, streamed and the no-handle control are scored separately (${carried.measured})`);

+  ok(/prompt_tokens per turn: 20\/20\/20\/20/.test(carried.detail), 'with the per-turn cost printed, because flat cost is what proves the transcript is theirs');

+  ok(failed.holds === false && /resumes a call, not a conversation/.test(failed.note), 'a handle that carried one turn but not four is called out as exactly that');
+  const noEnv = await a7.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'noted', raw: {} }), callStream: async () => ({ text: 'noted', raw: {} }) });
+  ok(noEnv.holds === null && /no session to pin/.test(noEnv.note), 'and with no handle from turn one there is nothing to measure');
+
+  // ── 7b: is the session a function of what we send? ──
+  const b7 = byName['the-session-is-not-derived-from-what-we-send'];
+  const derived = await b7.plan({ call: async (messages) => {
+    const first = String(messages[0].content || '');
+    const m = first.match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/);
+    // Keyed on the WHOLE opening: both openings start "A detail for later: ", so a prefix would collide and the
+    // fake would answer a question the rung never asked.
+    return { text: m ? m[0] : 'NONE', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S-' + first } } };
+  } });
+  ok(derived.holds === false, 'a session that follows the conversation we send breaks the claim');
+  ok(/same-opening=same other=diff/.test(derived.measured), `with both comparisons made against the first call (${derived.measured})`);
+  ok(/shortening the payload would lose it silently/.test(derived.note), 'and names the trap: trimming what we send would cost us the conversation with no error');
+  const sticky = await b7.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'NONE', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S-always' } } }) });
+  ok(sticky.holds === true && /connection or timing/.test(sticky.detail), 'one session regardless of content holds it, and offers the other explanation');
+
+  // ── 7c RETIRED — same reason, one level down. ──
+  // This priced the static prompt on the FIRST message against the LAST and is the measurement that decided the
+  // rewrite. There is no placement left to get wrong: assembleConvo emits one message, so the two arms it
+  // compared are now the same call.

+  // ── 0f: is the silence about size, or about repetition? ──
+  const f0 = byName['silence-is-about-size-not-repetition'];
+  const rep = async (messages) => {
+    const c = String(messages[0].content || '');
+    const mute = /(Filler line for bulk\. ){20}/.test(c);
+    return { text: mute ? '' : (c.match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/) || [''])[0], raw: {}, usage: { prompt_tokens: Math.round(c.length / 4) } };
+  };
+  const repeats = await f0.plan({ call: rep });
+  ok(repeats.holds === false && /repeated=SILENT varied=answered/.test(repeats.measured), `repetition muting it while the same size of varied text does not is the finding (${repeats.measured})`);
+  ok(/our compaction summaries and file dumps look like/.test(repeats.note), 'and it says why we care — that is a shape we generate ourselves');
+  const bothMute = await f0.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: '', raw: {}, usage: { prompt_tokens: 6000 } }) });
+  ok(bothMute.holds === false && /it is the size after all/.test(bothMute.note), 'both going silent is the other answer, and a ceiling every big message must stay under');
+  ok((await f0.plan({ call: async (messages) => ({ text: (String(messages[0].content).match(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/) || [''])[0], raw: {}, usage: {} }) })).holds === true, 'and an endpoint that answers both holds the claim');

+process.stdout.write('\n[12] what the 18:22 run left open: do RULES persist, does OUR shape work, and is escaping a coin flip\n');
+{
+  // The M line clipped `7a=pinned=Y streamed=` at exactly 18 characters, losing the streamed arm — the only one
+  // that speaks for the path production ships on. M is exempt from the screenshot cap, so width is free.
+  const wide = formatProdSpecReport({
+    model: 'm', endpoint: 'x', started: Date.now(), layers: [], pending: 0,

+  });
+  ok(/M 7a=pinned=Y streamed=Y unpinned=N/.test(wide), 'a 30-character measurement now survives the M line intact');
+  ok(SPEC.every((a) => !a.measuredExample || a.measuredExample.length <= 40), 'and nothing in the spec relies on more than the 40 it allows');

+  // ── 7d: a marker is not a rule ──

+  // The rung plants its own marker, so echo whatever it sent rather than a literal.
+  let planted7e = null;

+  const lostResult = await e7.plan({ call: wrap7e({ marker: true, result: false }) });
+  ok(lostResult.holds === false && /drops what our tools return/.test(lostResult.note), 'keeping the task but losing the tool RESULT is called out separately — that is most of a coding turn');
+  const lostBoth = await e7.plan({ call: wrap7e({ marker: false, result: false }) });
+  ok(lostBoth.holds === false && /works for chat and not for the shape/.test(lostBoth.note), 'and losing both means the handle works for chat and not for us');
+
+  // ── 6d: escaping flipped between two runs 35 minutes apart ──
+  const g6 = byName['escaping-is-consistent'];
+  const CLEAN = '<remote-workspace>\n<write path="ok.js">const ok = a < b && c > d || e_f * g | h;</write>\n</remote-workspace>';
+  const DIRTY = CLEAN.replace('e_f * g | h', 'e\\_f \\* g \\| h');
+  ok(g6.checkRuns([CLEAN, CLEAN, CLEAN, CLEAN]).holds === true, 'four clean runs = consistent');
+  ok(g6.checkRuns([DIRTY, DIRTY, DIRTY, DIRTY]).holds === true, 'and four escaped runs are consistent too — bad, but not a coin flip');
+  const flaky = g6.checkRuns([CLEAN, DIRTY, CLEAN, CLEAN]);
+  ok(flaky.holds === false && flaky.measured === '3/4 clean', `a mix is the finding, reported as a RATE (${flaky.measured})`);
+  ok(/no wrapper can be trusted/.test(flaky.note), 'and it says what that costs us: verification after every write');
+  ok(g6.repeat === 4, 'it really runs four times — one sample cannot tell consistent from lucky');

+process.stdout.write('\n[13] native tools, a long session, and the repo framing\n');
+{
+  // ── 4f: nothing has ever put a real `tools` array in the request ──
+  // 4e only checks it does not TALK about default_api. The belief that native tools are unsupported comes from the
+  // same session as the caching theory, and that one was wrong.
+  const f4 = byName['native-tool-calls-are-not-supported'];
+  const sent = [];
+  const withTools = (respond) => async (messages, o) => {
+    sent.push(o?.extra || null);
+    return respond(o);
+  };
+  // The 19:29 run sent ONE shape with tool_choice:'auto', which lets the model DECLINE — and it did, by going to
+  // look in its own sandbox instead. "Accepted and ignored" from that alone cannot tell unsupported from
+  // not-chosen, so four shapes now go out and `required` takes declining off the table.
+  const supports = (shape) => withTools((o) => {
+    const e = o?.extra || {};
+    const is = !e.tools && !e.functions ? 'bare'
+      : e.functions ? 'legacy'
+      : e.tools[0]?.functionDeclarations ? 'gemini'
+      : e.tool_choice === 'required' ? 'required' : 'auto';
+    if (is !== shape) return { text: 'It uses port 8443.', raw: { choices: [{ message: {} }] }, finish: 'stop' };

+    // The legacy shape answers in the legacy field, which is the whole reason for trying it.
+    return { text: '', finish: 'tool_calls', raw: { choices: [{ message: shape === 'legacy' ? { function_call: c.function } : { tool_calls: [c] } }] } };
+  });
+  for (const shape of ['auto', 'required', 'legacy', 'gemini']) {
+    sent.length = 0;
+    const works = await f4.plan({ call: supports(shape) });
+    ok(works.holds === false && new RegExp(`in the "${shape}" shape`).test(works.note), `a tool_call in the ${shape} shape breaks the claim, and names the shape we would ship`);
+  }
+  sent.length = 0;
+  const works = await f4.plan({ call: supports('auto') });
+  ok(/NATIVE FUNCTION CALLING WORKS/.test(works.note), 'called out in those words — it would replace the tag protocol');
+  ok(/their markdown escaping cannot reach into/.test(works.note), 'with the reason it matters: JSON arguments are not prose');
+  ok(sent.length === 5, `four shapes plus a no-tools control (${sent.length} calls)`);
+  ok(sent[0]?.tool_choice === 'auto' && sent[1]?.tool_choice === 'required', 'auto first, then required — the one that takes declining off the table');
+  ok(sent[2]?.functions?.[0]?.name === 'read_file' && !sent[2].tools, 'then the legacy functions/function_call shape a translating gateway may be the only one to know');
+  ok(sent[3]?.tools?.[0]?.functionDeclarations?.[0]?.name === 'read_file', "then Gemini's own shape — this is Gemini behind an OpenAI-compatible surface");
+  ok(sent[4] === null, 'and the control sends no tools at all, so an answer either way is attributable');
+  const ignored = await f4.plan({ call: withTools(() => ({ text: 'It uses port 8443.', raw: { choices: [{ message: {} }] }, finish: 'stop' })) });
+  ok(ignored.holds === true && /including tool_choice:required/.test(ignored.detail), 'every shape ignored holds the claim — and the detail says required was among them');
+  ok(/auto=N required=N legacy=N gemini=N/.test(ignored.measured), `with each shape scored separately (${ignored.measured})`);
+  const rejected = await f4.plan({ call: async (mm, o) => { if (o?.extra) throw new Error('HTTP 400 tools not supported'); return { text: 'x', raw: {} }; } });
+  ok(rejected.holds === true && /every shape was rejected outright/.test(rejected.detail), 'and outright rejection is a different fact, recorded as one');
+  const partial = await f4.plan({ call: async (mm, o) => {
+    if (o?.extra?.functions) throw new Error('HTTP 400 unknown field functions');
+    return { text: 'x', raw: { choices: [{ message: {} }] }, finish: 'stop' };
+  } });
+  ok(partial.holds === true && /legacy=ERR/.test(partial.measured) && /gemini=N/.test(partial.measured), `one shape 400ing costs only that shape (${partial.measured})`);
+
+  // ── 7f: four turns and one fact is not a conversation ──
+  const f7 = byName['a-long-pinned-session-holds-its-detail'];
+  const ANS = { file: 'src/ORANGE-WIDGET-7734.ts', env: 'ITB-3', flag: 'tampTyaCountryOverride', ticket: 'VBA-4471' };
+  const DEC = { file: 'src/ORANGE-WIDGET-7743.ts', env: 'ITB-8', flag: 'tampTyaCountryEnabled', ticket: 'VBA-4417' };
+  const longSession = (mode) => {
+    let asked = 0;
+    const turns = [];
+    return {
+      turns,
+      fn: async (messages, o) => {
+        turns.push({ n: messages.length, pinned: !!o?.extra });
+        const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+        if (!/Without guessing/.test(body)) return { text: 'Understood.', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S', assist_token: 'A' } }, usage: { prompt_tokens: 18 } };
+        const id = ['file', 'env', 'flag', 'ticket'][asked++];
+        const say = mode === 'all' ? ANS[id] : mode === 'decoy' ? DEC[id] : 'UNKNOWN';
+        return { text: say, raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S' } }, usage: { prompt_tokens: 18 } };
+      },
+    };
+  };
+  const good = longSession('all');
+  const held = await f7.plan({ call: good.fn });
+  ok(held.holds === true && /4\/4/.test(held.measured), `a twelve-turn pinned session that keeps every plant holds (${held.measured})`);
+  ok(good.turns.length >= 13, `it really runs a long session (${good.turns.length} turns) — four could not show depth`);
+  ok(good.turns.slice(1).every((t) => t.n === 1 && t.pinned), 'every turn after the first sends ONE message and carries the handle');
+  ok(/prompt_tokens 18→18/.test(held.detail), 'with the token line showing the cost stayed flat across all of them');
+  const confused = await f7.plan({ call: longSession('decoy').fn });
+  ok(confused.holds === false && /NEAR-MISS/.test(confused.note), 'answering with the near-miss is scored apart from forgetting');
+  ok(/clumsier than it should be/.test(confused.note), 'and tied back to the behaviour this whole investigation started from');
+  ok(/file=D env=D flag=D ticket=D/.test(confused.measured), `each plant is scored by name, not just totalled (${confused.measured})`);
+  const thin = await f7.plan({ call: longSession('none').fn });
+  ok(thin.holds === false && /thins out over a real one/.test(thin.note), 'and forgetting everything is the third distinct verdict');
+
+  // ── 8a/8b: the repo framing ──
+  // Both framings get the SAME length of prompt: comparing the repo idea against our real 23k prompt would
+  // measure prompt size and the answer would be worthless.
+  const a8 = byName['the-repo-framing-is-not-better'];
+  // A LADDER, because two arms at 12/12 rank nothing. The 08-13 run had exactly that and read as a tie when it
+  // was a ceiling — neither framing had been pushed hard enough to fail.
+  const emit = { workspace: '<remote-workspace><read path="src/config.ts"/></remote-workspace>', repo: '<repo><checkout path="src/config.ts"/></repo>' };
+  const ladder = (failAt) => async (messages) => {
+    const c = String(messages[0].content);
+    const kind = /<repo>/.test(c) ? 'repo' : 'workspace';
+    const level = /counted/.test(c) ? 'hard' : /make src.http-client/.test(c) ? 'medium' : 'easy';
+    if (failAt[kind] === level) return { text: 'I cannot do that.', raw: {} };
+    return { text: emit[kind], raw: {} };
+  };
+  const tooEasy = await a8.plan({ call: ladder({}) });
+  ok(tooEasy.holds === true && /the ladder is too easy to rank them/.test(tooEasy.note),
+    'neither arm breaking is reported as a fact about the LADDER, not as a tie it did not earn');
+  ok(/workspace=e2m2h2 clean . repo=e2m2h2 clean/.test(tooEasy.measured), `with the per-level scores visible (${tooEasy.measured})`);
+  const repoFurther = await a8.plan({ call: ladder({ workspace: 'hard' }) });
+  ok(repoFurther.holds === false && /got further up the ladder before it broke/.test(repoFurther.note),
+    'and the arm that survives further wins — which is what "push until one breaks" means');
+  ok(/workspace=e2m2h0 broke@hard/.test(repoFurther.measured), `naming the level it broke at (${repoFurther.measured})`);
+  const wsFurther = await a8.plan({ call: ladder({ repo: 'medium' }) });
+  ok(wsFurther.holds === true && /repo=e2m0h2 broke@medium/.test(wsFurther.measured), `the workspace framing surviving further keeps what we ship (${wsFurther.measured})`);
+  ok(/all four criteria required/.test(repoFurther.detail), 'a level counts only if every criterion passed, so partial credit cannot carry an arm up the ladder');
+
+  const b8 = byName['a-commit-does-not-carry-back-what-it-checked-out'];
+  const roundTrip = async (messages) => {
+    const p = String(messages[0].content);
+    const sha = (p.match(/sha="([0-9a-f]+)"/) || [])[1];
+    const body = (p.match(/>([\s\S]*?)<\/repo-result>/) || [])[1];
+    return { text: `<repo>\n<commit path="src/config.ts" base="${sha}">${body}export const RETRIES = 3;\n</commit>\n</repo>`, raw: {} };
+  };
+  const clean8 = await b8.plan({ call: roundTrip });
+  ok(clean8.holds === true && /body=exact sha=Y edit=Y/.test(clean8.measured), `an exact round trip that echoes the SHA holds (${clean8.measured})`);
+  const noSha = await b8.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: '<repo>\n<commit path="src/config.ts">export const RE = /a<b|c>d/g;\nexport const RETRIES = 3;\n</commit>\n</repo>', raw: {} }) });
+  ok(noSha.holds === false && /stale-write check has nothing to compare/.test(noSha.note), 'dropping the SHA kills the whole safety of the idea, and is named as that');
+  const mangled = await b8.plan({ call: async (messages) => {
+    const sha = (String(messages[0].content).match(/sha="([0-9a-f]+)"/) || [])[1];
+    return { text: `<repo>\n<commit path="src/config.ts" base="${sha}">export const RE = /a\\<b\\|c\\>d/g;\nexport const RETRIES = 3;\n</commit>\n</repo>`, raw: {} };
+  } });
+  ok(mangled.holds === false && /the SHA would certify it/.test(mangled.note), 'and an ALTERED body is the worse failure — the SHA would vouch for the corruption');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[13i] status is the axis you run while iterating; layers are the axis you read\n');
+{
+  // Layers are a dependency order — the right question for "what does this measure", the wrong one for "do I need
+  // to run this today". Most of the file is settled and re-running it costs minutes and returns nothing.
+  const open = SPEC.filter((a) => !a.retired && a.status === 'open'), flaky = SPEC.filter((a) => a.status === 'flaky');
+  ok(open.length > 0 && flaky.length > 0, `something is tagged open (${open.length}) and something flaky (${flaky.length})`);
+  ok(SPEC.filter((a) => !a.status && !a.retired).length > open.length, 'and most of it is settled, which is the whole reason a status axis exists');
+  ok(SPEC.every((a) => !a.status || ['open', 'flaky'].includes(a.status)), 'no third status has crept in unnoticed');
+  // FLAKY means it disagreed with ITSELF across runs, not that it is unimportant — those need re-sampling every
+  // time, which is the opposite of skippable.
+  ok(flaky.some((a) => a.name === 'escaping-is-consistent'), 'the escaping rate is flaky — 4/4, 2/4 and 3/4 on three runs');
+  ok(flaky.some((a) => a.name === 'overriding-its-identity-costs-us-nothing'), 'and the identity A/B, which reversed outright');
+
+  let calls = 0;
+  const ad = { baseURL: 'https://x.test/v1', async complete() { calls++; return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'x' } }] }; } };
+  const onlyOpen = await runProdSpec({ adapter: ad, model: 'x', layers: ['open'] });
+  ok(onlyOpen.results.length === open.length, `ticking "open" runs exactly the open rungs (${onlyOpen.results.length}/${open.length})`);
+  ok(onlyOpen.results.every((r) => open.some((a) => a.id === r.id)), 'and nothing else');
+  ok(new Set(onlyOpen.results.map((r) => r.layer)).size > 1, 'across layers — a status selection is not a layer in disguise');
+  const both = await runProdSpec({ adapter: ad, model: 'x', layers: ['open', 'flaky'] });
+  ok(both.results.length === open.length + flaky.length, 'two statuses union rather than intersect');
+  // Numbers and strings share one list, because the picker is one list.
+  const mixed = await runProdSpec({ adapter: ad, model: 'x', layers: [5, 'flaky'] });
+  ok(mixed.results.some((r) => r.layer === 5) && mixed.results.some((r) => flaky.some((a) => a.id === r.id)),
+    'a layer and a status can be ticked together, and both arrive');
+  ok(mixed.results.length === SPEC.filter((a) => a.layer === 5 && !a.retired).length + flaky.length, 'with no double-counting');
+  const all = await runProdSpec({ adapter: ad, model: 'x', layers: [] });
+  ok(onlyOpen.results.length < all.results.length / 3, `and the point of it: open is a fraction of the whole (${onlyOpen.results.length} of ${all.results.length})`);
+  ok(/ran=open/.test(formatProdSpecReport(onlyOpen)), 'the report header says which SELECTION ran, not just which layers it happened to touch');
+  ok(/ran=5,flaky/.test(formatProdSpecReport(mixed)), 'a mixed pick prints verbatim, so a screenshot is self-describing');
+  ok(!/ran=/.test(formatProdSpecReport(all)), 'and a full run says nothing, because there was no selection to report');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[13h] does a checked-out file stay readable, and how far do we go taking its tools?\n');
+{
+  // ── WHAT "CHECKED OUT" MEANS. If a file sent on turn one is still readable on turn seven, a checkout is a
+  // one-off. If not, the checked-out SET is state WE re-send every turn until commit or discard. ──
+  const file = byName['a-file-we-sent-once-stays-readable'];
+  const LINE = 'export const ID_RE = /a<b|c>d/g;';
+  const fileEp = (mode) => async (messages, o) => {
+    const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+    const raw = { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S', assist_token: 'A' } };
+    if (/TIMEOUT_MS set to/.test(body)) return { text: mode === 'gone' ? 'UNKNOWN' : '30_000', raw };
+    if (/Quote the ID_RE line/.test(body)) return { text: mode === 'verbatim' ? LINE : 'CANNOT', raw };
+    return { text: 'noted', raw };
+  };
+  ok((await file.plan({ call: fileEp('verbatim') })).holds === true, 'a file still quotable five turns later holds — a checkout is a one-off');
+  const gist = await file.plan({ call: fileEp('gist') });
+  ok(gist.holds === false && /good enough to discuss, useless to edit against/.test(gist.note),
+    'knowing WHAT is in it but not being able to quote a line is its own outcome — that is the difference between discussing a file and editing it');
+  ok(/re-sent before every commit/.test(gist.note), 'and says what follows for the protocol');
+  const gone = await file.plan({ call: fileEp('gone') });
+  ok(gone.holds === false && /the checked-out SET becomes state WE re-send every turn/.test(gone.note),
+    'and the file being gone entirely names the protocol it forces — bounded, but a different one');
+  ok(/what a summary discards first/.test(gone.detail), 'with why a file is a different question from the short facts the other rungs plant');
+  ok((await file.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'noted', raw: {} }) })).holds === null, 'no handle means the file was never left in a conversation');
+
+  // ── HOW FAR TO GO. 8c settled the identity half; this is the tools half, and it is a separate question. ──
+  const tools = byName['taking-its-own-tools-away-costs-us-nothing'];
+  const toolLadder = (failAt) => async (messages) => {
+    const c = String(messages[0].content);
+    const arm = /Everything else is still yours/.test(c) ? 'both' : 'ours-only';
+    const level = /counted/.test(c) ? 'hard' : /make src.http-client/.test(c) ? 'medium' : 'easy';
+    if (failAt[arm] === level) return { text: 'I cannot do that without the files.', raw: {} };
+    return { text: '<repo><checkout path="src/config.ts"/></repo>', raw: {} };
+  };
+  const permissiveWins = await tools.plan({ call: toolLadder({ 'ours-only': 'medium' }) });
+  ok(permissiveWins.holds === false && /got FURTHER up the ladder/.test(permissiveWins.note),
+    'the permissive arm surviving further is the finding — the same shape as the identity result');
+  ok(/we can state the fact instead/.test(permissiveWins.note),
+    'with the distinction that matters: the project really is out of reach, so that is a fact rather than a prohibition');
+  ok(/ours-only=e2m0h2 broke@medium . both=e2m2h2 clean/.test(permissiveWins.measured), `and where each arm broke (${permissiveWins.measured})`);
+  ok(/is an instruction forbidding a working tool/.test(permissiveWins.detail), 'and the detail states the argument, not just the numbers');
+  ok((await tools.plan({ call: toolLadder({ both: 'medium' }) })).holds === true, 'the restrictive arm surviving further would keep the restriction');
+  const neither = await tools.plan({ call: toolLadder({}) });
+  ok(neither.holds === true && /the ladder cannot rank them/.test(neither.note), 'and neither breaking says the ladder is too easy rather than claiming a tie');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[13g] retiring a rung keeps its id, and the design A/Bs\n');
+{
+  // ── RETIREMENT. Deleting a rung would renumber every rung after it in its layer, and those ids are quoted in
+  // reports already photographed. A retired rung keeps its id and its place, and simply never runs. ──
+  const retired = SPEC.filter((a) => a.retired);
+  ok(retired.length >= 4, `four rungs stopped earning their place and are retired (${retired.map((a) => a.id).join(',')})`);
+  ok(retired.every((a) => typeof a.retired === 'string' && a.retired.length > 20), 'each says WHY, so a missing id reads as a decision rather than an accident');
+  // A retirement reason has to say what happened to the question, not merely that it stopped running. Two
+  // shapes qualify: something REPLACED it, or it was ANSWERED AND ACTED ON — the second is how 1x and 7c
+  // went, where the fix removed the choice they were measuring.
+  ok(retired.every((a) => /supersede|refuted|measured nothing|coin flip|SILENT|asks it pinned|says why|rate instead|one shape now|acted on/i.test(a.retired)), 'and each names what replaced it, or what answering it removed');
+  // The whole point: the ids after a retired one must not move.
+  const l3 = SPEC.filter((a) => a.layer === 3).map((a) => a.id);
+  ok(l3.includes('3h') && l3[l3.indexOf('3h') + 1] === '3i', '3h keeps its slot, so 3i still means what it meant in the reports already taken');
+  ok(SPEC.find((a) => a.id === '3h').name === 'sandbox-state-persists', 'and still names the rung it always named');
+
+  let calls = 0;
+  const ad = { baseURL: 'https://x.test/v1', async complete() { calls++; return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'x' } }] }; } };
+  const run = await runProdSpec({ adapter: ad, model: 'x', layers: [3] });
+  ok(!run.results.some((r) => r.id === '3h'), 'a retired rung produces no result');
+  ok(run.results.length === SPEC.filter((a) => a.layer === 3 && !a.retired).length, 'and every other rung in the layer still runs');
+  const rep = formatProdSpecReport(run);
+  ok(/^Z retired .*3h \(measured nothing/m.test(rep), 'the report names it once with its reason, on its own line');
+  ok(!/OK .*\b3h\b/.test(rep) && !/^X 3h/m.test(rep), 'and never as a result — it did not pass and it did not fail, it did not run');
+
+  // ── THE A/Bs. Each decides something we would otherwise guess at, and each must call a WINNER by name. ──
+  const edit = byName['a-whole-file-commit-is-as-safe-as-a-targeted-edit'];
+  const CANARY = 'export const ID_RE = /a<b|c>d/g;';
+  const editEp = (wholeClean) => async (messages) => {
+    const body = String(messages[0].content);
+    if (/COMPLETE new file body/.test(body)) return { text: wholeClean ? `export const PORT = 8443;\n${CANARY}\nexport const RETRIES = 3;` : 'export const ID\\_RE = /a\\<b\\|c\\>d/g;', raw: {} };
+    return { text: 'FIND: export const TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000;\nREPLACE: export const TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000;\nexport const RETRIES = 3;', raw: {} };
+  };
+  ok((await edit.plan({ call: editEp(true) })).holds === true, 'a whole-file commit that survives as often as an edit holds the claim');
+  const editWins = await edit.plan({ call: editEp(false) });
+  ok(editWins.holds === false && /should lead with an edit verb/.test(editWins.note), 'and the edit surviving more often names the verb the protocol should lead with');
+  ok(/the line it never sends is the line that cannot come back wrong/.test(editWins.note), 'with the reason, which is the whole argument for a diff over a file');
+
+  const fmt = byName['our-xml-tags-are-as-safe-as-a-plainer-format'];
+  const fmtEp = (xmlOk) => async (messages) => {
+    const xml = /<remote-workspace>/.test(String(messages[0].content));
+    if (xml) return { text: xmlOk ? '<remote-workspace><read path="src/config.ts"/></remote-workspace>' : 'I will read the file. <read path="src/config\\_ts"/>', raw: {} };
+    return { text: 'BEGIN WORKSPACE\nREAD path=src/config.ts\nEND WORKSPACE', raw: {} };
+  };
+  ok((await fmt.plan({ call: fmtEp(true) })).holds === true, 'XML holding its own against a plainer syntax keeps the protocol as it is');
+  const plainWins = await fmt.plan({ call: fmtEp(false) });
+  ok(plainWins.holds === false && /our protocol is made of exactly the punctuation it mangles/.test(plainWins.note),
+    'and a plainer format winning is stated as what it is — our tags are built from the characters their formatter breaks');
+
+  const phr = byName['phrasing-does-not-decide-what-survives'];
+  const phrEp = (mode) => async (messages, o) => {
+    const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+    const raw = { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S', assist_token: 'A' } };
+    const d = body.match(/deployment marker is (SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6})/), f = body.match(/rollback marker is (SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6})/);
+    if (d) { phrEp.d = d[1]; phrEp.f = f[1]; return { text: 'noted', raw }; }
+    if (/deployment marker\?/.test(body)) return { text: mode === 'both' ? phrEp.d : 'NONE', raw };
+    if (/rollback marker\?/.test(body)) return { text: mode === 'neither' ? 'NONE' : phrEp.f, raw };
+    return { text: 'noted', raw };
+  };
+  ok((await phr.plan({ call: phrEp('both') })).holds === true, 'both phrasings surviving equally holds the claim');
+  const flaggedOnly = await phr.plan({ call: phrEp('flagged') });
+  ok(flaggedOnly.holds === false && /has to be written as an instruction to retain it/.test(flaggedOnly.note),
+    'only the flagged fact surviving produces a rule for every turn we send, which is the point of the rung');
+  ok(/both facts in ONE message at the same depth/.test(flaggedOnly.detail), 'with the control stated — nothing differs but the wording');
+
+  const flag = byName['asking-for-the-escaped-flag-changes-nothing'];
+  const LINE = 'const ok = a < b && c > d || e_f * g | h;';
+  const flagEp = (helps) => async (messages) => {
+    const asked = /escaped="no"/.test(String(messages[0].content));
+    return { text: (asked && helps) || !helps ? `<write path="ok.js">${LINE}</write>` : '<write path="ok.js">const ok = a \\< b;</write>', raw: {} };
+  };
+  ok((await flag.plan({ call: flagEp(false) })).holds === true, 'the flag making no difference holds the claim — it stays a signal only');
+  const helps = await flag.plan({ call: flagEp(true) });
+  ok(helps.holds === false && /MORE often/.test(helps.note),
+    'and it producing cleaner bodies is worth having even before we trust what it declares');
+  // BOTH DIRECTIONS. The 08-13 run came back 3/3 clean without the flag and 0/3 with it, and the old
+  // `with <= without` branch reported that as the claim HOLDING — a rung swallowing its own most actionable
+  // result. A clean body arriving LESS often breaks "it changes nothing" just as surely as more often.
+  const hurts = await flag.plan({ call: async (messages) => ({ text: /escaped="no"/.test(String(messages[0].content)) ? '<write path="ok.js">const ok = a \< b;</write>' : `<write path="ok.js">${LINE}</write>`, raw: {} }) });
+  ok(hurts.holds === false && /LESS often/.test(hurts.note), 'and it producing DIRTIER bodies is a finding too, not a pass');
+  ok(/asking it to audit its own formatting makes the formatting worse/.test(hurts.note),
+    'stated as what it means: the flag costs us the thing it was meant to protect');
+  ok(/with-flag=0\/3/.test(hurts.measured), `with both rates shown (${hurts.measured})`);
+
+  // The three A/Bs whose signal is CORRUPTION run three reps a side, because 6d measured escaping at 2 of 4 and a
+  // single sample of a coin flip decides nothing. 8f is deliberately one pass: both facts ride the SAME message,
+  // so the two arms are already perfectly controlled and repeating would only add turns.
+  for (const ab of [edit, fmt, flag]) {
+    ok(/\d\/3/.test((await ab.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'x', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S' } } }) })).measured || ''),
+      `${ab.id} runs three reps a side — escaping is a coin flip, so one sample decides nothing`);
+  }
+  ok(/stated-plainly=[YN] asked-to-remember=[YN]/.test((await phr.plan({ call: phrEp('both') })).measured),
+    '8f needs no reps — both phrasings ride one message, so the arms are controlled by construction');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[13f] four mechanisms, six pairs — every pair separated by at least one probe\n');
+{
+  // The claim being tested here is COMPLETENESS: that the four rungs together distinguish all four candidate
+  // mechanisms, not merely that each rung works. A discrimination set with a hole in it is worse than an honest
+  // "we cannot tell", because it produces a confident wrong answer.
+  const cap = byName['the-transcript-has-no-capacity-limit'];
+  const words = byName['it-keeps-our-exact-words'];
+  const order = byName['it-keeps-the-order-of-the-conversation'];
+
+  // ── capacity: a summary has a ceiling; a log and a fact store do not ──
+  const capEp = (keep) => {
+    const all = [];
+    return async (messages, o) => {
+      const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+      const ms = [...body.matchAll(/SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6}/g)].map((x) => x[0]);
+      if (ms.length) { all.push(...ms); return { text: 'noted', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S', assist_token: 'A' } } }; }
+      return { text: (keep === 'all' ? all : keep === 'late' ? all.slice(-12) : all.slice(0, 12)).join(', '), raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S' } } };
+    };
+  };
+  const held = await cap.plan({ call: capEp('all') });
+  ok(held.holds === true && /30\/30/.test(held.measured), `all thirty coming back holds — no ceiling (${held.measured})`);
+  const dropped = await cap.plan({ call: capEp('late') });
+  ok(dropped.holds === false && /drops the oldest, which is a summary or a window rather than a log/.test(dropped.note),
+    'keeping the LATE ones is the summary/window signature, and is named as that');
+  ok(/first10=0/.test(dropped.measured) && /last10=10/.test(dropped.measured), `with the age profile printed, not just a total (${dropped.measured})`);
+  const oddDrop = await cap.plan({ call: capEp('early') });
+  ok(oddDrop.holds === false && /what it drops is not simply the oldest/.test(oddDrop.note), 'and dropping in some other order is a different finding, kept separate');
+
+  // ── exact words: separates a window from a summary, and survives a refusal to quote ──
+  const wordEp = (mode) => async (messages) => {
+    const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+    const raw = { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S', assist_token: 'A' } };
+    if (/decommissioned" or the word "retired"/.test(body)) return { text: mode === 'verbatim' ? 'decommissioned' : 'retired', raw };
+    if (/word for word/.test(body)) return { text: mode === 'verbatim' || mode === 'window' ? 'One more: the nightly export runs at twenty past two.' : 'CANNOT', raw };
+    return { text: 'noted', raw };
+  };
+  ok((await words.plan({ call: wordEp('verbatim') })).holds === true, 'exact word plus a recent quote holds the claim');
+  const win = await words.plan({ call: wordEp('window') });
+  ok(win.holds === false && /a verbatim window over a compressed history/.test(win.note),
+    'recent quotable but the old wording lost is the WINDOW signature — the column that separates it from a summary');
+  const summ = await words.plan({ call: wordEp('summary') });
+  ok(summ.holds === false && /nothing we need later can depend on how we phrased it/.test(summ.note), 'and neither surviving gives the practical rule');
+  ok(/a refusal to QUOTE cannot masquerade as a lost transcript/.test(summ.detail),
+    'with the reason a word-choice question is asked at all — a refusal would otherwise read as summarisation');
+
+  // ── order: the only column separating a narrative summary from a bag of facts ──
+  const orderEp = (mode) => async (messages) => {
+    const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+    const raw = { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S', assist_token: 'A' } };
+    if (!/in the order I raised them/.test(body)) return { text: 'noted', raw };
+    if (mode === 'ordered') return { text: '1. the retry limit\n2. the database migration\n3. the release branch name', raw };
+    if (mode === 'scrambled') return { text: '1. the release branch name\n2. the retry limit\n3. the database migration', raw };
+    return { text: 'I am not sure what we discussed.', raw };
+  };
+  ok((await order.plan({ call: orderEp('ordered') })).holds === true, 'the right three in the right order holds the claim');
+  const scrambled = await order.plan({ call: orderEp('scrambled') });
+  ok(scrambled.holds === false && /a set of facts rather than a record of the conversation/.test(scrambled.note),
+    'having all three but not the order is the FACT-STORE signature');
+  ok(/cannot tell that we changed our mind/.test(scrambled.note), 'and names what that costs us, which is the reason order matters at all');
+  ok(/SCRAMBLED/.test(scrambled.measured), `scored distinctly from simply missing them (${scrambled.measured})`);
+  ok((await order.plan({ call: orderEp('lost') })).holds === false, 'and losing them entirely is its own outcome');
+
+  // ── COMPLETENESS. Four mechanisms, six pairs; each pair must differ in at least one column. ──
+  const predict = {
+    verbatim:  { quoteDeep: 1, quoteRecent: 1, exactWord: 1, thirty: 30, order: 1 },
+    summarised:{ quoteDeep: 0, quoteRecent: 0, exactWord: 0, thirty: 12, order: 1 },
+    windowed:  { quoteDeep: 0, quoteRecent: 1, exactWord: 0, thirty: 12, order: 0 },
+    factStore: { quoteDeep: 0, quoteRecent: 0, exactWord: 0, thirty: 30, order: 0 },
+  };
+  const names = Object.keys(predict);
+  const cols = ['quoteDeep', 'quoteRecent', 'exactWord', 'thirty', 'order'];
+  let pairs = 0, separated = 0;
+  for (let i = 0; i < names.length; i++) {
+    for (let j = i + 1; j < names.length; j++) {
+      pairs++;
+      const a = predict[names[i]], b = predict[names[j]];
+      const differs = cols.filter((c) => a[c] !== b[c]);
+      if (differs.length) separated++;
+      else process.stdout.write(`  (${names[i]} vs ${names[j]} are indistinguishable)\n`);
+    }
+  }
+  ok(pairs === 6 && separated === 6, `all ${pairs} pairs of mechanisms are separated by at least one probe (${separated}/${pairs})`);
+  ok(SPEC.some((a) => a.name === 'the-transcript-keeps-what-we-did-not-flag'), 'and every column in that table has a rung behind it — quote-deep');
+  ok(cols.every((c) => c === 'quoteDeep' || SPEC.some((a) => /exact-words|capacity-limit|order-of-the-conversation/.test(a.name))), 'quote-recent, exact-word, capacity and order');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[13e] is their history a summary, a verbatim record, or a window?\n');
+{
+  // Markers it was TOLD to remember came back from ten turns away; facts stated in passing came back 0 of 4. At
+  // least three mechanisms produce that, and they are not the same thing to build against. Asking it to quote an
+  // early turn WORD FOR WORD separates them: a summary cannot, and no attention failure prevents it.
+  const t7 = byName['the-transcript-keeps-what-we-did-not-flag'];
+  const DEEP = 'the staging cluster was decommissioned on the fourteenth of March';
+  const transcript = (mode) => {
+    let mk = null;
+    return async (messages, o) => {
+      const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+      const p = body.match(/remember this marker for later: (SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6})/);
+      if (p) mk = p[1];
+      const raw = { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S', assist_token: 'A' } };
+      if (/What marker did I ask/.test(body)) return { text: mode === 'nothing' || mode === 'recent-only' ? 'NONE' : mk, raw };
+      if (/staging cluster/.test(body)) return { text: mode === 'verbatim' || mode === 'all' ? 'It was decommissioned in March.' : 'UNKNOWN', raw };
+      if (/build timeout/.test(body)) return { text: mode === 'summary' || mode === 'nothing' ? 'UNKNOWN' : 'Ninety seconds.', raw };
+      if (/word for word/.test(body)) return { text: mode === 'verbatim' ? `Some background before we start. Note that ${DEEP}. Also…` : 'CANNOT', raw };
+      return { text: 'noted', raw };
+    };
+  };
+  const asked = [];
+  const spy = (inner) => async (m, o) => { asked.push(String(m[m.length - 1].content)); return inner(m, o); };
+
+  const verb = await t7.plan({ call: spy(transcript('verbatim')) });
+  ok(verb.holds === true && /verbatim=Y/.test(verb.measured), `quoting the first turn back word for word settles it — the history is verbatim (${verb.measured})`);
+  ok(asked.some((q) => /word for word/.test(q)), 'the decisive question is actually asked');
+  // The fillers are the short turns that carry neither background nor a question — they are what makes the deep
+  // fact actually deep rather than nominally so.
+  const filler = asked.filter((q) => q.length < 40 && !/\?|marker|background|word for word/.test(q));
+  ok(filler.length >= 6, `with ${filler.length} real turns in between, so depth is genuine rather than nominal`);
+  ok(asked.length === 12, `twelve turns in all: background, six fillers, a late fact, and four asks (${asked.length})`);
+
+  const summ = await t7.plan({ call: transcript('summary') });
+  ok(summ.holds === false && /their history is a SUMMARY/.test(summ.note),
+    'kept the flagged marker, lost the unflagged detail, cannot quote it back = summarised');
+  ok(/has to be said as something to remember, not mentioned in passing/.test(summ.note),
+    'and the practical rule falls straight out of it — that is the point of the rung');
+  ok(/flagged=Y unflagged-deep=N unflagged-recent=N verbatim=N/.test(summ.measured), `with all four signals separate (${summ.measured})`);
+
+  // Recent kept, old lost, cannot quote = a verbatim WINDOW over a compressed history — a different fix again.
+  // RECENCY IS TESTED BEFORE FLAGGING. The 08-13 run came back flagged=N deep=N recent=Y and the old branch order
+  // required `flagged` before it would look at recency — so a run where recency was the ONLY survivor reported
+  // "nothing survived at all", the opposite of what it measured.
+  const win = await t7.plan({ call: transcript('window') });
+  ok(win.holds === false && /it holds recent turns and not old ones/.test(win.note), 'recent kept but old lost is reported as a recency bias, not as nothing');
+  ok(/how far back it reaches is what a design has to fit inside/.test(win.note), 'and names the number a design would need');
+  const recentOnly = await t7.plan({ call: transcript('recent-only') });
+  ok(recentOnly.holds === false && /even a flagged marker did not survive the depth/.test(recentOnly.note),
+    'and recency surviving while a FLAGGED marker did not is called out — that combination is what the live run produced');
+
+  const none = await t7.plan({ call: transcript('nothing') });
+  ok(none.holds === false && /they cannot both be right/.test(none.note), 'and nothing surviving anywhere is set against the capacity rung rather than quietly reported');
+  ok((await t7.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'noted', raw: {} }) })).holds === null, 'no handle means no transcript to ask about');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[13d] the assumptions a rewrite would rest on — compaction, the reviewer, parallel turns\n');
+{
+  // ── COMPACTION. If the transcript is theirs we cannot edit it, so the only compaction available is: start a
+  // NEW session, seed it with a summary. Three things have to be true, and none had been measured. ──
+  const startFresh = byName['we-can-start-a-fresh-session-on-purpose'];
+  const sessions = (mode) => {
+    const held = {};
+    let n = 0;
+    return async (messages, o) => {
+      const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+      const plant = body.match(/marker: (SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6})/);
+      // 'sticky' hands back the SAME session for an unrelated opening — the failure that makes a compaction
+      // boundary imaginary.
+      const id = mode === 'sticky' ? 'S1' : (plant && !o?.extra ? 'S' + (++n) : o?.extra?.gemini_enterprise?.session || 'S' + n);
+      if (plant) { held[id] = plant[1]; return { text: 'noted', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: id, assist_token: 'A' } } }; }
+      const say = mode === 'leaky' ? held.S1 : held[id];
+      return { text: say || 'NONE', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: id } } };
+    };
+  };
+  const clean = await startFresh.plan({ call: sessions('ok') });
+  ok(clean.holds === true && /new-session=Y carries=new/.test(clean.measured), `a genuinely new session holds the claim (${clean.measured})`);
+  const sticky = await startFresh.plan({ call: sessions('sticky') });
+  ok(sticky.holds === false && /a compaction boundary would be imaginary/.test(sticky.note),
+    'being handed the SAME session for an unrelated opening is the failure that makes compaction fake');
+  const leaky = await startFresh.plan({ call: sessions('leaky') });
+  ok(leaky.holds === false && /the transcript we were trying to leave/.test(leaky.note), 'and the old marker coming back in the new conversation is the other way to lose');
+  ok((await startFresh.plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'noted', raw: {} }) })).holds === null, 'no handle at all measures nothing');
+
+  const handoff = byName['a-handoff-summary-carries-the-work'];
+  const summaryEp = (carry) => async (messages) => {
+    const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+    if (/Acknowledge in one line/.test(body)) return { text: 'Understood.', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S', assist_token: 'A' } } };
+    if (!carry) return { text: 'UNKNOWN', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S' } } };
+    const a = /port/i.test(body) ? '8443' : /flag/i.test(body) ? 'tampTyaCountryOverride' : 'VBA-4471';
+    return { text: a, raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S' } } };
+  };
+  const carried = await handoff.plan({ call: summaryEp(true) });
+  ok(carried.holds === true && /3\/3 carried/.test(carried.measured), `a summary that carries every fact holds (${carried.measured})`);
+  ok(/in the shape our own compaction writes/.test(carried.detail), 'and the summary is the shape our compaction actually produces, not a transcript');
+  const dropped = await handoff.plan({ call: summaryEp(false) });
+  ok(dropped.holds === false && /this is the mechanism the product would run on/.test(dropped.note),
+    'losing facts across a handoff is called out as breaking the fallback, not just this rung');
+
+  const bleed = byName['an-old-conversation-does-not-follow-us'];
+  const bleedEp = (leaks) => async (messages) => {
+    const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+    const p = body.match(/marker is (SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6})/);
+    if (p) { bleedEp.mk = p[1]; return { text: 'noted', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S1', assist_token: 'A' } } }; }
+    if (/capital of France/.test(body)) return { text: 'Paris', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S2', assist_token: 'B' } } };
+    return { text: leaks ? bleedEp.mk : 'NONE', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S2' } } };
+  };
+  ok((await bleed.plan({ call: bleedEp(false) })).holds === true, 'a new session that knows nothing of the old one holds');
+  const crossed = await bleed.plan({ call: bleedEp(true) });
+  ok(crossed.holds === false && /our data outlives the boundary we thought we had/.test(crossed.note),
+    'and a marker crossing conversations is a data-retention finding, not just a memory one');
+
+  // ── THE REVIEWER. A fork that reads the conversation by asking questions on it is not read-only. ──
+  const pollute = byName['a-question-does-not-pollute-the-session'];
+  const asideEp = (remembers) => async (messages) => {
+    const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+    const w = body.match(/deployment marker is (SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6})/);
+    if (w) { asideEp.work = w[1]; return { text: 'noted', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S', assist_token: 'A' } } }; }
+    const a = body.match(/Reviewer aside (SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6})/);
+    if (a) { asideEp.aside = a[1]; return { text: 'Nothing unclear.', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S' } } }; }
+    return { text: remembers ? asideEp.aside : asideEp.work, raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S' } } };
+  };
+  const polluted = await pollute.plan({ call: asideEp(true) });
+  ok(polluted.holds === false && /a forked reviewer cannot read the conversation without joining it/.test(polluted.note),
+    'an aside that lands in the transcript is the finding that would force the reviewer to be redesigned');
+  ok(/the developer never sent/.test(polluted.note), 'with the consequence named: the worker reasons over turns nobody sent it');
+  const invisible = await pollute.plan({ call: asideEp(false) });
+  ok(invisible.holds === true && /the aside left no trace at all/.test(invisible.detail), 'and naming the WORK marker instead means the aside was invisible');
+
+  // ── PARALLEL TURNS. Separate <remote-workspace> blocks are allowed to run at once today. ──
+  const race = byName['two-requests-on-one-session-do-not-race'];
+  const raceEp = (mode) => async (messages, o) => {
+    const body = String(messages[messages.length - 1].content || '');
+    const p = body.match(/marker: (SPEC-[A-Z0-9]{6})/);
+    if (p) { raceEp.mk = p[1]; return { text: 'noted', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S', assist_token: 'A' } } }; }
+    if (/ALPHA/.test(body)) return { text: 'ALPHA', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S' } } };
+    if (/BETA/.test(body)) { if (mode === 'reject') throw new Error('HTTP 409 session busy'); return { text: 'BETA', raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S' } } }; }
+    return { text: mode === 'corrupt' ? 'NONE' : raceEp.mk, raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 'S' } } };
+  };
+  ok((await race.plan({ call: raceEp('ok') })).holds === true, 'two concurrent requests both answering, session intact, holds the claim');
+  const corrupted = await race.plan({ call: raceEp('corrupt') });
+  ok(corrupted.holds === false && /parallel work on one conversation corrupts it/.test(corrupted.note),
+    'losing the marker after a race is the serious one — and separate workspace blocks run in parallel TODAY');
+  const rejected = await race.plan({ call: raceEp('reject') });
+  ok(rejected.holds === false && /would need serialising before any of this ships/.test(rejected.note), 'and one of them erroring means parallel turns need serialising');
+  ok(/HTTP 409/.test(rejected.detail), 'with the error kept, because WHY it refused decides whether serialising is enough');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[13c] what a product built on this would need to know\n');
+{
+  // ── WHICH language does its sandbox run? We have never established one. ──
+  // 3a proved something executes — it asked for a SHA-256 digest and got the right one — but never said in what,
+  // and the environment probe's "python: yes" is the model self-reporting against a checklist.
+  const lang = byName['its-cloud-environment-runs-both-languages'];
+  const langEp = (works) => async (messages) => {
+    const body = String(messages[0].content);
+    const n = (body.match(/'([0-9a-f-]{12})'/) || [])[1];
+    const sum = [...(n || '')].reduce((a, c) => a + c.charCodeAt(0), 0);
+    const asked = /JavaScript/.test(body) ? 'js' : 'py';
+    if (!works.includes(asked)) return { text: 'I cannot run that here.', raw: {} };
+    // Each language answers in ITS OWN sort order — that is what makes the arm attributable.
+    return { text: `${asked === 'js' ? '1,10,9' : '1,9,10'}
+${sum}`, raw: {} };
+  };
+  const both = await lang.plan({ call: langEp(['js', 'py']) });
+  ok(both.holds === true && /js=YES py=YES/.test(both.measured), `both languages running holds the claim (${both.measured})`);
+  const pyOnly = await lang.plan({ call: langEp(['py']) });
+  ok(pyOnly.holds === false && /only PYTHON runs there/.test(pyOnly.note), 'Python-only is a break, and names the language the verb has to say');
+  ok(/a protocol offering the other would fail on contact/.test(pyOnly.note), 'with the consequence: a verb naming the wrong language does not degrade, it dies');
+  ok(/js=no py=YES/.test(pyOnly.measured), `and each arm is scored separately (${pyOnly.measured})`);
+  const jsOnly = await lang.plan({ call: langEp(['js']) });
+  ok(jsOnly.holds === false && /only JAVASCRIPT runs there/.test(jsOnly.note), 'and JavaScript-only is the mirror answer');
+  const neither = await lang.plan({ call: langEp([]) });
+  ok(neither.holds === false && /nothing behind it, whatever we call it/.test(neither.note), 'neither running means the sandbox verb is dead whatever we name it');
+  // The lie this is built to catch: answering a JavaScript question in Python's sort order.
+  const swapped = await lang.plan({ call: async (messages) => {
+    const body = String(messages[0].content);
+    const n = (body.match(/'([0-9a-f-]{12})'/) || [])[1];
+    const sum = [...(n || '')].reduce((a, c) => a + c.charCodeAt(0), 0);
+    return { text: `1,9,10
+${sum}`, raw: {} };          // numeric order for BOTH arms
+  } });
+  ok(/js=OTHER-LANG/.test(swapped.measured), `a JavaScript request answered in Python's sort order is caught (${swapped.measured})`);
+  ok(swapped.holds === false, 'and does not count as JavaScript working');
+
+  // ── how much work fits in ONE turn? Verified by internal consistency, since we cannot know its numbers. ──
+  const chain = byName['its-cloud-environment-chains-work-in-one-turn'];
+  const nums = Array.from({ length: 40 }, (_, i) => 100000 + i * 137);
+  const good = chain.check(`${nums.join(',')}\n${nums.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0)}`, {}, chain);
+  ok(good.holds === true && /40 numbers, total CORRECT/.test(good.measured), `a total that matches its own numbers proves a write, a read and a computation in one reply (${good.measured})`);
+  const lied = chain.check(`${nums.join(',')}\n5000000`, {}, chain);
+  ok(lied.holds === false && /it wrote an answer rather than running anything/.test(lied.note), 'a total that does not match means it guessed — and then every step of real work costs a round trip');
+  ok(/and the numbers it printed add to \d+/.test(lied.detail), 'with both figures printed so the arithmetic can be checked by eye');
+  ok(chain.check('I would rather not.', {}, chain).holds === null, 'and no numbers at all measures nothing rather than failing');
+
+  // ── batching: round trips are the cost, and depth is counted in TURNS ──
+  const batch = byName['it-batches-operations-in-one-reply'];
+  ok(batch.check('<remote-workspace>' + '<read path="a"/>'.repeat(4) + '</remote-workspace>').holds === true, 'four reads in one reply holds');
+  const one2 = batch.check('<remote-workspace><read path="a"/></remote-workspace>');
+  ok(one2.holds === false && /a turn off whatever depth the session holds/.test(one2.note),
+    'one at a time is a break, and the note names the real cost — turns are the thing that runs out');
+  ok(/it emitted no read at all/.test(batch.check('I will look at those.').note), 'and no read at all is called out separately from too few');
+
+  // ── can a whole file come back? a commit carries the ENTIRE body ──
+  const whole = byName['a-whole-file-body-comes-back'];
+  const echo = (cutAt) => async (messages) => {
+    const body = String(messages[0].content).split('\n\n').slice(1).join('\n\n');
+    return { text: cutAt && body.length > cutAt ? body.slice(0, cutAt) : body, raw: {} };
+  };
+  const full = await whole.plan({ call: echo(0) });
+  ok(full.holds === true && /40LY 120LY 300LY/.test(full.measured), `every size coming back complete holds (${full.measured})`);
+  // 6,000 sits between the 120-line body (~5.4k) and the 300-line one (~13.5k), so exactly one size is cut.
+  const cut = await whole.plan({ call: echo(6000) });
