+    for (const nm of [R, S, T, U, V, W, X, Y, Z, AA, AD]) await r9(nm).plan({ call: capCall, model: SEL });
+    const NEED2 = ['table', 'window', 'pencil', 'candle', 'ladder', 'bottle', 'carpet', 'engine', 'pantry', 'kitchen', 'hallway', 'garage', 'valley', 'attic', 'tunnel', 'cellar', 'orchard', 'station', 'library', 'factory', 'door', 'check', 'task', 'answers', 'nothing', 'none', 'unknown', 'green', 'yellow', 'color', 'storeroom', 'spare', 'key', 'changed', 'decided', 'open', 'retry', 'module', 'notes'];
+    const hid2 = new Set();
+    for (const t of out2) { const body = t.includes('=== THE CONVERSATION SO FAR') ? t.slice(t.indexOf('=== THE CONVERSATION SO FAR')) : t; for (const r of masker2.analyze(body.replace(/<js>[\s\S]*?<\/js>/g, '<js></js>'), {}).runs) if (!r.show && NEED2.includes(r.t)) hid2.add(r.t); }
+    ok(out2.length > 60 && hid2.size === 0, `none of the words the new rungs turn on is masked by the shipped masker${hid2.size ? ' - MASKED: ' + [...hid2].join(', ') : ''} (${out2.length} messages)`);
+  }
+  // 9ae — the shape of a session as it grows (the developer, 2026-10-13)
+  {
+    // A session that keeps every part whole - or, with `keep`, only the last `keep` parts: older ones are cut to a
+    // stub, it SAYS so in its shape line, and their entries can no longer be looked up. `wallAt`: calls fail from there.
+    const gwG = ({ keep = 0, wallAt = 0, lies = false } = {}) => {
+      const parts = []; let n = 0;
+      const call = async (msgs) => {
+        const q = String(msgs[msgs.length - 1].content);
+        if (wallAt && parts.length >= wallAt) return { err: 'HTTP 500: context too large' };
+        const whole = (i) => !keep || i >= parts.length - keep;
+        const shape = `shape count ${parts.length}${parts.map((p, i) => `, ${i + 1} ${whole(i) || lies ? 'full' : 'cut'} ${whole(i) || lies ? 5000 : 40}`).join('')}`;
+        const view = parts.filter((_, i) => whole(i)).join('\n');
+        const asks = [...q.matchAll(/[Ff]or entry (\d+) of part (\d+): ([^.]+)\./g)].map((m) => { const has = new RegExp(`^In part ${m[2]}, entry ${m[1]}, .*? holds (\\d+) units`, 'm').exec(view); const opt = has && [...m[3].matchAll(/write ([a-z]+) for (\d+)/g)].find((o) => o[2] === has[1]); return opt ? opt[1] : 'nothing'; });
+        parts.push(q);
+        return { text: `${shape}\nanswers: ${asks.length ? asks.join(' ') : 'none'}`, ms: 2000 + parts.length * 300, raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 's1', assist_token: 't' + (++n) } }, usage: {} };
+      };
+      return { call, parts };
+    };
+    const G = 'a-growing-session-keeps-every-message-whole';
+    const runG = async (o) => { const g = gwG(o); const v = await r9(G).plan({ call: g.call, model: SEL }); return { ...v, g }; };
+    const whole = await runG();
+    ok(whole.holds === true && /steps=30\/30 ~\d+k tokens \| first shortened or gone at step=- \| first lookup missed at step=-/.test(whole.measured), `9ae: thirty parts, every one reported whole and every lookup right (${whole.measured})`);
+    ok(/ 2:1\/F1S0X0\/5k\/YYY\/\d+s /.test(whole.detail) && / 30:29\/F29S0X0\/145k\/YYY\/\d+s/.test(whole.detail) && /"one more message, all full" at every step/.test(whole.detail), 'the transcript is one short entry per step: count, how many are full, the words it says it holds, the three lookups, the seconds');
+    ok(whole.g.parts.length === 30 && whole.g.parts[0].length > 30000 && /for entry \d+ of part 1:/.test(whole.g.parts[9]) && /For entry \d+ of part 5:/.test(whole.g.parts[9]) && /For entry \d+ of part 9:/.test(whole.g.parts[9]), 'each message is a part of ~9k tokens, and asks for an entry in the oldest part, a middle part and the part before');
+    const compacts = await runG({ keep: 6 });

+    ok(quiet.holds === false && /first shortened or gone at step=- \| first lookup missed at step=8/.test(quiet.measured) && /it never reported a message shortened/.test(quiet.note), 'a session that forgets while still SAYING everything is full is caught by the lookups');
+    const walled = await runG({ wallAt: 12 });

+  }
+  // 9af-9ah — from the gateway's own documentation (docs/api_docs), after the model list changed (2026-10-13)

+  // 9ae must not call replies nobody could read "held" (the survey of 2026-10-13: four models, every step back in a

+  {
+    const mute = await r9('a-growing-session-keeps-every-message-whole').plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'I cannot help with that request.', ms: 900, raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: 's1' } }, usage: {}, http: { headers: {} } }), model: SEL });

+
+  // 9ak — how long a turn takes as the conversation grows, on either lane (2026-10-13)
+  {
+    const units = (i) => (i * 7919) % 10007;
+    // `slope` ms per 1000 characters sent; `session`: replies carry a handle and a call on it is quick
+    const gwK = ({ slope = 0, session = false, base = 1000 } = {}) => async (msgs, opt = {}) => {
+      const whole = msgs.map((m) => m.content).join('\n');
+      const onHandle = !!(opt.extra && opt.extra.gemini_enterprise);
+      const q = /How many units does record (\d+) hold/.exec(String(msgs[msgs.length - 1].content));

+    };
+    const K = 'a-turn-takes-no-longer-as-the-conversation-grows';

+  // 9am — what the session transport assumes about the real gateway (2026-10-13)
+  {
+    const gwM = ({ frames = 'all', unknown = 'new', keepsCut = true } = {}) => {
+      const SID = 'abc123def456'; const held = new Map([[SID, []]]);
+      const sidOf = (opt) => opt?.extra?.gemini_enterprise?.session || null;
+      const answer = (text, turns) => { const all = turns.join('\n'); const mk = (/deployment marker is (\S+?)\./.exec(all) || [])[1]; const w = (/spare word is (\w+)/.exec(all) || [])[1];

+        if (/What is the deployment marker/.test(text)) return mk || 'NONE'; return 'NOTED'; };

+        const text = msgs[msgs.length - 1].content; let sid = sidOf(opt);
+        if (sid && !held.has(sid)) { if (unknown === 'refuse') throw new Error('HTTP 400 session not found'); if (unknown === 'new') sid = 'fresh' + held.size; held.set(sid, []); }
+        if (!sid) sid = SID;
+        return { text, sid, turns: held.get(sid) };
+      };
+      const call = async (msgs, opt = {}) => { const r = route(msgs, opt); r.turns.push(r.text); return { text: answer(r.text, r.turns), ms: 500, raw: { gemini_enterprise: { session: r.sid, assist_token: 't' } }, usage: {}, http: { headers: {} } }; };
+      const callStream = async (msgs, opt = {}) => {
+        const r = route(msgs, opt);
+        if (opt.signal) { await new Promise((res) => opt.signal.addEventListener('abort', res, { once: true })); if (keepsCut) r.turns.push(r.text); throw new Error('aborted'); }
+        r.turns.push(r.text);
+        return { text: answer(r.text, r.turns), ms: 700, streamed: true, chunks: 3, envFrames: frames === 'all' ? 3 : 0, envFirst: frames === 'all', envSession: frames === 'all' ? r.sid : null, raw: frames === 'all' ? { gemini_enterprise: { session: r.sid, assist_token: 't2' } } : {} };
+      };
+      return { call, callStream };
+    };
+    const MI = 'the-session-transport-s-assumptions-hold';

+  // 9aj — can a cache discount be ASKED for: request-side parameters, one arm each (2026-10-13)
+  {
+    // A gateway that reports a seen front as cached and bills in full - unless `honours` names a request field, in
+    // which case a request carrying it gets the seen front at a tenth. `refuses` names a field it answers 400 to.
+    const gwJ = ({ honours = null, refuses = null, rises = false } = {}) => {
+      let left = 5e7, n = 0; const seen = new Set(); const sent = [];
+      const textOf = (m) => (Array.isArray(m.content) ? m.content.map((p) => p.text).join('') : m.content);
+      const call = async (msgs, opt = {}) => {
+        const extra = opt.extra || {};
+        sent.push({ extra, parts: Array.isArray(msgs[0].content), n: msgs.length });
+        if (refuses && refuses in extra) throw new Error('HTTP 400 unknown field ' + refuses);
+        const whole = msgs.map((m) => m.role + ':' + textOf(m)).join('\n');
+        const p = Math.round(whole.length / 4);
+        const front = msgs[0].role + ':' + textOf(msgs[0]);
+        const hit = seen.has(front) ? Math.round(front.length / 4) : 0; seen.add(front);
+        const discount = honours && honours in extra;
+        left -= discount ? (p - hit) + Math.round(hit / 10) + 2 : p + 2;
+        if (rises && ++n === 5) left += 9000;
+        return { text: 'READY', ms: 700, raw: {}, usage: { prompt_tokens: p, completion_tokens: 2, total_tokens: p + 2, prompt_tokens_details: { cached_tokens: hit } }, http: { headers: { 'x-ratelimit-remaining-credits-day': String(left), 'x-cost-tier': 'standard' } } };
+      };
+      return { call, sent };
+    };
+    const JI = 'no-request-parameter-gets-a-resent-conversation-billed-for-less';
+    const runJ = async (o) => { const g = gwJ(o); const v = await r9(JI).plan({ call: g.call, model: SEL }); return { ...v, g }; };
+    const none = await runJ();
+    ok(none.holds === true && /plain=\d{4}c 1\.00>1\.00/.test(none.measured) && /key=\d{4}c 1\.00>1\.00/.test(none.measured) && /nothing asked for on the request made a resend cheaper/.test(none.note), `9aj: cached tokens reported on every arm and every arm billed in full holds it (${none.measured.slice(0, 120)})`);
+    ok(none.g.sent.some((s) => s.extra.prompt_cache_key) && none.g.sent.some((s) => s.extra.prompt_cache_retention === '24h') && none.g.sent.some((s) => s.parts) && none.g.sent.some((s) => s.extra.store === true) && none.g.sent.some((s) => s.extra.service_tier === 'flex') && none.g.sent.some((s) => s.extra.user), 'the arms really send a cache key, a retention, a marked content part, store, a user and a service tier');
+    ok(/every usage key seen: [^|]*prompt_tokens_details\.cached_tokens/.test(none.detail) && /headers about cost: x-cost-tier/.test(none.detail), 'every usage key and every header about cost is printed, whatever it is called');
+    const keyed = await runJ({ honours: 'prompt_cache_key' });
+    ok(keyed.holds === false && /BILLED FOR LESS with key/.test(keyed.note) && /keep/.test(keyed.note) && !/with[^-]*\bstore\b/.test(keyed.note), `a field that gets the resend billed for less breaks it and is named (${keyed.note.slice(0, 110)})`);
+    const refused = await runJ({ refuses: 'service_tier' });
+    ok(refused.holds === true && /flex=Ec E>E/.test(refused.measured) && /refused or failed: flex/.test(refused.note) && /flex: HTTP 400/.test(refused.detail), 'a field the gateway refuses is reported as refused, and the other arms still count');

+  }

+  // 9ai — a conversation resent as a list of turns: cached or billed in full (2026-10-13, the new lanes)
+  {
+    // A stateless gateway that reads every message. `caches`: a request whose front it has seen before reports those
+    // tokens as cached and bills them at a tenth.
+    const gwC = ({ caches = false } = {}) => {
+      let left = 5e7; const seenFronts = new Set(); const sent = [];
+      const call = async (msgs) => {
+        const whole = msgs.map((m) => m.role + ':' + m.content).join('\n');
+        const p = Math.round(whole.length / 4);
+        // the longest front of this request that an earlier request also began with
+        let hit = 0;
+        if (caches) for (let n = msgs.length - 1; n >= 1; n--) { const front = msgs.slice(0, n).map((m) => m.role + ':' + m.content).join('\n'); if (seenFronts.has(front)) { hit = Math.round(front.length / 4); break; } }
+        for (let n = 1; n <= msgs.length; n++) seenFronts.add(msgs.slice(0, n).map((m) => m.role + ':' + m.content).join('\n'));
+        sent.push({ n: msgs.length, system: msgs[0].role === 'system' });
+        left -= (p - hit) + Math.round(hit / 10) + 2;
+        const q = String(msgs[msgs.length - 1].content);
+        const rec = /How many units does record (\d+) hold/.exec(q), has = rec && new RegExp(`^Record ${rec[1]}: .*? holds (\\d+) units`, 'm').exec(whole);
+        return { text: has ? has[1] : 'READY', ms: 800, raw: {}, usage: { prompt_tokens: p, completion_tokens: 2, total_tokens: p + 2, ...(caches ? { prompt_tokens_details: { cached_tokens: hit } } : {}) }, http: { headers: { 'x-ratelimit-remaining-credits-day': String(left) } } };
+      };
+      return { call, sent };
+    };
+    const CI = 'a-conversation-resent-with-one-more-turn-is-billed-in-full';
+    const runC = async (o) => { const g = gwC(o); const v = await r9(CI).plan({ call: g.call, model: SEL }); return { ...v, g }; };
+    const plain = await runC();
+    ok(plain.holds === true && /cached tokens per turn=-\/-\/-\/-\/- again=- control=-/.test(plain.measured) && /answers right 5\/5/.test(plain.measured) && /paid for again each time/.test(plain.note), `9ai: no cache field and the same price a token holds it (${plain.measured.slice(0, 150)})`);
+    ok(plain.g.sent.filter((s) => s.system).map((s) => s.n).join() === '2,4,6,8,10,10,10', 'five requests, each the one before plus an exchange under one system message; a fresh conversation of the same size; the last one again');
+    const cachedRun = await runC({ caches: true });
+    ok(cachedRun.holds === false && /ARE cached and billed for less/.test(cachedRun.note) && /cached tokens per turn=0\/\d{4}\/\d{4}\/\d{4}\/\d{4} again=\d{4} control=0/.test(cachedRun.measured), `a front it has seen before, reported as cached and billed for less, breaks it (${cachedRun.measured.slice(0, 170)})`);
+    ok(r9(CI).solo === true, 'it runs alone: its cost is the budget header\'s drop');
+    // 4a: the ONE construct is a <js> snippet - a correct block is not "prose"
+    const a4 = SPEC.find((a) => a.name === 'emits-our-tool-block');
+    ok(a4.check('Reading it. <remote-workspace><js>return await read("src/config.ts");</js></remote-workspace> /--- you wetl ---').holds === true && a4.check('<remote-workspace>\n<read path="src/config.ts"/>\n</remote-workspace>').holds === true && a4.check('I would read src/config.ts.').holds === false, '4a: a <js> block that reads the file counts, as the old <read/> verb does; prose does not');
+  }
+  // LAYER 4 UNDERSTANDS THE ONE CONSTRUCT (2026-10-13). On the first run against the new models five rungs broke
+  // on replies that were CORRECT: the model answered with a <js> snippet - read(), write(), edit(), parallel() - and
+  // the checks only knew the old tag verbs. These are the replies from that run.

+    ok(w.holds === true && /through write\(\) in a snippet/.test(w.measured || ''), '4c: a write() whose string spells < & > as \\x3c \\x26 \\x3e is byte-exact in the file, and counts');
+    ok(by4('write-body-stays-raw').check('<remote-workspace><js>return await write("src/note.md", "if (a &lt; b &amp;&amp; c &gt; d) { x++; }");</js></remote-workspace>').holds === false, 'an HTML-escaped body inside write() is still caught');
+    const g = by4('it-batches-operations-in-one-reply').check('Reading the four requested files. <remote-workspace><js>return await parallel({ config: () => read("src/config.ts"), httpClient: () => read("src/http-client.ts"), routes: () => read("src/routes.ts"), test: () => read("test/config.test.ts") });</js></remote-workspace>');
+    ok(g.holds === true && /4 read\(s\) in one reply/.test(g.measured), `4g: four read() calls in one snippet are four reads in one reply (${g.measured})`);
+    const j = by4('it-will-not-write-a-file-it-was-never-given');
+    const jGood = j.check('I will update it and look at routes. <remote-workspace><js> const res = await parallel({ cfg: () => edit("src/config.ts", "export const TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000;", "export const TIMEOUT_MS = 60_000;"), routes: () => read("src/routes.ts") }); return res;</js></remote-workspace>');
+    ok(jGood.holds === true && /invented-body=N asked-for-it=Y did-the-known-file=Y/.test(jGood.measured), `4j: edit() on the file it has and read() of the one it lacks is the good outcome (${jGood.measured})`);
+    const jBad = j.check('<remote-workspace><js>await write("src/routes.ts", "export function health() { return { ok: true, build: process.env.BUILD_TAG }; }");</js></remote-workspace>');
+    ok(jBad.holds === false && /invented-body=Y/.test(jBad.measured), 'a write() of the file it was never shown is still the catastrophic case');
+    const l = by4('it-does-our-file-work-through-our-verbs').check('I will update `TIMEOUT_MS` to 60_000 in `src/config.ts` and verify the build. <remote-workspace><js> const editResult = await edit("src/config.ts", "export const TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000;", "export const TIMEOUT_MS = 60_000;"); return editResult;</js></remote-workspace>');
+    ok(l.holds === true && /wrote-through-us=Y/.test(l.measured), `4l: an edit() in a snippet is writing through us (${l.measured})`);
+    const big = await by4('our-tool-protocol-survives-a-huge-message').plan({ call: async () => ({ text: 'Reading `src/config.ts` and writing the note. <remote-workspace><js> const config = await read("src/config.ts"); await write("src/note.md", "if (a < b && c > d) { x++; }\\n"); return config;</js></remote-workspace>', ms: 900 }), model: 'm' });
+    ok(big.holds === true && /=BRWC/.test(big.measured) && !/B--C/.test(big.measured), `4k: read() and write() in a snippet are the read and the write it asked for, at every size (${big.measured})`);
+  }
+
+  // End to end through the real runner and probeCall: an adapter where only -withtools honours tools, streamed in
+  // real delta frames. This is what the release-plan step runs.
+  {
+    const body = (msg, extra = {}) => ({ model: 'm', choices: [{ index: 0, message: { role: 'assistant', ...msg }, finish_reason: msg.tool_calls ? 'tool_calls' : 'stop' }], usage: { prompt_tokens: 5, completion_tokens: 3 }, ...extra });
+    const decide = (req) => {
+      const h = /-withtools$/.test(req.model);
+      const last = req.messages[req.messages.length - 1]; const lt = String(last.content || '');
+      if (req.response_format) return { content: h ? '{"colour":"red","count":3}' : 'Red, three.' };
+      if (/List every tool/.test(lt)) return { content: h && req.tools ? '["cb_read_workspace_file"]' : '["default_api"]' };
+      if (last.role === 'tool') return { content: h ? 'Token ' + (/SPEC-\w+/.exec(lt) || [''])[0] : 'No idea.' };
+      const plain = /contains: token: (SPEC-\w+)/.exec(lt); if (plain) return { content: plain[1] };
+      if (req.tools && h && req.tool_choice !== 'none') return { content: null, tool_calls: req.tools.slice(0, /Two things/.test(lt) ? 2 : 1).map((t, i) => ({ id: 'c' + i, type: 'function', function: { name: t.function.name, arguments: '{"path":"a"}' } })) };
+      return { content: 'READY' };
+    };
+    const adapter = {
+      baseURL: 'https://gw.example.test/v1',
+      listModels: async () => ({ data: [{ id: SEL }, { id: WT }] }),
+      complete: async (req) => { const j = body(decide(req)); Object.defineProperty(j, '__http', { value: { status: 200, headers: { 'x-ratelimit-limit-requests-minute': '60' } }, enumerable: false }); return j; },
+      async *completeStream(req) {
+        const d = decide(req);
+        if (d.tool_calls) {
+          for (const [i, c] of d.tool_calls.entries()) {
+            yield { choices: [{ index: 0, delta: { tool_calls: [{ index: i, id: c.id, type: 'function', function: { name: c.function.name, arguments: '' } }] } }] };
+            yield { choices: [{ index: 0, delta: { tool_calls: [{ index: i, function: { arguments: c.function.arguments } }] } }] };
+          }
+          yield { choices: [{ index: 0, delta: {}, finish_reason: 'tool_calls' }] };
+        } else yield { choices: [{ index: 0, delta: { content: d.content || '' }, finish_reason: 'stop' }] };
+      },
+    };
+    const res = await runProdSpec({ adapter, model: SEL, layers: [9] });
+    const by = Object.fromEntries(res.results.map((r) => [r.id, r]));
+    ok(res.results.length === 43 && res.results.every((r) => r.layer === 9), `layers:[9] runs exactly the forty-three layer-9 rungs (${res.results.map((r) => r.id).join(',')})`);
+    ok(by['9a'].holds === false && by['9b'].holds === false && by['9c'].holds === false, `9a-9c find the -withtools lane (${by['9c'].measured})`);
+    ok(/sel:NNN wt:CCC/.test(by['9c'].measured), 'and 9c says it is only that lane');
+    ok(by['9e'].holds === false && /wt:F2/.test(by['9e'].measured), `real delta frames are reassembled into a call (${by['9e'].measured})`);
+    ok(by['9d'].holds === false && by['9f'].holds === false && by['9g'].holds === false, '9d, 9f and 9g each find what that lane does');
+    ok(by['9h'].holds === true && /requests-minute=60/.test(by['9h'].detail), 'and 9h reads the headers off the real response envelope');
+    const rep = formatProdSpecReport(res);
+    ok(/^SPEC gemini-x-pro via gw\.example\.test/.test(rep) && /M 9a=/.test(rep) && /D 9c codes/.test(rep), 'the report carries the measured line and the per-lane detail');
+    ok(res.results.flatMap((r) => r.samples || []).some((s) => s.mdl === 'm'), 'every call leaves its sample, as the other layers do');
+  }
+}
+
+process.stdout.write(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed\n`);
+process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);

=== add test/test-progress-masking.mjs
new 82676a318b2c7d7be4c907f4
+/**
+ * LIVE OUTPUT IS MASKED, INCLUDING ACROSS A CHUNK BOUNDARY.
+ *
+ * The RESULT of a tool was masked before entering the transcript, but PROGRESS — the stdout the developer is
+ * watching while a build runs, and a snippet's own console.log — was emitted raw. On a machine where the screen
+ * gets photographed that is the half that matters (found 2026-09-19).
+ *
+ * Masking each chunk on its own does not fix it: a value SPLIT across two chunks is in neither, so neither
+ * matches. The matcher folds whitespace, so a split across a newline is still one value and flushing whole lines
+ * is not a fix either. The gate instead retains a tail, masks (tail + chunk) together, and releases the tail only
+ * when nothing is writing — no live child process, or the step is over.
+ *
+ *   [1] a value arriving in ONE chunk is masked
+ *   [2] a value SPLIT across two chunks is masked — the half-value never reaches the screen
+ *   [3] a value split across a NEWLINE is masked (the fold makes it one value)
+ *   [4] every byte still arrives, in order — masking must not drop or reorder output
+ *   [5] with no filter active nothing is buffered: output passes straight through
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-progress-masking.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, mkdir, writeFile, rm, copyFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { createPluginManager } from '../src/plugins/index.mjs';
+import { createFilterPipeline } from '../src/plugins/filters.mjs';
+import { runMinimalWorker } from '../src/agent/minimal-worker.mjs';
+import { createTokenStore } from '../src/tokens.mjs';
+// the test data is in the store OUTSIDE the container (vault.mjs) - for a test, a folder under its own CB_HOME
+const tdFam = () => path.join(process.env.CB_HOME, '.code_boss', 'secure', 'plugins', 'pii-testdata', 'families');
+// ITS OWN HOME (the store of test data lives under it; vault.mjs never uses the developer's real one)
+process.env.CB_HOME = (await import('node:fs')).mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-progmask-home-'));
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+const SSN = '482-11-9037';
+const NAME = 'Okonkwo';
+const PLUGIN_SRC = path.join(process.cwd(), 'examples', 'plugins', 'pii-testdata__1.mjs');
+
+// Drive the worker with a tool whose impl streams whatever chunks the case asks for, then returns.
+async function streamThrough(chunks, { withFilter = true } = {}) {
+  const proj = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-prog-'));
+  const plugDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-prog-plug-'));
+  let filter = null;
+  if (withFilter) {
+    await copyFile(PLUGIN_SRC, path.join(plugDir, 'pii-testdata__1.mjs'));
+    await mkdir(tdFam(), { recursive: true });
+    await writeFile(path.join(tdFam(), 'smiths.json'), JSON.stringify({
+      label: 'smiths', family: { deers_family_id: '20240001' },
+      members: { sponsor: { first_name: 'Gwendolyn', last_name: NAME, ssn: SSN } },
+    }));
+    // the tokens are code_boss's (tokens.mjs): the plugin issues them through the manager, the pipeline masks by them
+    const store = createTokenStore({ secret: 'k' });
+    const mgr = createPluginManager({ onLog: () => {}, tokens: store });
+    mgr.setPluginsDir(plugDir);
+    await mgr.reconcile();
+    await mgr.detectAll();
+    filter = createFilterPipeline(() => mgr.enabledFilters(), { tokens: store });
+  }
+  const seen = [];
+  const extraTools = {
+    streamer: {
+      schema: { description: 'streams', parameters: { type: 'object', properties: {} } },
+      impl: async (_args, ctx) => {
+        for (const c of chunks) { ctx.onProgress?.(c); await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 5)); }
+        return { content: 'streamed' };
+      },
+    },
+  };
+  let i = 0;
+  const adapter = { async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: i++ === 0 ? 'Go.\n<remote-workspace><js>return await streamer({});</js></remote-workspace>' : 'Done.' } }], usage: { total_tokens: 5 } }; } };
+  await runMinimalWorker({
+    adapter, model: 'test', signal: { aborted: false },
+    messages: [{ content: 'stream it' }], projectDir: proj,
+    requireBranch: () => 'ok', requireApproval: () => true,
+    onChatEvent: (e) => { if (e.type === 'tool-progress') seen.push(String(e.chunk || '')); return true; },
+    onPhase: () => {}, onLog: () => {},
+    extraTools, extraVerbs: { streamer: 'streamer' },
+    ...(filter ? { contentFilter: filter } : {}),
+  });
+  await rm(proj, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+  await rm(plugDir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+  return { seen, joined: seen.join('') };
+}
+
+console.log('\n[1] a value arriving in ONE chunk is masked');
+{
+  const { joined } = await streamThrough([`ssn is ${SSN} done\n`]);
+  ok(!joined.includes(SSN), `the raw value never reached the screen (${JSON.stringify(joined.slice(0, 70))})`);
+  ok(/#@smiths\.sponsor\.ssn~[^#\s]+#[0-9a-z]{7}/.test(joined), 'it was replaced with a token');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[2] a value SPLIT across two chunks is masked — this is what per-chunk masking gets wrong');
+{
+  const { joined } = await streamThrough(['ssn is 482-11-', '9037 done\n']);
+  ok(!joined.includes(SSN), `the reassembled stream does not contain the value (${JSON.stringify(joined.slice(0, 80))})`);
+  ok(!joined.includes('482-11-'), 'and not even the leading half of it — no partial reached the screen');
+  ok(/#@smiths\.sponsor\.(?:ssn|last_name)~[^#\s]+#[0-9a-z]{7}/.test(joined), 'a token is there in its place');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[3] a value split MID-LINE stays retained until its line completes, then masks whole');
+{
+  const { joined } = await streamThrough([`name is Okon`, `kwo\n`, 'more output\n']);
+  ok(!joined.includes(NAME), `the name split mid-word never appears (${JSON.stringify(joined.slice(0, 80))})`);
+  // A complete line is released at once — that is what keeps a slow build's output LIVE rather than invisible
+  // until the step ends. It is also the documented residual: a value that itself contains a line break, split at
+  // that break, would go out in two halves. Stdout does not wrap identifiers, so the line is the right cut for a
+  // stream (a wrapped document is a different problem, and the RESULT masker handles documents whole).
+  const { seen } = await streamThrough(['first line done\n', 'second still going']);
+  ok(seen.some((s) => /first line done/.test(s)), 'a complete line is released without waiting for the step to end');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[4] every byte still arrives, in order');
+{
+  const parts = ['alpha ', 'bravo ', 'charlie ', 'delta ', 'echo\n'];
+  const { joined } = await streamThrough(parts);
+  ok(joined.includes('alpha') && joined.includes('echo'), 'the first and last chunks both arrived');
+  const order = ['alpha', 'bravo', 'charlie', 'delta', 'echo'].map((w) => joined.indexOf(w));
+  ok(order.every((n, k) => n >= 0 && (k === 0 || n > order[k - 1])), `and in the order they were written (${order.join(',')})`);
+}
+
+console.log('\n[5b] a snippet edit\'s DIFF is masked before it becomes a step event — a diff is file content');
+{
+  const proj = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-prog-diff-'));
+  const plugDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-prog-diff-plug-'));
+  await copyFile(PLUGIN_SRC, path.join(plugDir, 'pii-testdata__1.mjs'));
+  await mkdir(tdFam(), { recursive: true });
+  await writeFile(path.join(tdFam(), 'smiths.json'), JSON.stringify({ label: 'smiths', family: { deers_family_id: '20240001' }, members: { sponsor: { first_name: 'Gwendolyn', last_name: NAME, ssn: SSN } } }));
+  const store = createTokenStore({ secret: 'k' });
+  const mgr = createPluginManager({ onLog: () => {}, tokens: store }); mgr.setPluginsDir(plugDir); await mgr.reconcile(); await mgr.detectAll();
+  const filter = createFilterPipeline(() => mgr.enabledFilters(), { tokens: store });
+  let i = 0;
+  const SNIP = 'Go.\n<remote-workspace><js>\nawait write("notes.txt", "sponsor ssn " + "482-11-" + "9037" + "\\n");\nreturn "wrote";\n</js></remote-workspace>';
+  const adapter = { async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: i++ === 0 ? SNIP : 'Done.' } }], usage: { total_tokens: 5 } }; } };
+  const steps = [];
+  await runMinimalWorker({ adapter, model: 'test', signal: { aborted: false }, messages: [{ content: 'x' }], projectDir: proj, requireBranch: () => 'ok', requireApproval: () => true, onChatEvent: (e) => { if (e.type === 'js-step') steps.push(e); return true; }, onPhase: () => {}, onLog: () => {}, contentFilter: filter });
+  const w = steps.find((e) => e.fn === 'write');
+  ok(w && typeof w.diff === 'string' && w.diff.length > 0, `the write step carries a diff (${w ? (w.diff || '').length : 'no step'} chars)`);
+  ok(w && !w.diff.includes(SSN), 'and the diff does NOT contain the value that was written');
+  ok(w && /#@smiths\.sponsor\.(?:ssn|last_name)~[^#\s]+#[0-9a-z]{7}/.test(w.diff), 'it carries the token instead');
+  await rm(proj, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+  await rm(plugDir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+}
+
+console.log('\n[5] with no filter active, output passes straight through unbuffered');
+{
+  const { seen, joined } = await streamThrough([`ssn is ${SSN}\n`], { withFilter: false });
+  ok(joined.includes(SSN), 'nothing is masked when no filter is installed — the gate does not invent a policy');
+  ok(seen.length === 1, `and the chunk is not held back (${seen.length} progress events for 1 chunk)`);
+}
+
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);

new 8aa615062753c8fb8e0dff7a
+/**
+ * Tests for the AGENTS.md (+ legacy CODE-BOSS.md) discovery + formatter.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-project-context.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, mkdir, writeFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { discoverCodeBossFiles, formatProjectContext, loadProjectContext } from '../src/agent/project-context.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+function ok(cond, label) {
+  if (cond) { pass++; console.log('  ✓', label); }
+  else { fail++; console.log('  ✗', label); }
+}
+function eq(actual, expected, label) {
+  if (actual === expected) { pass++; console.log('  ✓', label); }
+  else { fail++; console.log('  ✗', label, '\n     expected:', JSON.stringify(expected), '\n     actual:  ', JSON.stringify(actual)); }
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  const root = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-ctx-'));
+  // Hermetic: point the developer-global ~/AGENTS.md lookup (pcHomeDir → CB_HOME) at an EMPTY temp home so these
+  // cases never pick up a real ~/AGENTS.md on the test machine. Case [12] writes into it; restored at the end.
+  const _prevCbHome = process.env.CB_HOME;
+  const ghome = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-ctx-home-'));
+  process.env.CB_HOME = ghome;
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[1] empty project → no context');
+    {
+      const { context, files } = await loadProjectContext(root);
+      eq(context, '', 'empty string returned');
+      eq(files.length, 0, 'no files');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[2] single AGENTS.md at root → "in project root" heading + body');
+    {
+      const file = path.join(root, 'AGENTS.md');
+      await writeFile(file, 'Use forward slashes and TypeScript only.\n');
+      const { context, files } = await loadProjectContext(root);
+      ok(files.length === 1 && files[0].relDir === '.',
+         `one file at root (got: ${JSON.stringify(files.map((f) => f.relDir))})`);
+      eq(files[0].name, 'AGENTS.md', 'matched filename recorded');
+      ok(/in the project root/.test(context), `heading mentions project root`);
+      ok(/AGENTS\.md/.test(context), `heading names the file`);
+      ok(/Use forward slashes and TypeScript only/.test(context), `body included verbatim`);
+      // Cleanup for next test
+      await rm(file);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[3] multiple AGENTS.md files → list with per-directory headings');
+    {
+      await mkdir(path.join(root, 'app-foo'));
+      await mkdir(path.join(root, 'lib-bar'));
+      await mkdir(path.join(root, 'lib-bar', 'sub'));
+      await writeFile(path.join(root, 'app-foo', 'AGENTS.md'), 'Foo specifics.\n');
+      await writeFile(path.join(root, 'lib-bar', 'AGENTS.md'), 'Bar shared rules.\n');
+      await writeFile(path.join(root, 'lib-bar', 'sub', 'AGENTS.md'), 'Sub overrides.\n');
+      const { context, files } = await loadProjectContext(root);
+      eq(files.length, 3, '3 files found');
+      ok(/3 project-instructions file/.test(context), `header notes the count`);
+      ok(/app-foo\/AGENTS\.md/.test(context), `app-foo section heading`);
+      ok(/lib-bar\/AGENTS\.md/.test(context), `lib-bar section heading`);
+      ok(/lib-bar\/sub\/AGENTS\.md/.test(context), `lib-bar/sub section heading`);
+      ok(/Foo specifics/.test(context) && /Bar shared rules/.test(context) && /Sub overrides/.test(context),
+         `every body present`);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[4] one file in a subdir (none at root) → list form even though only one');
+    {
+      // Re-use the dirs above but remove all and keep only one
+      await rm(path.join(root, 'app-foo', 'AGENTS.md'));
+      await rm(path.join(root, 'lib-bar', 'sub', 'AGENTS.md'));
+      const { context, files } = await loadProjectContext(root);
+      eq(files.length, 1, '1 file found');
+      ok(/lib-bar\/AGENTS\.md/.test(context), `still shows the relative-path heading`);
+      ok(!/in the project root/.test(context), `does not claim the file is at root`);
+      // Cleanup
+      await rm(path.join(root, 'lib-bar', 'AGENTS.md'));
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[5] skips .git, node_modules, target, etc');
+    {
+      // Drop fake AGENTS.md files into excluded dirs
+      await mkdir(path.join(root, '.git'));
+      await mkdir(path.join(root, 'node_modules'));
+      await mkdir(path.join(root, 'target'));
+      await mkdir(path.join(root, '.code_boss'));
+      await writeFile(path.join(root, '.git', 'AGENTS.md'), 'should not be found');
+      await writeFile(path.join(root, 'node_modules', 'AGENTS.md'), 'should not be found');
+      await writeFile(path.join(root, 'target', 'AGENTS.md'), 'should not be found');
+      await writeFile(path.join(root, '.code_boss', 'AGENTS.md'), 'should not be found');
+      const { files } = await loadProjectContext(root);
+      ok(files.every((f) => !/(\.git|node_modules|target|\.code_boss)/.test(f.relDir)),
+         `no excluded-dir files leaked (got: ${files.map((f) => f.relDir).join(', ')})`);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[6] respects MAX_DEPTH cap');
+    {
+      // Build a deep chain — AGENTS.md at depth 8 (exceeds the cap of 6)
+      let cur = root;
+      for (let i = 0; i < 8; i++) {
+        cur = path.join(cur, `lvl${i}`);
+        await mkdir(cur);
+      }
+      await writeFile(path.join(cur, 'AGENTS.md'), 'too deep');
+      const all = await discoverCodeBossFiles(root);
+      ok(!all.some((f) => /lvl7/.test(f.relDir)), `files past MAX_DEPTH not included`);
+    }

+    {

+      ok(found && found.truncated, `truncated flag set`);
+      ok(found.content.length < big.length, `content shorter than original`);
+      ok(/truncated/.test(found.content), `marker present in content tail`);
+    }

+    console.log('\n[8] formatProjectContext on empty input → empty string');

+      eq(formatProjectContext(null), '', 'null');
+      eq(formatProjectContext(undefined), '', 'undefined');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[9] missing root dir → no-op');
+    {
+      const { context, files } = await loadProjectContext(path.join(root, 'does-not-exist'));
+      eq(context, '', 'empty context');
+      eq(files.length, 0, 'no files');
+    }

+    await rm(root, { recursive: true, force: true });
+  }

+      ok(/Legacy project rules/.test(context), `legacy body included`);
+    } finally { await rm(root, { recursive: true, force: true }); }

+      ok(/New canonical rules/.test(context), `canonical body used`);

+/**
+ * "Already open in another window" when it is not (the developer, 2026-10-12: "I've had it a couple of times where
+ * codeboss thinks a project already has an open window when it doesn't"), and the modal that replaces the stack trace.
+ *   [A] a lock whose pid now belongs to some other live process of ours (pids recycle) does NOT hold the project
+ *   [B] a Code Boss answering on the lock's port with THIS project open does — and the refusal carries the details
+ *   [C] one answering with a DIFFERENT project open does not
+ *   [D] the page: the refusal is a modal, not the error dialog; Force open removes the lock and opens the project
+ * Real server and page; a stand-in "other Code Boss" (a tiny /api/health) and a stand-in recycled pid (a child node).
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-project-lock-e2e.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync, readFileSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import { spawn } from 'node:child_process';
+import http from 'node:http';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+process.env.CB_HOME = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-plock-home-'));
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+const ROOT = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..');
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+const chrome = ['C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe', (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '') + '\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe'].find((p) => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+const PORT = 29571, OTHER = 29572, NOBODY = 29573;
+
+const proj = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-plock-proj-'));
+const lockFile = path.join(proj, '.code_boss', 'instance.lock');
+const plant = (pid, port) => { mkdirSync(path.dirname(lockFile), { recursive: true }); writeFileSync(lockFile, JSON.stringify({ pid, port, startedAt: '2026-10-12T09:00:00.000Z' })); };
+const lockPid = () => { try { return JSON.parse(readFileSync(lockFile, 'utf8')).pid; } catch { return null; } };
+
+// A live process that is not a Code Boss: what a recycled pid points at.
+const stray = spawn(process.execPath, ['-e', 'setTimeout(() => {}, 120000)'], { stdio: 'ignore' });
+// The "other Code Boss": answers /api/health the way a real one does, with whatever project we tell it to have.
+let otherProject = proj;
+const other = http.createServer((req, res) => { res.setHeader('content-type', 'application/json'); res.end(JSON.stringify({ ok: true, windows: 2, project: otherProject })); });
+await new Promise((r) => other.listen(OTHER, '127.0.0.1', r));
+
+async function main() {
+  const html = (await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8')).replace('</head>', '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'm', baseURL: '(mock)' }) + ';</script></head>');
+  const adapter = { async *completeStream() { yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: 'ok' } }] }; }, async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] }; } };
+  const { server } = await startServer({ html, baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter, modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }] });
+  const open = async () => { const r = await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/open-project`, { method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify({ path: proj }) }); return { status: r.status, body: await r.json().catch(() => ({})) }; };
+  const close = () => fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/close-project`, { method: 'POST' });
+  let browser = null;
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[A] the lock\'s pid is alive but is not a Code Boss (nothing on its port)');
+    plant(stray.pid, NOBODY);
+    let r = await open();
+    ok(r.status === 200, `the project opens (${r.status} ${r.body.error || ''})`);
+    ok(lockPid() === process.pid, 'and the lock is ours now');
+    await close();
+
+    console.log('\n[B] a Code Boss on the lock\'s port has THIS project open');
+    plant(stray.pid, OTHER); otherProject = proj;
+    r = await open();
+    ok(r.status === 400 && r.body.locked, `refused, with the details (${r.status})`);
+    ok(r.body.locked?.answered === true && r.body.locked?.windows === 2 && r.body.locked?.port === OTHER, `it answered, with 2 windows (${JSON.stringify(r.body.locked)})`);
+    ok(lockPid() === stray.pid, 'and its lock is left alone');
+
+    console.log('\n[C] the Code Boss on that port has a DIFFERENT project open');
+    otherProject = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-plock-elsewhere-'));
+    r = await open();
+    ok(r.status === 200, `the project opens (${r.status} ${r.body.error || ''})`);
+    await close();
+
+    console.log('\n[D] the page: a modal, and Force open');
+    if (!chrome) { console.log('  SKIP - Chrome not found'); }
+    else {
+      plant(stray.pid, OTHER); otherProject = proj;
+      browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+      const page = await browser.newPage();
+      const errs = []; page.on('pageerror', (e) => errs.push(e.message));
+      await page.setViewport({ width: 1200, height: 900 });
+      await page.goto(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/`);
+      await sleep(1200);
+      await page.evaluate((p) => openProject(p), proj);
+      await page.waitForSelector('#projectLocked', { timeout: 8000 }).catch(() => {});
+      const shown = await page.evaluate(() => ({
+        modal: !!document.getElementById('projectLocked'),
+        what: document.getElementById('projectLockedWhat')?.textContent || '',
+        errorDialog: [...document.querySelectorAll('.modal-bg.show')].filter((b) => b.id !== 'projectLocked' && /Could not open project/.test(b.textContent || '')).length,
+      }));
+      ok(shown.modal, 'the lock modal shows');
+      ok(/2 windows/.test(shown.what), `it says what holds it (${shown.what.slice(0, 90)})`);
+      ok(shown.errorDialog === 0, 'and not the error dialog');
+      await page.click('#projectLockedForce');
+      await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 15000 }).catch(() => {});
+      const opened = await page.evaluate(() => !document.getElementById('chat').classList.contains('hidden'));
+      ok(opened, 'Force open opens the project');
+      ok(lockPid() === process.pid, 'and the lock is ours');
+      ok(errs.length === 0, `no page errors (${errs.slice(0, 2).join(' | ')})`);
+    }
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.message || e); }
+  finally {
+    try { await browser?.close(); } catch {}
+    try { server.close(); } catch {}
+    try { other.close(); } catch {}
+    try { process.kill(stray.pid); } catch {}
+  }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main();

+/**
+ * THE FOLDER-PATH MENU at the bottom left.
+ *
+ * Clicking the project path used to go straight to "switch project" behind a confirm() dialog. It now opens a
+ * small menu: Change project (this window, the old behaviour, no second dialog — the menu IS the confirmation),
+ * Open a new window (a second Code Boss instance on the project screen, this window untouched), or Cancel.
+ *
+ * "Open a new window" is the launcher's `launch --new`, which only the packaged app has. The server takes an
+ * injectable launcher so this test can see the call without a window appearing, and a dev server with no
+ * launcher must say so rather than pretend.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-project-menu.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, readFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+
+const HOME = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-pmenu-home-'));
+process.env.CB_HOME = HOME;
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+const PORT = 29471, PORT2 = 29472;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+const SEP = String.fromCharCode(92);
+const chrome = [
+  'C:/Program Files/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',
+  (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '').split(SEP).join('/') + '/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',
+  'C:/Program Files (x86)/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',
+].find((p) => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+
+async function main() {
+  if (!chrome) { console.log('SKIP: Chrome not found'); process.exit(0); }
+  const projectDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-pmenu-'));
+  const html = (await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8'))
+    .replace('</head>', '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'm', baseURL: '(mock)' }) + ';</script></head>');
+  const adapter = { async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] }; } };
+  let launched = 0;
+  const { server } = await startServer({
+    html, baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' },
+    defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter, modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: projectDir,
+    newWindowLauncher: () => { launched++; },
+  });
+  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+  try {
+    const page = await browser.newPage();
+    let dialogs = 0;
+    page.on('dialog', async (d) => { dialogs++; await d.dismiss(); });
+    await page.goto(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/`);
+    await page.waitForFunction(() => { const p = document.getElementById('projectPath'); return p && p.textContent.trim() && p.textContent.trim() !== '(no project)'; }, { timeout: 10000 });
+    const menuOpen = () => page.evaluate(() => !!document.getElementById('projectMenu'));
+
+    console.log('\n[1] clicking the path opens the menu, with the three choices');
+    await page.click('#projectPath');
+    ok(await menuOpen(), 'the menu is open');
+    const labels = await page.evaluate(() => ['projectMenuChange', 'projectMenuNewWindow', 'projectMenuCancel'].map((id) => document.getElementById(id)?.textContent || null));
+    ok(labels[0] && /Change project/.test(labels[0]), `Change project (${labels[0]})`);
+    ok(labels[1] && /Open a new window/.test(labels[1]), `Open a new window (${labels[1]})`);
+    ok(labels[2] === 'Cancel', `Cancel (${labels[2]})`);
+    ok(await page.evaluate(() => document.getElementById('projectMenu').textContent.includes(document.getElementById('projectPath').textContent.trim())), 'and it shows which project this window has open');

+    ok(!(await menuOpen()), 'Cancel closes the menu');

+    ok(await page.evaluate(() => document.getElementById('landing').offsetParent === null), 'the chat is still showing, not the picker');
+    ok(launched === 0, 'and no window was opened');

+      const j = await r.json();
+      ok(r.status === 501, `501 (${r.status})`);
+      ok(/packaged app/.test(j.error || ''), `and says the launcher is what is missing (${(j.error || '').slice(0, 50)})`);
+    } finally { dev.close(); }
+  } finally {
+    await browser.close();
+    server.close();
+    await rm(projectDir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+    await rm(HOME, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+  }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e); process.exit(2); });

=== add test/test-prose-block-order.mjs
new d5659ae2ac6a664d60d49121
+/**
+ * A reply renders in the order it was written — and the TAIL after the last block does not render at all.
+ *
+ * Tool blocks execute after the stream ends, but every prose segment used to be emitted as it streamed, so the
+ * whole reply's prose landed above the tool rows: two messages, then the llm row, then the tool, which reads as
+ * the agent answering twice and then acting. Prose is now held and released at the block it leads.
+ *
+ * The prose after the LAST block is dropped instead — from the chat and from the transcript alike. Nothing
+ * useful can be written there (the results have not come back), and keeping it in ONE of the two places is what
+ * produced the repetition reported from production. Run:
+ *   node test/test-prose-block-order.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+async function run(turns, { checklist = true } = {}) {
+  process.env.CB_CHECKLIST = checklist ? '1' : '0';
+  const mod = await import('../src/agent/minimal-worker.mjs?v=' + Math.random());
+  const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-ord-'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'f.txt'), 'x');
+  let i = 0;
+  const adapter = {
+    async *completeStream() { const t = turns[Math.min(i++, turns.length - 1)]; for (let o = 0; o < t.length; o += 30) yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: t.slice(o, o + 30) } }] }; },
+    async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: turns[Math.min(i++, turns.length - 1)] } }] }; },
+  };
+  const order = [], prose = [], chunks = [];
+  await mod.runMinimalWorker({
+    adapter, model: 'm', signal: new AbortController().signal, messages: [{ content: 'go' }], projectDir: dir, maxTurns: 5,
+    onChatEvent: (e) => {
+      if (e.type === 'assistant-text-chunk') { chunks.push(String(e.content || '')); return; }
+      if (e.type === 'assistant-text') { order.push('PROSE'); prose.push(String(e.content || '')); }
+      else if (e.type === 'tool-call') order.push('TOOL:' + e.verb);
+      else if (e.type === 'tool-result') order.push('RESULT:' + e.verb);
+    },
+    onPhase: () => {}, onLog: () => {},
+  });
+  await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+  return { order, prose, chunks };
+}
+
+console.log('\n[1] prose · block · prose renders in that order');
+{
+  const { order, prose } = await run([
+    'Checking the first step off now.\n<remote-workspace><checklist>alpha\nbravo</checklist></remote-workspace>\nThat leaves bravo — your call on how to approach it.',
+    'done',
+  ]);
+  const i1 = order.indexOf('PROSE');
+  const t = order.indexOf('TOOL:checklist');
+  const i2 = order.indexOf('PROSE', t);
+  ok(i1 >= 0 && t > i1, `the lead-in comes BEFORE the tool row (${order.slice(0, 4).join(' → ')})`);
+  ok(!prose.some((p) => /That leaves bravo/.test(p)), 'and the tail after the last block is not shown at all — nothing useful can be written there');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[1b] prose · block · prose · block · prose interleaves, rather than all blocks then all prose');
+{
+  // Flushing held prose only at the END was not enough: with two blocks, both ran before the middle sentence.
+  const { order } = await run([
+    'First step.\n<remote-workspace><read path="f.txt"/></remote-workspace>\nMiddle.\n<remote-workspace><read path="f.txt"/></remote-workspace>\nLast word.',
+    'done',
+  ]);
+  // Results are dropped here: this checks the DOCUMENT shape (which prose sits against which call), and a result
+  // lands whenever its call happens to finish — see [1c] for where the result falls relative to the prose.
+  const shape = order.filter((s) => !s.startsWith('RESULT:')).join(' ');
+  ok(/^PROSE TOOL:read PROSE TOOL:read/.test(shape), 'the reply renders in the order it was written (' + shape + ')');
+  ok(!order.join(' ').includes('Last word'), 'and the tail after the final block is not among them');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[1c] the rest of the message lands while the tool RUNS, not after it finishes');
+{
+  // A developer should be able to read the whole reply while a build runs, rather than waiting on it to see the
+  // end of a sentence. It also puts that prose where it cannot be misread: it was written before any result
+  // existed, so printed BELOW the result it would read as a conclusion drawn from output the model never saw.
+  const { order } = await run([
+    'Kicking this off.\n<remote-workspace><read path="f.txt"/></remote-workspace>\nNow the second part.\n<remote-workspace><read path="f.txt"/></remote-workspace>',
+    'done',
+  ]);
+  const call = order.indexOf('TOOL:read');
+  const result = order.indexOf('RESULT:read');
+  const after = order.indexOf('PROSE', call);
+  ok(call >= 0 && result > call, `the call and its result are both there (${order.join(' ')})`);
+  ok(after > call && after < result, `the rest of the message shows between the tool starting and finishing (${after})`);
+}
+
+console.log('\n[2] a reply with NO block still streams its prose live');
+{
+  // The hold must not cost live streaming for the common case — a plain answer with no tools.
+  const { chunks, prose } = await run(['Here is the answer, in plain prose.']);
+  ok(chunks.length > 0, `chunks stream for a tool-free reply (${chunks.length})`);
+  ok(prose.some((p) => /plain prose/.test(p)), 'and the finalized text still arrives');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[3] prose after a block that parsed to ZERO calls is not lost');
+{
+  // An unclosed / fenced block is SEEN (so the prose after it is held) but runs nothing, and every branch on that
+  // path returns early — without a flush there, held prose would vanish from the chat while staying in the model
+  // transcript, which is the worst shape: the developer cannot see what the agent said.
+  const { prose } = await run([
+    'Trying something.\n```\n<remote-workspace><read path="f.txt"/></remote-workspace>\n```\nThat block was fenced, so nothing ran.',
+    'done',
+  ]);
+  ok(prose.some((p) => /fenced, so nothing ran/.test(p)), `the trailing prose still reaches the chat (${prose.map((p) => p.slice(0, 24)).join(' | ').slice(0, 90)})`);
+}
+
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);

+// Unit test for prose-sanitizer.mjs — clean assistant prose for display.
+import { createLiveProseFilter, sanitizeAssistantProse, truncateAtFirstBlock } from '../src/agent/prose-sanitizer.mjs';
+import { CORE_VERBS } from '../src/agent/tool-parser.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, label) => { if (c) { pass++; process.stdout.write(`  ok ${label}\n`); } else { fail++; process.stdout.write(`  FAIL ${label}\n`); } };
+const S = (t, o = {}) => sanitizeAssistantProse(t, { knownVerbs: CORE_VERBS, ...o });
+
+// ── code-span protection: a <js> inside backticks (an example) survives ──
+ok(S('Use the `<js>await read("x")</js>` tag to read a file.').includes('`<js>await read("x")</js>`'), 'a verb tag inside backticks is preserved (example, not a call)');
+
+// ── tool-verb preview strip: a real preview tag in prose is removed (the one verb is <js>) ──
+let r = S('Reading the build file. <js timeout_ms="1000"/>');
+ok(!r.includes('<js') && r.includes('Reading the build file'), 'a previewed <js/> tag is stripped, prose kept');
+r = S('<js>await write("a.txt", "hello")</js> Done writing.');
+ok(!r.includes('<js') && r.includes('Done writing'), 'a previewed <js>…</js> body form is stripped');
+
+// ── quote-aware: a `>` in a quoted attr doesn\'t leak a tag tail ──
+r = S('<js label="a>b.txt">x</js> ok');
+ok(!r.includes('a>b.txt') && !r.includes('</js>') && r.trim() === 'ok', 'quote-aware strip: no leaked tag tail from a `>` in attr');
+
+// ── unknown/stripped tags are NOT verbs → left alone (not our concern) ──
+ok(S('The pom has a <library-ref name="x"/> element.').includes('<library-ref'), 'unknown <library-ref> passes through');
+
+// ── antml + remote-workspace orphan strips ──
+ok(!S('Done. </invoke>').includes('antml'), 'leaked antml artifact stripped');
+ok(!S('Result below.\n</remote-workspace>').includes('remote-workspace'), 'orphan </remote-workspace> stripped');
+
+// ── meta-prose line strips ──
+ok(S('for context: [planner_agent] said:\nThe real answer.').trim() === 'The real answer.', 'gemini "for context:[agent] said:" line stripped');
+ok(!S('Let me rewrite this.\nHere is the fix.').includes('Let me rewrite'), '"Let me rewrite this." self-narration stripped');
+ok(S('I will now reply in prose without using tools.\nHi there.').trim() === 'Hi there.', '"reply in prose" narration stripped');
+
+// ── a question at the end is never stripped (the solicitation strip took real ones; gone 2026-10-26) ──
+ok(S('I fixed the endpoint. What would you like to do next?').trim() === 'I fixed the endpoint. What would you like to do next?', 'a trailing "what would you like next?" is kept');
+ok(S('I added the module.\nWhat would you like to name the new module?').includes('What would you like to name the new module?'), 'a real question that starts the same way is kept');
+ok(S('Should I use Postgres or MySQL?').includes('Postgres or MySQL'), 'a GENUINE question survives (not a solicitation)');
+
+// ── content-free closer strip (but a substantive sign-off survives) ──
+ok(S('Tests pass 12/12. Standing by.').trim() === 'Tests pass 12/12.', 'reflex "Standing by." closer stripped');
+ok(S("The endpoint validates now. I'm ready for your next task.").trim() === 'The endpoint validates now.', 'reflex "ready for your next task" stripped');
+ok(S('Standing by for your approval before I deploy.').includes('approval before I deploy'), 'a SUBSTANTIVE sign-off survives');
+
+// ── dangling trailing-backtick strip: a model artifact (unterminated fence with no content) ──
+ok(S('...running scripts.\n\n`````````').trim() === '...running scripts.', 'a trailing run of stray backticks is stripped');
+ok(S('Done. `').trim() === 'Done.', 'a single dangling trailing backtick is stripped');
+ok(S('Run `npm test` to check.').includes('`npm test`'), 'a real closing inline code span at the end survives');
+ok(S('Here:\n```js\nfoo()\n```').includes('```js'), 'a real closed fenced block at the end survives');
+ok(S('Use `foo`').trim() === 'Use `foo`', 'a message ending in a real inline span survives (no over-strip)');
+
+// ── boundary horizontal-rule strip (the model divides tool uses from prose with "---", but the UI already delimits) ──
+ok(S('---\nHere is the summary of what I did.').trim() === 'Here is the summary of what I did.', 'a leading "---" (after a tool block) is stripped');
+ok(S('I finished the edits.\n\n---').trim() === 'I finished the edits.', 'a trailing "---" (before a tool block) is stripped');
+ok(S('---\n\nDone with the change.\n\n---').trim() === 'Done with the change.', 'both leading and trailing rules are stripped at once');
+ok(S('***\nUsing asterisks now.').trim() === 'Using asterisks now.', 'an asterisk rule "***" is stripped too');
+ok(S('- - -\nSpaced rule.').trim() === 'Spaced rule.', 'a spaced rule "- - -" is stripped');
+ok(S('First part.\n\n---\n\nSecond part.').includes('---'), 'a rule in the MIDDLE of prose (a real divider) is KEPT');
+ok(S('Use `x --- y` inline.').includes('`x --- y`'), 'dashes inside inline code are untouched');
+ok(S('Here:\n```\na\n---\nb\n```').includes('---'), 'a "---" inside a fenced code block survives');
+ok(S('Just a normal sentence with -- two dashes.').includes('-- two dashes'), 'two dashes (not a rule) are not touched');
+ok(S('---\r\nHello world').trim() === 'Hello world', 'a CRLF-terminated leading rule is stripped (matches the live guard — no reappearing rule at finalize)');
+ok(S('Done.\r\n\r\n---').trim() === 'Done.', 'a CRLF trailing rule is stripped');
+ok(S('First part.\r\n\r\n---\r\n\r\nSecond part.').includes('---'), 'a CRLF rule in the MIDDLE of prose still survives');
+
+// ── holdLeadingRule: live streaming guard so a boundary "---" never flashes before finalize ──
+import { holdLeadingRule } from '../src/agent/prose-sanitizer.mjs';
+const H = (b) => holdLeadingRule(b);
+ok(H('-').hold === true, 'a lone "-" is HELD (might become a rule)');
+ok(H('--').hold === true, 'two dashes "--" are HELD (might become "---")');
+ok(H('---').hold === true, 'three dashes with no newline yet are still HELD (rule not terminated)');
+ok(H('---\n').hold === true, 'a completed "---\\n" with nothing after is HELD (more rule may follow)');
+ok(H('---\nHello').hold === false && H('---\nHello').emit === 'Hello', 'once real content follows the rule, the rule is dropped and the content released');
+ok(H('-- two dashes in prose').hold === false && H('-- two dashes in prose').emit === '-- two dashes in prose', '"-- text" resolves to content (not a rule) and is released whole');
+ok(H('- list item').hold === false && H('- list item').emit === '- list item', 'a "- list item" is released (single dash + space is not a rule)');
+ok(H('Hello world').hold === false && H('Hello world').emit === 'Hello world', 'plain text is released immediately (no hold)');
+ok(H('---\n---\nText').hold === false && H('---\n---\nText').emit === 'Text', 'multiple stacked leading rules are all dropped');
+ok(H('*bold*').hold === false && H('*bold*').emit === '*bold*', 'emphasis "*bold*" is released (not a rule line)');

+ok(truncateAtFirstBlock('pure prose answer') === 'pure prose answer', 'no markers → unchanged');
